# Current Architecture

This describes **implemented source**, not completion of the [accepted development design](designs/2026-09-24-omp-vscode-development.md). Chat runs over `omp --mode rpc-ui` ([ADR-0038](decisions/0038-host-chat-over-rpc-ui-on-a-broker-pipe-child.md)); [ADR-0040](decisions/0040-switch-one-editor-between-rpc-chat-and-native-pty.md) restores the unmodified native TUI as another mode of the same editor, with exact old-root exit before transport replacement. Collab, relay, room/link, raw recorder and the Collab-era Agents Hub remain deleted. Separate native Models/Agents settings editors implement the user-requested boundary change of 2026-10-08 without restoring that roster. Source/headless/native-command evidence is distinct from installed-window acceptance.

The implementation has five runtime boundaries:

- **VS Code extension host** (`src/extension.ts`, `src/wiring/`, `src/host/chat-runtime.ts`, `src/host/native-session.ts`, `src/host/rpc-launch.ts`, `src/host/rpc-reconcile.ts`, `src/host/rpc/`, `src/host/pty-client.ts`, `src/views/`): owns the session index/claims, actual Chat or Terminal runtime, editor bindings, launcher, notifications and diagnostics. A Chat runtime holds an `RpcSession` whether or not an editor is open; Terminal holds the managed PTY and authenticated native state observer. It resolves installed OMP per launch without a version gate and starts neither transport directly from the extension host. The window's mutable state is one `WindowState` object that `activate` creates (`createWindowState`) and installs in the module's one `let` binding; functions reach it through `windowState()`, and `deactivate` disposes through it and leaves it installed. Six wiring modules hold the window's regions behind a `register…(deps)` handle stored on that object: `src/wiring/window-routing.ts` (the window registry, cross-window hand-over of sessions and launches, other windows' requests), `src/wiring/session-actions.ts` (the Sessions view's click, open, forget, stop, kill, delete and refresh), `src/wiring/guest-dispatcher.ts` (the `omp:*` page messages, chat commands, draft and selection handoffs, detail tabs), `src/wiring/editor-panels.ts` (creating, binding, rendering and placing the session editors), `src/wiring/bridge-host.ts` (the bridge endpoint, document provisioning, live-editor adoption and binding delivery) and `src/wiring/recent-sessions.ts` (the empty chat's recent sessions and the open of one of them, over the Sessions view's own rows and click). Each takes its dependencies as getters and callbacks, never `extension.ts`, and has its own test with fake dependencies. The Ctrl+Shift+M file window is `createFileWindow` (`src/wiring/file-window.ts`), held on the window state beside the routing and built the same way.
- **Detached broker** (`src/broker/pty-broker.ts`, `src/broker/rpc-child.ts`, `src/broker/rpc-ring.ts`): independently surviving authenticated loopback IPC per slot. Kinds are `managed-rpc` (Chat pipe child), `managed-omp` (native TUI PTY), `folder-shell` (separate shell) and `stats-dashboard` (standalone Stats PTY). The runtime/native addon are content-staged outside the installed extension ([ADR-0012](decisions/0012-run-child-process-entries-from-staged-copies.md), [ADR-0032](decisions/0032-prove-the-pty-runtime-with-a-console-child.md)).
- **Managed OMP process**: installed `omp --mode rpc-ui` for Chat or default interactive OMP for Terminal, one extension-owned writer per claimed conversation. `-e` plus `--preload` (or, for OMP's own compiled program, `BUN_OPTIONS`; see Launch) loads process-bound authenticated host control.
- **Session Webview** (`src/webview/`, `src/chat/model.ts`): the same WebviewPanel and immutable editor slot E show the host-owned Chat projection or xterm TerminalPane. Mode navigation belongs to the active session editor title; Copy Screen is native Terminal's title overflow action. Neither managed view duplicates these controls or normal live/grid status inside the page ([ADR-0043](decisions/0043-place-session-mode-actions-in-editor-title.md)). Replacing the child renews the document credential, not the editor.
- **Folder-shell Webview** (`src/webview/shell/`): an xterm renderer over a folder shell PTY; a folder's Open Terminal creates a separate shell and recoverable slot, never a second managed OMP writer.

**What ends a broker, and which runtime it runs on** ([ADR-0074](decisions/0074-run-chat-brokers-on-the-sessions-own-bun-runtime-with-code-exe-as-fallback.md), [design](designs/2026-10-14-chat-brokers-on-the-session-bun-runtime.md)). A broker is one of two things. Terminal, folder-shell and Stats brokers, and every Chat broker that could not use Bun, are `process.execPath` of the extension host run with `ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE=1` (`src/host/pty-runtime.ts`), so their image is VS Code's own `Code.exe`. A **Chat (`managed-rpc`) broker starts on the `bun.exe` that runs the session's own OMP** when the plan is eligible (`src/host/chat-broker-runtime.ts`, `PtyBrokerClient.chooseChatRuntime`): the setting `ompDesk.chatBrokerRuntime` is `auto` (`vscode` forces `Code.exe`), Windows x64, the OMP launch is a bun shim, `bun.exe` passes the executable trust rule (`src/host/executable-trust.ts`: ADR-0054's owner and access rules on the file and every directory above it, a file another account may only read and execute), and the pipe-only self-check (`pty-broker.js --self-check --pipe-only`, a proof of its own that never satisfies the full one) reports Bun 1.4.0 or newer. Any refusal, and a Bun broker that cannot be spawned or ends before it publishes a record, starts the broker on `Code.exe` instead and logs the reason (`Chat broker runtime: …`); a broker that published a record owns its slot and is never replaced. The broker names its own runtime in its record (`runtime: { engine, version }`; absent in an earlier build's record, which was always `Code.exe`); an attach nowhere depends on it, so a broker of either runtime, and one from an earlier or later build, is re-adopted the same way. Every broker survives window close, reload and a full quit of VS Code. The one outside force known to end a `Code.exe` broker is VS Code's updater: "Restart to Update" runs Inno Updater, which waits 30 s for every `Code.exe` of the install to exit and then terminates the rest (observed 2026-10-13, VS Code 1.141.0 to 1.141.1: five session brokers and the stats broker among seven processes terminated; the OMP child of each ended with its broker, because an `rpc` child lives on the broker's pipe). It captures by the install's `Code.exe` path, so a Chat on a Bun broker (`bun.exe` broker, `bun.exe` OMP) is not captured and a fresh window re-adopts it with its transcript; the other sessions are relaunched from their files. `src/wiring/updater-notice.ts` recognises a termination afterwards (the updater's temp log names the terminated pids; a broker descriptor with one of them) and tells the user once. A running Bun broker keeps the `bun.exe` image it started from: `bun upgrade` renames the running file aside, and a second upgrade fails until the old processes (the broker and the OMP child) end; a broker can outlive its session, and Stop or Kill All ends it. A Bun broker that published a record but could not be attached leaves that Chat `unconfirmed` and keeps the file off the Bun path for the next Chat. The cost is about 110 MB more commit per Bun broker than a `Code.exe` one, with the working set unchanged.

**Chat broker runtime and the launcher kind ([ADR-0074](decisions/0074-run-chat-brokers-on-the-sessions-own-bun-runtime-with-code-exe-as-fallback.md), [ADR-0075](decisions/0075-carry-bun-runtime-flags-by-launcher-kind-and-probe-before-using-an-environment-carrier.md)).** Both decisions read the same fact, `OmpBinary.launcher` (`bun-shim` or `program`; absent on a hand-built value), and agree on what it means: only a `bun-shim` launch has a `bun.exe` that is Bun's own CLI and therefore a runtime a broker can share. The Chat broker's runtime is chosen from the launcher kind first: `bun-shim` goes on to the trust read and the pipe-only proof and, when both pass, the broker starts on that `bun.exe`; `program` (OMP's own compiled `omp.exe` from the winget package, `omp.sh` or mise, which embeds a private Bun that is not a broker runtime: the mode that would turn it into one, `BUN_BE_BUN=1`, is not an OMP contract and is inherited by every `omp` it starts) and a launch with no kind never get a Bun broker: the broker starts on `Code.exe` with the reason `launcher` logged, and such a Chat still ends in a VS Code update. The two cuts do not overlap: ADR-0075's `BUN_OPTIONS` carrier (and its per-executable probe in `src/host/launch-capability.ts`) is used only for `program`/unknown launches, whose broker is always `Code.exe`, so Desk's `BUN_OPTIONS` never reaches a broker that runs on Bun; a `bun-shim` launch carries its flags on the command line, which the Bun broker passes to the OMP child unchanged (`pty-client-bun.test.ts` runs it through a real Bun broker). The child inherits the broker's own environment (the extension host's at broker start, scrubbed of `SCRUBBED_INHERITED_ENV`) plus the launch spec's changes; the broker never applies those changes to itself, so anything added to a Bun broker's own environment (a memory flag, say) would reach every OMP child. The caches are keyed independently and neither reads the other's verdict: ADR-0075's probe answer is per OMP executable (path, size, modification time) and means "this OMP honours `BUN_OPTIONS`"; ADR-0074's stored pipe-only proof is per `bun.exe` path, size and modification time (no file id), its in-process proof and its failure hold also include the file id, and the trust read is not kept: it runs again on every Bun-eligible launch. A user's own `BUN_OPTIONS` or global `bunfig.toml` applies to a Bun broker as it applies to the OMP child that runs the same file. Terminal, folder-shell and Stats launches never take a plan, whatever the launcher kind. The log level of the runtime line follows the reason: see "Levels of the Chat broker runtime line" below.

**Source layers** ([ADR-0065](decisions/0065-enforce-one-import-direction-between-source-layers.md), [design](designs/2026-10-12-extension-host-module-structure.md)). `src/` is divided into layers by directory, with one import direction: `src/shared/` (pure helpers, no `src/` imports), `src/chat/` (the reducer both sides run; may import `shared`), `src/protocol/` (message contracts and their parsers; `chat`, `shared`), `src/wire/` (process-boundary contracts and the `vscode`-free primitives they share: the PTY and control wire protocols, the broker IPC dial/handshake, identity proofs, the Win32 native binding, private storage and atomic files, the native file-observation and inventory models, the OMP settings core; `protocol`, `chat`, `shared` only, 2026-10-30, ADR-0065 amendment), `src/host/`, `src/broker/` and `src/omp/` (process-side code; `wire`, `protocol`, `chat`, `shared`; `host` also imports `broker` and `omp`; `broker` and `omp` import each other but never `host`), `src/views/` (tree providers; also `wire` and `host`), `src/webview/` (the pages; `protocol`, `chat`, `shared` only, never `host`, `views`, `broker` or `omp`) `src/runtime/` (the `SessionRuntime` port and its in-process implementation, [ADR-0066](decisions/0066-extract-a-session-runtime-port-and-enforce-it-with-a-vscode-free-layer.md); `wire`, `host`, `broker`, `omp` and below; no file under it may refer to `vscode`, directly or through any chain of non-test imports, and its port module `session-runtime.ts` imports only data-shape types from an allow-list) and `src/wiring/` (modules that connect host services to VS Code surfaces; `wire`, `host`, `views`, `runtime` and below). Only `wiring` and `src/extension.ts` may import `runtime`. **Session runtime port.** A window opens, restores and re-attaches a conversation through `LocalSessionRuntime` (`open`, `restore`, `reattach`; and `replaceWriterHeld` inside a lease it already holds), not through the session index; launching and re-adopting a host is `LocalLauncher`, which reaches the window only through the `LauncherWindow` hooks in the order a launch runs them (retire the document, reserve the broker slot, launch, adopt, watch the broker, prepare the view, attach, record the run intent). What still bypasses the port, by name, is listed in the [design](designs/2026-10-13-session-runtime-port.md) §6. `src/extension.ts` may import every layer except `src/webview/` (it is the host bundle's entry, so page code may not enter through it either) and is the only file directly under `src/`. `import type` counts as an import, and any import cycle fails the check. `node scripts/check-imports.mjs` (run by `npm run typecheck`, tested by `scripts/check-imports.test.mjs`) reports one line per violation; test files (`*.test.ts`, `*.test.tsx`), test stubs and `test/**` are exempt from the layer rule but not from the cycle rule. The remedy for a violation is to move the imported module to the lowest layer both importers may see, never an exception.

**Contributed ids and the `ompDesk.` namespace** ([ADR-0068](decisions/0068-rename-contributed-ids-to-ompdesk-and-migrate-settings-at-activation.md), [design](designs/2026-10-13-extension-namespace.md)). Every id the manifest contributes — commands (category `OMP Desk`), settings, the container and views, context keys, keybindings and the `when` clauses that name them — is `ompDesk.<name>`; the bare `omp.` namespace is Oh My Pi's own. `omp.detail` and `omp.stats` have no serializer and became `ompDesk.detail` and `ompDesk.stats`. Not renamed, because VS Code persists or scopes them and no user or other extension sees them: the webview view types (`omp.session`, `omp.session.b2.<tab>.<editor>`, `omp.shellTerminal`, `omp.settings`, which also keep the `^omp[.]session` keybinding regex and the `onWebviewPanel:` events), every Memento, catalog record and SecretStorage key (`omp.sessionIndex.v1`, `omp.bridge.v1/…`, `omp.hostControl.*`, …), tree element ids, the status bar item id, the `omp:*` page messages and every name OMP itself reads. For the releases in which users still have old names in `settings.json` and `keybindings.json`: `src/wiring/legacy-settings.ts` moves the 13 old settings of the User and Workspace layers at the start of every activation (awaited at most 10 seconds before any setting is read; the old keys stay contributed, hidden and deprecated; no marker, so a value that reappears is moved too; read-only workspace files are not written; layers the new key cannot hold are left alone; one log line per key and layer), and `src/wiring/legacy-aliases.ts` keeps the 92 command ids that existed before the rename (the 87 of 0.3.1, the last release, plus five added on the way to 0.4.0) (and the generated view commands) registered, first in `activate`, uncontributed, as aliases that forward their arguments to the new commands and warn once per id, and publishes `sessionMode`, `streaming` and `composerPopupOpen` under their old context-key names too. All of it (aliases, mirror, old skip-shell ids, the migration and the hidden deprecated keys) is kept until 1.0.0 and removed in that release. A one-time notice (`ompDesk.renameNotice.v1`) tells a profile that ran an earlier release what moved; another extension's `omp.` names are never touched.

**Session runtime port, cuts 4 to 6.** `LocalSessionRuntime` also holds the stop verdict operations (ungated, the caller's lease and the recording stay in the window until cut 7 below), the services of the end of a process (`exit`: `rpcExited`, `nativeExited`, `endEnded`, `brokerLost`; `control`: host control; `identity`: conversation identity binding; `nativeWatch`: the native Terminal's watch and observed handover) and the conversation surface `conversations` (`ConversationPort`, over the `ChatRuntime` the runtime owns): page attach, `command`, `navigate`, `notify`, the view-only and legacy views and the value reads `stateOf`, `activityOf`, `promptsOf` and `rewindOf`, so the chat model never leaves the runtime. The window reacts through hooks run after the state they describe is committed.

**Session runtime port, cut 7.** Kill is `SessionRuntime.kill` (`LocalKill` in `src/runtime/local-kill.ts`: the identity probe, `taskkill`, and the recording that follows a proven kill); the window only builds the selection and shows the notices and refreshes (`KillWindow`). The gated `stop`, `stopRecorded`, `replaceWriter`, `beginTransition`/`endTransition` and `readTransitionFacts` are runtime operations that the compound flows (stop, delete, new session in place, view switch, Restart) call, and the runtime owns the per-conversation record (`RuntimeRecords`: process, control channel, fence, failure, native watch). The live Chat session never leaves the runtime: `conversations.incarnationOf` names it by a number that is never reused, `liveOf` reads its facts as a `LiveConversation` value, and the footer reads, model and thinking choices, Compact, Shake, export, Share, rename, the Context view's reads and subscription, the settings editor's apply and the view switch's pending join are operations keyed by `(conversation, incarnation)` that answer `stale` when the conversation holds another session. `LocalSessionRuntime` has no `sessionOf` and no `chat` member.

Visible terminal panes check a quiet host every ten seconds with a non-resetting status probe and a four-second response bound. Initial attach and actual stream discontinuities recover through snapshots, never ordinary focus. Silence disables input but preserves the copyable screen; folder shells name the folder's Reconnect Terminal action. This is liveness detection, not a shell bridge or editor-membership recovery protocol: a host-only restart cannot address the old shell page unless it still holds that panel. Reconnect uses attach-only runtime staging and retains the exact broker/slot. Open Terminal deliberately creates another shell; a host-less page close cannot be intercepted.

**Terminal presentation.** Native and folder-shell panes share resource-resolved VS Code terminal settings. The configured terminal family (editor fallback) stays first, followed by common and installed Nerd/symbol families, existing bundled Codicon and monospace. Bounded installed-family discovery does not modify user settings or add a font dependency. Configuration events update/refit existing instances without replacing screens; only the broker-authorized owner resizes. The fitted-grid remainder uses terminal background and native scrollbar chrome is hidden beneath xterm's auto-hiding scrollbar. Both terminal surfaces are headerless and use terminal editor icons, with a Starting status before the first ready state. Grid, input ownership and live shell-cleanup verdicts are display-only tooltip metadata; shell titles remain compact.

**Native start admission.** The host projects an explicit starting flag while a native editor's open or mode request admits a stopped session. Before a broker pipeline exists, attach/probe requests receive that lifecycle projection rather than an unavailable terminal frame. The page keeps Starting visible and hides stopped controls and stale warnings until the attempt completes. Explicit same-mode Terminal opens refresh the authenticated handle and frontend; an exited writer is reconciled before resuming, and an unreachable reopen is reported visibly without launching another writer.
The terminal pipeline belongs to one exact writer. A proved Close or natural process exit disposes it, so a retained editor's next start binds a fresh frontend instead of asking its stopped broker for a screen.

**Shell sizing and retirement.** The app wrapper's full-height flex rules live in the shared terminal stylesheet, so standalone shell documents use the same sizing chain and ResizeObserver refit as session documents. On an authenticated broker's confirmed child exit, the host removes the shell recovery record and broker-slot mapping, then requests authenticated shutdown with `requireStopped: true`, without sending a child stop or signalling a PID. The broker can retire even while its frontend remains attached, so the Processes row disappears on the next visible refresh. The editor keeps its final screen and exited state after broker shutdown; closing it stays silent. Successful Processes stops also retire the recovery record after confirmed child exit, even while the stopped broker is closing. None of this proves escaped descendant commands ended; live or unreachable shells retain their explicit Keep Running / Terminate decision.
An installed isolated-window check (2026-10-08) typed `exit` into a real folder shell and observed its visible Processes row disappear after 2.274 seconds, without Stop or Refresh. The retained editor showed exit code 0 with a copyable screen, and closing it produced no editor or native confirmation. The shell recovery catalog was empty, the exact broker had exited, and the owned test window left no running process. Typecheck and 102 targeted tests passed on that packaged baseline; this check does not prove descendant-tree emptiness or later unrelated changes.
Closing a live folder shell still asks **Keep Running / Terminate**; cancellation never authorizes a stop. Confirmed Terminate uses the authenticated broker to stop its child, requires child absence, then requests `shutdown(requireStopped: true)` before retiring the shell slot and mapping. A Windows `tree: unknown` / `verified: false` verdict describes descendant completeness, not a live shell or failed stop, and remains in Output without a warning after successful child exit and broker shutdown. Genuine stop/shutdown failures retain recovery metadata, warn with the Processes Stop action, and refresh the child facts without pretending the closed editor is still open.
Broker shutdown first waits until replies for previously accepted stop requests have been queued, including a stop refusal. A child-exit notification can start natural retirement while the explicit stop is still collecting its kernel verdict; that concurrent shutdown must not close the endpoint before the stop caller receives its correlated result. Read-only requests remain independent, and no tree or child-absence proof is promoted by this ordering.
The same window also serializes natural folder-shell metadata retirement and shutdown with the entire Processes Stop → shutdown sequence on the index's existing per-slot lifecycle gate. A confirmed explicit stop cancels its captured queued natural watcher before releasing that gate; its authenticated shutdown remains the authority, not a closed socket or broker absence. Ordinary editor teardown only unsubscribes future events and preserves retirement already queued for a confirmed natural exit. Session stop routes and read-only Processes observations keep their existing behavior.
Installed isolated acceptance also exercised editor-close Terminate on an idle shell and on a shell running `ping -t localhost`. Both produced truthful child-absent / tree-unknown diagnostics followed by authenticated broker shutdown, empty recovery mappings and an automatically disappearing Processes row without a warning or manual Stop/Refresh. The observed original shell and broker generations were absent; the ping process was absent in that case, not a general descendant-tree guarantee. Natural-exit screen retention and silent close still passed, with 110 targeted tests and typecheck on the same packaged snapshot. Cleanup left no owned test processes running.
A later frozen-snapshot isolated acceptance passed typecheck, build and 164 targeted tests, with 56 installed runtime/helper payload files matching the packaged build. Its real modern NodeService extension host armed the staged owner monitor against the independently read owning-main kernel generation. One actual Processes Stop with the shell editor open received the validated child-absent verdict and authenticated `requireStopped` shutdown acknowledgement, removed the row and recovery mappings, and retained the exited screen without a false warning. The acknowledgement was established by the guarded host success output and its authenticated helper response checks, not inferred from socket closure. A fresh shell from the same artifact then exited with code 0, retired automatically without Stop/Refresh, kept its copyable screen and closed silently. Cleanup reconciled all 20 observed owned process generations, the isolated window and its listener; no main-profile operation or PID signal was used. This establishes installed arming and these lifecycle paths, not real last-window grace expiry, cross-window concurrency or an empty descendant tree.

Live VS Code theme changes are observed on the renderer's ancestors. The shared renderer re-reads the palette and assigns `terminal.options.theme`, updating the host remainder background in the same callback without recreating xterm or changing fonts, grid, input ownership or screen contents. Its observer disconnects on disposal.

**Terminal cell widths.** The OMP TUI counts Unicode 11+ widths (`Bun.stringWidth`), so the Webview renderer and the broker's headless screen mirror both run xterm.js's Unicode 11 table (`@xterm/addon-unicode11`, `src/shared/terminal-unicode.ts`); wide emoji and CJK therefore advance the cursor by the two cells the TUI addressed, in the live stream and in reattach snapshots. Grapheme-cluster sequences (VS16 symbols, ZWJ families, skin-tone modifiers) still differ; see the [native terminal design](designs/2026-10-03-native-terminal-ux.md).

**Terminal file links.** Native and folder-shell panes activate OSC 8 through the shared xterm handler and detect plain relative/absolute paths with a host-validated link provider (wrapped and wide cells preserved). Hover underlines with a pointer and a plain-click hint. The host resolves against the current session/folder cwd, rechecks existing regular local files at activation, and opens non-preview editor tabs at one-based line/column positions. Only `file:` and the narrowly decoded OMP `vscode://file` form map to local files; other custom schemes/network files are refused. HTTP(S) retains the extension-owned browser confirmation. Cwd, route and document authority remain host-owned; PTY environment is unchanged. Existing broker serialization omits OSC 8 targets on attach/reopen snapshots: visible resolvable OMP path labels (compact Read and path/position text) remain clickable via the plain provider, but arbitrary/abbreviated labels without a resolvable path cannot recover their snapshot-only target. New live output retains OSC 8 normally.

Terminal link opening goes through `vscode.open` (new non-preview tab; text at its line/column, images/PDFs/binaries in their viewer) and failed activations show a short warning. The link provider caches host validations per text (30 s hits, 5 s misses), answers cached rows synchronously and shares in-flight requests, so a repainting TUI cannot flicker or drop the hovered link; `~/` paths resolve against the user's home. See the [native terminal design](designs/2026-10-03-native-terminal-ux.md).

**Chat file links.** The Chat reuses the Terminal mode's detector, validation cache and host open path (one convention). `src/webview/lib/chat-file-links.ts` finds candidate references (`detectChatFileLinks`: the Terminal detector plus `@path [lines a-b]` mentions, a path-shaped filter, and Markdown destinations that are a path or `file:` URL); `ChatFileLinks` asks the host through the same `omp:terminal-link-validate` / `omp:terminal-link-open` pair, with `folders: true` so a folder is a valid target too, through the shared `ValidationCache` (30 s hits, 5 s misses, one request per target, at most eight in flight). Terminal links send no `folders` and stay files only. `FileLinks.tsx` wraps a reference in a focusable `role="link"` span only after the host proves an existing regular file or folder; text, selection and copy are never altered and nothing is linked without the page's provider (detail tabs have none). Linked: assistant and user Markdown prose, bold/italic/strike text, inline code spans, Markdown links, tool-row read/edit targets (a read's selector line, an edit's first changed line), section titles, grep/find line numbers and glob entries. Fenced code blocks stay text. The host accepts the pair in Chat mode too (panel and bridge routes), resolves against the session's cwd, and still refuses everything but an existing local file or folder; the position grammar is `:12`, `:12:3`, `:12-30`, `:L12`, `#L12`, `#L12-34`, `#L12C3` and `file:` fragments (the start of a range is revealed). Activation (`handleTerminalLink`, `src/host/terminal-links.ts`): a click or Enter opens a file in a non-preview tab; Ctrl+Shift+Click or Ctrl+Shift+Enter (`action: "reveal"`) only selects it in VS Code's Explorer, without opening it, through the workbench's `revealInExplorer` command, which shows the Explorer; Ctrl+Click or Ctrl+Enter (`action: "os"`) starts `explorer.exe /select,"<path>"` (`revealPathInOs`), opening the containing folder in File Explorer with the item selected; VS Code's `revealFileInOS` is not used because, while its Explorer list has focus, it reveals the Explorer's selection instead of its argument. A folder has no tab, so a plain or Ctrl+Shift click reveals it in the Explorer. The Explorer shows only paths inside an open workspace folder (`workspace.getWorkspaceFolder`); for anything else a warning points to Ctrl+Shift+Click. Extensions have no API to select an Explorer row without showing the Explorer, so revealing switches the side bar. The link's right-click menu offers the same three actions through VS Code's `webview/context` menu: the span carries `data-vscode-context` (`fileLinkMenuContext`: `webviewSection: "ompFileLink"` and the target), VS Code turns its keys into context keys for the menu's `when` clauses and passes the object with the panel's viewType as `webview` to `ompDesk.fileLink.open` / `reveal` / `revealInOs`; `openFileLinkFromMenu` checks the object (`fileLinkMenuTarget`), finds the editor with that viewType and sends the same `omp:terminal-link-open` request a click sends. VS Code's own Cut/Copy/Paste stay below them. A web link's right-click menu (`webLinkMenuContext`, `webviewSection: "ompWebLink"`, in a Chat and in the TODO/Agents detail tabs) offers **Open in Editor** and **Open in Browser** (`ompDesk.webLink.openInEditor` / `openInBrowser`); the command re-validates the URL with `webLinkUrl` (`webLinkMenuUrl`) and opens it through the same `openPageWebLink` a click uses, needing no editor lookup because a web link does not depend on the session's cwd. The Chat prompt's `file-links` rule (below) asks the model to write paths in the form this detector links. Verified against the installed OMP 18.6.3: its TUI links only through OSC 8 — `fileHyperlink` (`pi-tui/src/render/hyperlink.ts`: `vscode://file/<path>:<line>:<col>` in VS Code terminals, else a plain `file:` URI) on tool paths (read, edit, write, grep, find, ast-grep/ast-edit), skill and image chips and the status-line folder, and `resolveMarkdownLinkTargets` (`pi-coding-agent/src/internal-urls/hyperlink-targets.ts`) for model-authored Markdown links (relative to the session cwd, existing files or directories, `file:` URLs, `#`/`?` suffix kept). It does not link bare paths, inline code, fenced code or `@` mentions itself; those are linked here as the VS Code terminal's text detection links them in Terminal mode. Not supported: internal-URL schemes (`local://`, `agent://`, …), which the TUI resolves but the host open path refuses, and bare `file://` URLs in prose (the TUI does not link those either).

**Chat keys and the empty session** (`src/shared/key-hints.ts`, `src/shared/jsonc.ts`, `src/shared/code-span.ts`, `src/host/key-hints.ts`, `src/host/key-hints-source.ts`, `src/wiring/key-hints-wiring.ts`, `src/webview/lib/key-hints-store.ts`, `EmptyState.tsx`, `KeyboardShortcutsButton.tsx`; [design](designs/2026-10-18-chat-keys-and-empty-state.md), [ADR-0068](decisions/0068-rename-contributed-ids-to-ompdesk-and-migrate-settings-at-activation.md) amendment). The host sends the page `omp:chat-keys`: for each OMP Desk command of the empty state, the key as the user has it (`null` when a removal took the key away) and, when another command is bound to the same key, a conflict (`{ by, command }`). `computeKeyHints` merges three sources: the manifest defaults, the user's own `keybindings.json` of the active profile (read as JSONC by `key-hints-source.ts`; a rebinding replaces the default, a `-command` entry removes it, and the last applicable user entry wins) and the other installed extensions' `contributes.keybindings` (their removals are honoured, a removal that names no key included). A binding is a conflict only when its `when` is not obviously false where the key is pressed (`canApply`: a Chat's focus makes the editor, terminal and notebook focus keys false; the host's platform keys are known; an unknown key may be true). VS Code's built-in keybindings are not read, and a profile that shares only part of its settings may read the wrong file. The result is recomputed when `keybindings.json` or `vscode.extensions.onDidChange` fires (`ChatRuntime.refreshKeyHints`), never blocks activation and falls back to the manifest defaults on a read error. The empty session shows the keys in four groups with a `<kbd>` each; a conflicting key carries a warning icon whose click sends `omp:open-key-conflict`, and the host opens Keyboard Shortcuts searched for that key (`ompDesk.openKeyConflict`, `guest-dispatcher.ts`). `Ctrl+/` (`ompDesk.showChatKeyboardShortcuts`) and the composer's keyboard button open Keyboard Shortcuts filtered to `ompDesk.`. `Alt+Q` is bound under `editorTextFocus`: with a selection it quotes it, with none it inserts `composeLineReference`'s `` `path:N` `` span (`src/wiring/editor-context.ts`) through the same `omp:insert-text` path as the quote.

**Chat code highlighting** (`src/chat/code-theme.ts`, `src/host/code-theme.ts`, `src/wiring/code-theme-wiring.ts`, `src/webview/lib/code-{theme-store,theme-style,highlight,tokens,languages}.ts`, `CodeBlock.tsx`; [design](designs/2026-10-13-chat-code-highlighting.md), [ADR-0067](decisions/0067-highlight-chat-code-with-highlightjs-and-host-resolved-theme-rules.md)). A Webview has no token colours, so the host resolves the TextMate rules of the colour themes that may be active (`workbench.colorTheme` and the preferred themes of the active kind, with their `include` chains and `editor.tokenColorCustomizations`; `vscode`-free core, thin wiring that re-resolves on settings, theme and extension changes) and `ChatRuntime` posts them as `omp:chat-code-theme` (at most three themes) from `attachPage` and `resend`, whether or not a conversation exists, and `refreshCodeThemes()` on change. The page picks the entry whose id is `data-vscode-theme-id` on `<body>` (plain code when none matches), tokenizes fenced code with highlight.js, names the tokens with TextMate scopes and colours them with a matcher modelled on `vscode-textmate`; tokenizing is cached by (language, text), runs one block per queued task after first paint, skips blocks over 10,000 characters or 400 lines, and waits for an unfinished fence to settle for 400 ms. Copy always gets the plain source. The page also sets `--omp-inline-code` from the theme's string colour for inline code (text on a 14 % tint of itself; High Contrast keeps the preformatted-text colours).

Fence languages (`code-languages.ts`, amendment of ADR-0067 on 2026-10-13): 43 canonical language ids, each with a TextMate root scope and suffix, keyword and built-in tables and a word strategy, and many aliases matched case-insensitively against the fence's first word (`languageId`): ts/tsx/js, json, python, cs, powershell, shell, html/xml, css, markdown, yaml, diff, rust, go, cpp, c, java, kotlin, swift, php, ruby, sql, dockerfile, toml, ini (`.editorconfig`), properties, makefile, scala, dart, r, perl, graphql, protobuf, haskell, elixir, objectivec, groovy (`gradle`), hcl (`tf`), nginx, lua, bat (`cmd`), gitignore (`.dockerignore`). highlight.js tokenizes all but two: `gitignore` and `hcl` have no highlight.js grammar and use small scanners (`code-mini-languages.ts`) that emit highlight.js's HTML shape, so one classifier names every token. Grammars are registered on first use (`engineFor`), so only the languages a conversation shows are compiled; `text`, `plaintext`, `txt`, `mermaid` and unknown ids stay plain.

Tool card code (`use-highlighted-runs.ts`, `CodeRuns.tsx`, `CodePreview`/`DiffPreview`/`TextPreview`/`MatchOutput` in `tool-renderer-primitives.tsx`, `ToolTargetText` in `FileLinks.tsx`; ADR-0067 amendment of 2026-10-13): the code a tool card shows goes through the same tokenizer, caps, cache, queue and settle delay as a fence; `useHighlightedRuns` is the one hook (a fence, a header command and every preview use it, so streaming behaves alike: plain first paint, colour after the text has stopped changing for 400 ms, coloured text stays coloured while new text arrives). A preview's language is `languageForPath(path)` (file name first: `Dockerfile`, `Makefile`, `.gitignore`; then the extension through the fence alias table; read selectors such as `:10-40` are ignored), and an unknown one stays plain. `useCodeLines` tokenizes the whole text when it is within 10,000 characters and 400 lines, so a construct opened above the visible lines keeps its colour; a longer text is tokenized from the visible window for as many lines as fit the caps and the rest is plain. A diff is read as two texts, the old file (context and `-` lines) and the new one (context and `+` lines), each tokenized whole, so the signs, numbers and added/removed row backgrounds stay the diff's own; a construct spanning two hunks is cut at the gap. Search match lines are tokenized as one text per file. The `bash` (shell), `python` and `powershell` tools' command in the card header is coloured as its language. Plain text nodes are not split when there is no colour, so selection and the tests that read them are unchanged.

**Chat code-symbol links** (`src/shared/code-symbols.ts`, `src/host/symbol-links.ts`, `src/webview/lib/chat-symbol-links.ts`, `CodeSymbolSpan` in `FileLinks.tsx`; [design](designs/2026-10-10-code-symbol-links.md), [ADR-0058](decisions/0058-resolve-chat-code-symbols-in-the-host-window-through-workspace-symbol-providers.md)). An inline code span of **assistant reply text** (`SymbolLinksEligibleContext`, set by `AssistantContentView`, cleared in thinking) that is not a link and parses as a symbol (`parseCodeSymbol`: one to four ASCII identifiers joined by `.`, after removing generic arguments, `[]`/`?` suffixes, one call argument list and a whole-span `[Attribute]` wrapper; no keyword, no last name under three characters, no bare all-lowercase word unless called) is offered to the host once it has been on screen and after the 200 ms streaming settle. A span that is also path-shaped (`A.Bcd`) waits for the file validation to refuse it first. `ChatSymbolLinks` batches unique tokens (40 ms window, 16 per `omp:terminal-link-symbols` request, two requests in flight, 64 unsettled tokens in all, retries included, up to twice that for tokens in the viewport, which are also sent first; request ids are document-wide), caches answers (found 30 s, answered none 10 s) and retries an `unavailable` token after 5 s and again after 20 s, then rests it for 60 s. The host (`handleSymbolLinks` in the `handleTerminalGuestMessage` link case, both panel route and bridge stream operation `terminal-link-symbols`, same document/session/cwd currency check as validate/open) parses each token again, looks a batch's tokens up four at a time and asks `SymbolLinkResolver`, whose `SymbolSource` is `vscode.executeWorkspaceSymbolProvider` converted by `symbolEntry` (only `file` URIs have a path; a location with no range has no position; a name or container too long to keep is rewritten to what matching reads of it, and a container that cannot be rewritten makes the entry opaque). It queries the last segment (and once more the full dotted text when a dotted name found nothing), keeps entries inside the session cwd or an open workspace folder that contains it, matches the exact name (`NameAttribute` too for an attribute; the entry's container must end with a dotted name's preceding segments) with a kind matrix, counts distinct declaration locations, groups them into symbols (the partial parts of a type and the overloads of a member in one container are one symbol, opened at the type's own file or the first location by path and line) and answers `found` only for one symbol (same-named types in different containers: `ambiguous` with the symbol count, unless exactly one lies inside the cwd; the page links an ambiguous token to the workspace symbol search through `omp:terminal-link-open` with `search: true`, which the host re-parses and runs as `workbench.action.quickOpen` with `#<last segment>`, never to a guessed definition; a bare capitalized name prefers type-like entries over members; an opaque entry named like the symbol leaves it plain, `none`), logging one line per lookup (entry counts, matching locations, verdict, milliseconds). `unavailable` (an error, a cut answer) is never cached; `pending` (provider not ready: no query of the window has returned a symbol yet, so an empty answer is not a miss; a full queue, a timeout, an invalidated lookup, a batch that had no room or time for the token) is no verdict and never cached either, and no try of the page: the host probes a not-ready provider with a canary query (2 s doubling to 30 s, ten minutes), and when it becomes ready or its queue drains (the provider's, or the file search's after it refused lookups) it sends `omp:terminal-link-symbols-retry` to every page that asked, whose spans without an answer then ask again. Window-wide bounds: four provider calls in flight, a slot released only when the provider's own call settles, 64 waiting queries that give up after the query timeout, overflow refused at once, 8 s per query, 15 s per batch, 1,024 cached answers (found 60 s, none 15 s); a provider that has answered nothing and let a call time out is not asked more than its slots; a generation counter, bumped by a file-system watcher, saves, renames and workspace-folder changes, fences cached, in-flight and queued work, and a batch whose generation moved answers its symbol tokens `pending`. The reply carries `path:line:column` (relative to the cwd inside it) and a click is the ordinary `omp:terminal-link-open`, re-validated by the host. `ompDesk.linkCodeSymbols` (default on) turns it off: the host answers `disabled` and the page stops asking for 30 s. Limit: a workspace symbol search is ranked and may be capped (providers may cap results), not an exhaustive index, so "exactly one" means one among the entries the provider returned, and an answer Desk itself cuts (over 1,000 entries) never gives a unique match; unsaved edits reach the provider but not the invalidation until a save.

Bare file names (`session-index.ts:849`, `session-index.ts(849)`) and the trailing part of a path (`host/session-index.ts:579`) use the same exchange from a second source: `parseBareFileName` (`src/shared/code-symbols.ts`: a name, optionally with the `/`-joined directories that end its path, never anchored by a leading separator, drive, `~`, `.` or `..`) is run by page and host; the host answers a file-name token from `FileNameLinkResolver` (`src/host/file-name-links.ts`: the cwd's own file first, else `vscode.workspace.findFiles` with the exact path tail, `files.exclude`/`search.exclude`, its own limiter of 2 searches, 5 s timeout and 512-entry cache) before the symbol lookup. The cache is dropped by `SymbolLinkResolver.invalidate()` (a file created, deleted or renamed, a workspace folder change); a change or save of a file's contents calls `invalidateContent()` instead, which leaves file answers alone, and a search that was running when files changed is not discarded: each file it found is `stat`ed again and the answer is returned uncached (`unavailable` when none remains). One file is a `found` target; several are `ambiguous` with `files: true`, and the click (`omp:terminal-link-open` with `search: "file"`) is re-parsed and re-searched by `handleTerminalLink`, which asks the host to show a QuickPick of exactly those files (`lookupFileName`, `pickFile`) and opens the chosen one at the span's line. Design: the 'Bare file names' section of the code-symbol-links design.

**Catalog adoption and the startup restore** (`src/wiring/catalog-adoption.ts`, `registerCatalogAdoption`; [ADR-0034](decisions/0034-store-sessions-in-one-profile-catalog.md) amendment 2026-10-29, [ADR-0065](decisions/0065-enforce-one-import-direction-between-source-layers.md) seam list). The module reads the catalog at activation (predecessor import, first adoption, then the restore pass and the watch), adopts what the catalog's commits change, and owns the state of the startup restore (the pending flag, the pass with its cohort and kept broker connections, the deferral display-only reads wait on, `GONE_RECORD_SWEEP_DELAY_MS`). It reaches the window only through `CatalogAdoptionDeps` (the stores, the launcher, `restoreTabs`, which stays in `extension.ts` because it projects into editors and routes). Adoption is by record key: every change of the catalog, this window's own commit or a peer's change a refresh read, reaches the module as the keys it changed (`CatalogRefresh.keys`); a run reloads the broker or shell slot store only when its record changed, the folder list only when the folder record changed, and the session rows only when the session record changed, and then only the rows whose committed copy differs from the merge base (`SessionIndex.refreshFromStore({ changedOnly })`, which reports `touched` rows and `bindingsChanged`); the launcher is repainted only when a run touched something. A watcher signal reads the document once and lets the notification drive the run; an own commit reads nothing; an explicit request (focus, view shown, Refresh, activation) reads and adopts everything, so a missed notification costs a refresh and never correctness; a key a run could not adopt is kept for the next run. The run of a notification waits one event-loop turn (the committing store takes its commit as its own view after `update` returns). Measured on a 300-row catalog (10 commits each): a peer's reply-marker commit used to cost two full adoptions (2 document reads, 4 slot-store reloads, 2 folder reloads, 600 rows read, 2 repaints, 7.6 ms) and now costs one read, one row and one repaint (5.0 ms, the read being most of it); an own commit used to cost one read, 2 slot reloads, a folder reload and 300 rows twice and now costs nothing when nothing merged in.

**Session flows** (`src/wiring/session-flows.ts`, `registerSessionFlows`, handle `WindowState.sessionFlows`; a region cut of the [module-structure design](designs/2026-10-12-extension-host-module-structure.md), import direction per [ADR-0065](decisions/0065-enforce-one-import-direction-between-source-layers.md)). The compound flows of one session's editor: `openTab` (open or focus one tab: wait for the catalog, join the attempt already in flight, show the editor before any question, ask about an outside writer, admit through `SessionRuntime.open`), `openSessionInMode`, `switchSessionMode` (a stopped session's view change ends in a launch and is a `resume`, a live one is a `view-switch` under the runtime's fence), `restartChat` and `resumeFromChat`. The module keeps no state of its own: `openingTabs`, `pendingSessionViewChanges`, `draftRestores`, `editorEventSequence` and `outcomes` are read by the launcher, the editor panels and the close handlers too, so they stay in `WindowState` and arrive through `SessionFlowsDeps` as getters. What stays in `extension.ts`, each reached through a dep: `handleRestoreOutcome` and `presentRestoreOutcome` (shared with the startup restore and the late page replay), `reviveEditorPanel` (the editor panels take it as a dep too), `openSession` (the Sessions click admission and its routing), `startNewSession` and `newSessionInPlace`, `pendingEntry`, `timedLaunch` and `pushSessionView`. Tests: `src/wiring/session-flows.test.ts` pins the ordering decisions with fake deps; the lifecycle suite drives the flows through the real extension, its harness reaching them through `windowState().sessionFlows`.

**Entry-module test harness** (`src/extension-harness.ts`, `installWindowForTest` in `src/extension.ts`; the bundle entry of `src/extension-lifecycle.test.ts`). The lifecycle test no longer appends source text to `extension.ts` before bundling it. It bundles `src/extension-harness.ts`, an ordinary module that imports the functions it drives from `./extension`, builds the `harness` object over them with the fixture state the cases use, and installs a window through `installWindowForTest(next)`, the one function that assigns the module's `window` binding. `extension.ts` exports those functions for it (a comment above the first of them says so); they are not an API, and `scripts/check-imports.mjs` lets exactly this file import `extension.ts` and refuses any non-test file that imports it. This is the smallest equivalent of a `createWindow(deps)` factory, not that factory: the module-level `windowState()` singleton, three module `let` bindings (`window`, `legacyContextMirror`, `resolvingExecutable`) and about 320 top-level functions remain in `extension.ts`, and the test still replaces `vscode` and the native transports by import specifier in its esbuild plugin. A real factory would first turn the remaining function groups into closures over a window or into wiring modules like the two above (the groups are readable from the file: for example the tab and editor-panel lifecycle, Chat event and guest-message routing, provider login and model refresh, the folder-shell paths, the launcher tree and its ownership facts), then take the `vscode` namespace and the transports as constructor dependencies; only then would the harness call the factory and the plugin's specifier substitution go. None of that is done here.

**Chat internal OMP URL links** (`src/shared/internal-links.ts`, `src/host/internal-links.ts`, `src/host/session-file-lookup.ts`, `src/wiring/session-file-documents.ts`, `InternalLink` in `FileLinks.tsx`; [design](designs/2026-10-10-code-symbol-links.md), section "Internal OMP URLs"). An `agent://<id>`, `history://<id>`, `artifact://<n>` or `local://<path>` in assistant text, inline code or a Markdown destination uses the file-link exchange (`omp:terminal-link-validate` / `-open`, target = the canonical URL, no new message). `extension.ts` routes a request whose target `parseInternalLink` accepts to `handleInternalLink` before `handleTerminalLink`; the host resolves it against the conversation's own session: the roster's agent detail tab (`RpcSession.subagentKnown`), else the agent's output `<id>.md` or a `local/` file as a read-only `omp-session-file:` document (registered first in `activate`; `onFileSystem:omp-session-file`), or the `omp-artifact:` document. The lookup never follows a link out of the artifacts directory, and an unresolved URL stays plain text.

**Chat commit-hash links** (`src/shared/commit-links.ts`, `src/host/commit-links.ts`, `src/wiring/commit-links.ts`, `CommitLinks.tsx`; [design](designs/2026-10-17-commit-hash-links.md), amended 2026-10-27 twice). A 7 to 40 digit hex inline-code span, or a lower-case hash with a digit and a letter in assistant prose, uses the file-link exchange with the target `commit://<hex>` (no new message). `extension.ts` routes a request whose target `parseCommitLinkTarget` accepts to `handleCommitLink` before the internal-URL and file handlers. `CommitLinkResolver` asks `getCommit` of the Git extension API (`wiring/commit-links.ts`) in the repositories containing the conversation's cwd, deepest first, keeps a hit only when its full hash starts with the hex (git rejects an ambiguous abbreviation), and bounds the work (one lookup per hash, two at once, 3 s for a whole lookup with the slot wait included, below the page's 4 s validation deadline; a minute for a commit and 3 s for a miss). A hash is a link whenever it names a commit of that repository, HEAD or not; validation never looks at HEAD, so there is no host-to-page signal and no per-repository watcher. A click, for every commit alike, ends in the built-in Git extension's `git.viewCommit` with the repository's root `Uri` and the **full hash the lookup found** (never the page's text), which opens the commit's changes as a multi-file diff editor. Before that, when the commit is the one HEAD points at, the click selects it in the Source Control Graph, the only commit the built-in Graph can select (`workbench.scm.history.focus`, then `workbench.scm.action.graph.revealCurrentHistoryItem`; neither takes an argument, no command takes a hash): HEAD is read at the click (`repository.status()` bounded to 2 s, then `state.HEAD.commit`, since the remembered lookup and the Git extension's state both lag), the Graph is driven only when this is the one repository the Git extension has open (the Graph shows a repository the workbench picks and an extension cannot read or set), and a Graph step that fails or is refused is logged and never stops the diff, which runs last so it keeps the focus. The Graph-filter notice and its `state.vscdb` read stay removed. The wiring checks that the Git extension still has the repository open before either step, because `git.viewCommit` shows a repository picker for a root it does not know. A commit that no longer exists, or a repository that cannot show it, gives a notice and leaves the link as it is. Every click writes one info line to the OMP Desk log naming the steps (`commit link <hash12>: view commit`, `commit link <hash12>: HEAD, select in graph, view commit`, `…: HEAD, select in graph failed: <reason>, view commit`, `…: view commit failed: <reason>`, or `commit link <hex>: nothing shown, no such commit in this session's repository`). `CommitHashSpan` asks as a commit first and renders the code-symbol span only when the host says there is none.

**Chat link rules** (`src/shared/link-rules.ts`, `src/host/link-rules.ts`, `src/wiring/link-rules.ts`, `src/webview/lib/link-rules-store.ts`, `LinkRules.tsx`; [design](designs/2026-10-27-chat-link-rules.md)). The user's own regular expressions and URL templates (`ompDesk.chatLinkRules`, `application` scope; the host reads `inspect().globalValue` only, so a workspace or folder value, a repository's `.vscode/settings.json` included, is never read) draw matching text of an assistant reply as links that open a web address like every Chat web link (an editor tab on a click, the external browser on Ctrl+Click; the page sends only that gesture as `mode`, the host builds the URL). `shared/link-rules.ts` is the one copy of every rule: `compileLinkRule` (shape, pattern screen: no back-references, no repeat (but `?`) of a group that holds a repeat or an alternative, a budget for variable repeats (two unbounded, or one unbounded and one `{n,m}` up to 40, or three of those, each `?` counting 1) and at most 8 quantifiers, no match of the empty text; URL template: `http(s)` with a static host, `$0`..`$9`/`$$` placeholders), `buildLinkRuleUrl` (the rule is run on the matched characters alone, each value `encodeURIComponent`ed, the result must pass `webLinkUrl` and keep the template's host), `findLinkRuleMatches` (bounded scans: the first 2000 characters in pieces of at most 500 cut at whitespace, 32 matches, 200-character matches; the earlier rule wins an overlap) and the `link-rule://<position>/<text>` click target. The host sends the screened rules as the epoch-free window-wide `omp:chat-link-rules` (every time a page announces itself, the empty list included, and, through `ChatRuntime.refreshLinkRules()` and the runtime port, to every attached page when the setting changes; the model is `omp:chat-keys`); problems are reported once per distinct value (`createLinkRuleSource`: log and one notice). The page keeps the rules in a module-level store and compiles every entry again; `Markdown.tsx` re-parses from scratch when the store's version changes. In prose `textLinks()` ranks a web, internal or `proc://` address above a rule match and a rule above a commit hash, a match inside a longer path-shaped token is left to the path, and an inline code span links only when a rule matches all of it; user text, tool output, thinking and fenced code stay text (`AssistantReplyContext`). A click is the existing `omp:terminal-link-open` with only a `link-rule://` target (no URL, mode or action): `extension.ts` routes it to `handleLinkRuleOpen` before the commit, internal-address and file handlers, which finds the rule in the user's current settings by position, runs `buildLinkRuleUrl` on the text and opens the result with `openPageWebLink(url, "external")`; a page never supplies a URL.

**Chat diagrams** (`src/webview/components/MermaidBlock.tsx`, `lib/mermaid-render.ts`, `lib/mermaid-theme.ts`, `src/webview/mermaid/`; [ADR-0069](decisions/0069-load-mermaid-as-a-separate-local-script-and-show-diagrams-as-svg-images.md), [design](designs/2026-10-13-chat-mermaid-diagrams.md)). `Markdown.tsx` sends a fence whose first word is `mermaid` (any case) to `MermaidBlock` with the same `closed` flag `CodeBlock` gets. An unclosed block is the code block; a closed one asks `lib/mermaid-render.ts` for a diagram. The renderer is `media/mermaid.js`, a classic script of its own (esbuild target `mermaid`, options in `scripts/mermaid-bundle.mjs`: `mermaid` 12.2.0, 3.48 MB, `elkjs` and `katex` aliased to throwing stubs), added to the document by the page on the first closed block as a sibling of its own script (`document.currentScript.src`) with the document nonce. `guest.js`, the policy (`script-src 'nonce-N' ${cspSource}`, `style-src 'nonce-N' ${cspSource}`, `style-src-attr 'unsafe-inline'`, `img-src … data:`), `localResourceRoots` and the host are unchanged, and a page with no diagram never requests it. The script publishes `ompMermaid = { contract, render }` (`src/webview/mermaid/contract.ts`); a page and a script of different builds refuse each other (`contract`). `src/webview/mermaid/entry.ts` configures mermaid on every theme change: `securityLevel: "strict"`, `htmlLabels: false`, `suppressErrorRendering`, `layout: "dagre"`, `maxTextSize` 8,000, `maxEdges` 200 (flowcharts), theme `base`, and a `secure` list that also holds `theme`, `themeVariables`, `themeCSS`, `layout`, `look`, `fontFamily`, `fontSize`, `htmlLabels` and `darkMode`, so a diagram's own `%%{init}%%` or front matter cannot change them; mermaid creates its `<style>` through `document.createElement`, which `adoptNonceForCreatedStyles` (ADR-0028) stamps. `lib/mermaid-theme.ts` resolves the page's theme variables to hex colours (`themeVariables`), a theme key names them, and a `MutationObserver` on `<html>` (`style`, `class`) and `<body>` (`class`, `data-vscode-theme-id`) re-keys and notifies the mounted blocks. Results (diagram or failure) are cached by theme key and source in a bounded LRU (8 MB of text); a one-at-a-time queue, one task per diagram, draws them, and a block unmounted before its turn withdraws. The SVG is shown as an `<img src="data:image/svg+xml">` with explicit width and height from its `viewBox` (decoded once off screen first, so a broken image is a failure, not an icon), so it cannot run script, load a resource or touch the page. The swap from code to image is one accepted reflow; a remount or a returning theme paints the image in its first render. A source over 8,000 characters or 80 statements is refused before mermaid loads (the caps bound a render at about half a second warm in the types measured; block diagrams are never drawn), and only the assistant's own reply text is drawn (`AssistantReplyContext`: a user message, thinking and a tool result keep the code block); every failure is the code block plus one muted `Diagram not rendered: …` line. Diagram | Source, Copy (the source) and click-to-full-size are remembered per source for the life of the page. **Open diagram in a tab** (ADR-0069 amendment of 2026-10-14): a block the limits refuse (or a diagram drawn too large) shows that button; it posts `omp:open-mermaid { source }` (panel-only, at most 50,000 characters, `protocol/mermaid-preview.ts`), and `host/mermaid-previews.ts` opens or reveals one `ompDesk.mermaidPreview` tab per SHA-256 of the source (`createMermaidPreviewHtml`: the guest policy, `connect-src 'none'`, `media/mermaid.js` then `media/mermaid-preview.js`, and an `omp-mermaid-scope` meta tag that gives the renderer in that document the wider tab limits). The tab (`webview/mermaid-preview/`) asks for its source with `omp:mermaid-preview-ready`, shows **Rendering…** before the render, has Fit/zoom/100 % and Copy source, follows the theme (a slow render waits for **Redraw**), and shows the message and the source on failure; closing the tab is the only cancel. Leftover tabs are swept with the detail tabs. Tests: `lib/mermaid-theme.test.ts` (colours), `host/mermaid-previews.test.ts`, `protocol/mermaid-preview.test.ts` and `mermaid-ui.test.ts` (the real app, the real renderer, the tab's document and the real document policy in headless Chromium).

**Chat figures, charts and math** (`src/webview/components/{SvgFigureBlock,TableChart,MathBlock}.tsx`, `lib/{svg-figure,figure-palette,table-chart,latex-math,latex-unicode,latex-block}.ts`, `figure-styles.ts`, `chart-styles.ts`, `math-styles.ts`; [design](designs/2026-10-19-chat-rich-markdown.md), [ADR-0078](decisions/0078-show-latex-math-as-unicode-text-from-a-project-owned-converter.md)). Three features of OMP's terminal UI, all in `Markdown.tsx`'s block parse and `lib/markdown-inline.ts`, no new dependency, no policy change. A closed `svg` fence that is top-level (`BlockContext.inList` and `quoted` false) goes to `SvgFigureBlock`: `prepareSvgFigure` tokenises the source itself (no DOM), keeps only an allow-list of elements and attributes, resolves the TUI's `var(--fg|…|c6)` tokens from the page theme (`figurePaletteFrom(MermaidTheme)`), and the page shows the result only as a `data:image/svg+xml` `<img>`; the figure is drawn only when `AssistantReplyContext` is true. A top-level table of the assistant's reply is followed, in the same block (a Fragment), by `TableChart`: `planTableChart` ports the TUI's local chart rules for bar, grouped bar and line, and the chart is React-generated inline SVG coloured by `--vscode-charts-*` variables, `aria-hidden` under a `role="img"` wrapper; the last table of a message waits 600 ms unchanged (`CHART_SETTLE_MS`) and a page-wide `Hide chart` is the opt-out. Math is a token of the inline grammar: `scanInline` (shared by `renderInline` and `inlinePieces`, so Find and Quote agree) merges the regex tokens with the spans of `nextMathSpan` (the first to start wins; off when `emphasis` is false and on lines over 4,000 characters) and a span's shown text is `latexToUnicode(body)`; an own-line `$$`/`\[` block or bare math environment (`mathBlockAt`) is a `MathBlock` whose leaf has the code leaf's shape (shown lines `latexToBlock(body)`). Because a display block may close far below the tail, `MarkdownParse.openMath` turns off the streaming block reuse of `parseMarkdown` while an own-line opener has no closer.

**Tool output in Chat** ([design](designs/2026-10-14-chat-tool-output.md), [ADR-0067](decisions/0067-highlight-chat-code-with-highlightjs-and-host-resolved-theme-rules.md) amendment). An opened tool call (`components/ToolCard.tsx`) is one head, one output bar and one body. The head carries the result gist (`src/chat/tool-gist.ts`, pure: exit code, size, exact duration; a duration derived from the assistant entry's timestamp is marked `≈` and, like an observed one, shown only above 1.5 s; the page records the observed duration of a call whose `tool_execution_start` it saw, `ActiveTool.startObserved`). OMP's appended notices are cut from the text by `src/chat/tool-notices.ts` and become one footer line with the `artifact://N` link. The body rests as a 12-row preview (`components/tool-view.tsx`, `OutputRows`: tail for Bash and Eval, head for the rest, exact markers) and `Show all` switches the card to the **windowed full view** (`lib/use-windowed-rows.ts`: fixed-height rows in the transcript's normal flow, one scroller, DOM bounded whatever the length; sticky toolbar with Collapse, filter, wrap, Copy and `⋯`; screen-reader mode renders the text whole up to a bound). What the card remembers beyond being open (full, format, folds, filter, wrap, raw) lives by call in `lib/tool-view-state.ts`, outside the card, and is forgotten when the card closes. Detectors run on settled text only (`lib/output-format.ts`): JSON and newline-delimited JSON as folding trees from a linear lexer over the original text (`lib/json-tokens.ts`, `lib/json-rows.ts`, `components/JsonRows.tsx`, 1 MiB bound, coloured by the host-resolved theme rules, exempt from the highlight caps), then tables and stack traces (`lib/output-rows.ts`: aligned columns, frames dimmed and linked through the existing `omp:terminal-link-*` exchange), with error, warning and pass line tint; each has a badge and a **Formatted | Text** switch. The `⋯` menu (`components/ToolMenu.tsx`: `role="menu"`, roving focus, the shared `useDismissibleLayer`) offers Copy command, output and details, **View raw result and details** (`lib/raw-details.ts`: the result as the model saw it and `details` cut at 300-character strings and 12-item arrays; Copy details cuts strings at 20,000 characters and never carries an image payload) and **Open full output**. The artifact route is `omp:open-artifact {artifactId, epoch}` (`protocol/messages.ts`; the id is 1 to 9 digits and the message names no path). All three entry points call `GuestDispatcher.openArtifact`: the guest dispatcher, `handlePassiveSlotMessage` in `extension.ts` and `DetailTabsHost.openArtifact` (`host/detail-tabs.ts`). It drops a request whose epoch is not the conversation's current one, takes the session file from the index and asks `wiring/artifact-documents.ts` to open `omp-artifact:/<id>/<title>?session=<encoded transcript path>` read-only (the last path segment, such as `Full output · bash · artifact 0`, is the editor tab title; a bare `/<id>` of an older tab still resolves). That module registers the content provider as the first statement of `activate` (`onFileSystem:omp-artifact` in `activationEvents`), so an editor tab Reload Window restores is served too; the provider and the route share `host/artifact-lookup.ts`, which on every read requires an absolute `.jsonl` path with an OMP session header, lists the artifacts directory (the transcript path without `.jsonl`; a sub-agent's transcript sits in the parent's) without recursion, accepts only a file named `<id>.<type>.<ext>` that is a regular file by `lstat` whose `realpath` stays inside the directory, and reads at most 16 MiB (a larger file is named in the document, never read). A surviving bridge page cannot send the message (`lib/artifact-opener.ts` reads `routeKind`), so its menu item is disabled with the reason. In a group (`components/ToolOverview.tsx`) the open row offers `Expand all N` / `Collapse all` over the members' own disclosures (not the tool display mode, `Ctrl+O` / `Ctrl+Shift+O`), and `lib/tool-navigation.ts` moves focus between mounted heads with the arrow keys, Home and End, which `lib/transcript-scroll.ts` does not take for scrolling. `Esc` closes the `⋯` menu or clears the filter first (both stop the event), then the card, then the group.

**Chat prompt** (`src/omp/chat-prompt.ts`). Chat features that depend on how the model formats a reply say so in one short section appended to the system prompt: a fixed preamble ("The user reads your replies in OMP Desk, a chat panel in VS Code") and the list `CHAT_PROMPT_INJECTIONS`, one rule per feature (today `file-links`, and `code-links`, which asks the model to link every code symbol at each mention, tables and lists included, as a Markdown link to the definition line it has seen in a read or search, `` [`App.start`](src/app.ts:12) ``, or to the file alone when that is all it knows, `` [`Parser`](src/parse.ts) ``, to leave plain only a symbol it has not located, and never to guess a line or search just for one; `Markdown.tsx` renders inline code in a link label as code and links it once the host proves the file, the same path as any Markdown file link; a symbol the model leaves plain is the host's to link, see **Chat code-symbol links**). The `-e` host-control module calls `registerChatPrompt`, whose `before_agent_start` handler returns the system prompt with the section appended, only for an RPC main session (`ctx.mode === "rpc"`, `ctx.agent.kind === "main"`): the native TUI renders replies itself, and a subagent answers its parent. Like Rewind it needs only the module loaded with a non-absent bootstrap. The text is constant, so the provider's cache prefix stays stable. Every rule costs tokens on every request, so the module header sets the guideline for adding one (one imperative rule, an inline example instead of a description, nothing Markdown already implies, no runtime values) and `chat-prompt.test.ts` holds each rule to 280 characters and the section to 1,500. Observed with installed OMP 18.8.5 in `--mode rpc`: the model quoted the appended section and followed it. A second, per-message reminder (`LINK_REMINDER`, one paragraph of about 340 characters starting `[OMP Desk reminder]`: files and folders as paths in inline code, code symbols as links) follows every user prompt of each provider request, when `ompDesk.linkReminder` is on (default off): the module's `context` handler (the extension `context` event, emitted on every request with a copy of the messages) inserts a separate `role: "user"`, `synthetic` message with that text right after each prompt that is not itself synthetic or a history rewrite, unless one is already there. The prompt object is not touched. It reaches the model only: the session file and Chat never contain it. It is a separate message because OMP marks any message it finds rewritten for a request as per-call and the Anthropic provider never anchors a cache breakpoint on or before such a message: appending the text to the prompt cost an uncached first request (about 12k tokens in a fresh session), while an inserted message leaves the breakpoint on the real prompt. The text is the same for every prompt, so earlier requests' bytes never change. The setting reaches the module as `OMP_VSCODE_LINK_REMINDER` (`1`/`0`), set by `rpcOmpLaunchSpec` when the session starts or resumes; a change applies to sessions launched afterwards ([design](designs/2026-10-10-code-symbol-links.md)).

**Chat attachments: images and documents** (`src/webview/lib/draft/attachments.ts`, `draft/attached-documents.ts`, `draft/draft-documents.ts`, `draft/attach.ts`, `attach-reader.ts`, `attach-dom.ts`, `components/AttachmentStrip.tsx`, `read-linked.ts`, `Composer.tsx`, `src/host/attached-files.ts`, `src/shared/file-blocks.ts`, `src/shared/text-file.ts`, `src/shared/attach-limits.ts`; [design](designs/2026-10-16-chat-document-attachments.md)). The numbers live in one place each: `MAX_CHAT_IMAGE_BYTES` = 5 MiB, `MAX_CHAT_IMAGES_BYTES` = 10 MiB (`protocol/chat-messages.ts`), and `MAX_ATTACH_ITEMS` = 1000, `MAX_MESSAGE_CHARS` = 200 000 and `MAX_DOCUMENT_BYTES` (`shared/attach-limits.ts`); there is no count limit below `MAX_ATTACH_ITEMS`, so the composer derives what it enforces from them. A file is classified by its first bytes, never by its name or type (`classifyFile` in `attach-reader.ts`, `readAttachedFiles` in the host, the same `text-file.ts` rule): a supported image is an image, an unsupported image format or a binary is refused by name, and any other UTF-8 text without control characters is a **document**, an empty one included. The algorithm of a paste, a drop or a pick (the count guard, the classification, the byte and character budgets that two overlapping attaches share through reservations in the draft value, the markers, every refusal sentence) is `attach` of the Draft module (`draft/attach.ts`) against the cell and an `AttachReader`, so it is tested in node with a fake reader; `attach-reader.ts` holds the real `File` readers and `attach-dom.ts` the `DataTransfer` helpers that stay with the composer's events. An image is read in the page as base64 and travels in the prompt message to `RpcSession`, which puts it in OMP's `prompt`, `steer` or `follow_up` line. A document is a `[File #N: name]` marker in the draft (one atomic chip, the same reference finder as `[Image #N]`, `findReferences` in `image-references.ts`) and a tile under the field. A file the page holds as bytes (a paste, a Shift-drop) is **held**: its text is decoded in the page. A file the host attached by path (the Attach button's dialog, a drag from VS Code's Explorer or an editor tab, `omp:attach-uris`, `omp:pick-files`) is **linked**: the page holds only an opaque per-tab `ref`, its size and its character count, never the path, and a **send** posts `omp:read-files` and waits for `omp:files-read` so the model gets the file as it is on disk then (a failure of any file refuses the send and keeps the draft; an open editor with unsaved changes is told to the user). The sent text is the draft's prose followed by one block per document, `<file path="…" n="N" chars="C">`, the escaped text and `</file>` (`composeFileBlocks`), parsed back only for a user message and a skill card (`splitFileBlocks`: the chain must end exactly at the end of the text, numbers ascend, the prose holds each marker, and `C` is exact) and drawn as one closed row per file with its own Copy; a text that does not parse is prose. **Decision 25:** at send, U+200B is inserted inside the file's text before every sigil OMP would act on (`@` mentions, `^model`, `+N` turn budgets and `/skill:` tokens, by OMP's own boundary rules; `escapeFileText`), so a file's text is never read by OMP as an instruction; the draft's own typed text is not escaped, `C` counts the escaped text, and `unescapeFileText` removes exactly what was inserted (decided from the stored text alone, so a file that already holds U+200B round-trips) wherever Desk shows or restores the text. Every restore of a sent message (rewind, queue edit, ↑/↓, hand-off) goes through `draftFromSentText` and becomes held documents. The cross-session prompt history stores a message with each block reduced to its path and no text (`reduceFileBlocks`), and a recall re-attaches those files by reference or says which one could not come back. The bounds a prompt crosses: the grammar above; the broker's stdin line (`PTY_RPC_MAX_WRITE_BYTES`, 16 MiB, the tightest bound on the panel route: 10 MiB of images is 13.3 MiB of base64 and the longest text 1.2 MB escaped, a sum `chat-messages.test.ts` checks); and, on the fallback bridge route only, one sealed frame of 256 KiB per request, because only the host fragments (`bridge-fragments.ts`), so `BridgeClient.canCarry` makes the composer refuse a prompt (an image or a document) that route cannot carry instead of losing the connection, and the pick and drop routes that need the host are not offered there. OMP normalizes every attached image before the model and the session see it (`normalizeModelContextImages`: at most 1568 px a side, about 500 KB) and keeps the bytes out of the session file in its content-addressed blob store (`blob:sha256:` references), which `history-blobs.ts` resolves for the transcript (one blob up to 16 MiB, 64 MiB per read); a document is part of the user text, which OMP stores verbatim. The base64 check is a linear scan (`isCanonicalBase64Text`): the group-repetition regular expression overflows V8's stack on a multi-megabyte string. A drag from VS Code's Explorer or an editor tab carries `file:` addresses, not files: the page sends `omp:attach-uris` (panel route only) and the host reads each local-drive path that is a regular file within the limits (`readAttachedFiles`; no network share from a drop, other scheme or device path) and answers `omp:attached-files` with images, documents (a `ref`, never the text) and one sentence per refusal. The workbench delivers a drag over a webview to the page only while Shift is held (a drag that first enters over the page itself is the one exception, which the composer's document-level listeners keep from the shell script); no setting or API changes that, so the attach tooltip and the empty chat's key hints say "Shift+drag".

**Why an unshifted file drag only sometimes reaches the Chat (VS Code 1.142.0, commit `6d62b1d5`; read in its `workbench.desktop.main.js` and `contrib/webview/browser/pre/index.html`, and reproduced with CDP `Input.dispatchDragEvent` in a hidden window, 2026-10-16).** The page is not in control of this. Each visible webview editor runs a window-level drag monitor (`WebviewWindowDragMonitor`, created when the editor becomes visible and disposed when it is hidden): on the workbench window's `dragstart`, `drag` and `dragover` it sets the webview `iframe`'s `style.pointerEvents` to `none` unless `shiftKey` is set (then `auto`), and it sets `auto` on `dragend` or on a `mousemove` with `buttons === 0`. A drag from outside the window (the file manager) fires no `dragstart`, and no event over the iframe reaches the window, so the iframe is blocked the first time the drag passes over any other workbench element (tab bar, side bar, title bar); the editor drop target (`editor.dropIntoEditor`, overlay `editor-group-overlay`) then takes the drop and opens the file. Only a drag that enters straight over the page is delivered to it. The webview shell adds the same: a `dragenter` the page did not `preventDefault` (file items, no Shift) posts `drag-start`, and every `dragover` is forwarded to the window, so `Composer`'s document-level `dragenter`/`dragover`/`drop` listeners call `preventDefault` and `stopPropagation` for an acceptable drag, which keeps both messages from being sent. Measured in one hidden window with a 17-byte file (the 2.6 MB synthetic file is refused cleanly in the same flow, `reading` back to 0): direct entry reaches the page (`dragenter` 1, `dragover` 2, `drop` 1, the iframe style untouched); entry through the tab strip leaves the iframe at `none`, the page sees nothing and the overlay is present; Shift over the page sets `auto` and the page receives the drag. **The block outlives the drop that opened the file.** After a tab-strip entry that was dropped (the file opened in an editor, the Chat tab hidden) and a return to the Chat tab by keyboard (`Ctrl+PageUp`), the Chat iframe is still `none` and three direct-entry drags in a row reach the page not at all. Nothing in the monitor's code unblocks it except the `mousemove` and `dragend` listeners, and the table is what lifts it (each row started from that same state; "then drag" is a direct-entry drag that ended in a drop):

| What was done | Iframe afterwards | Next direct drag reaches the Chat |
|---|---|---|
| nothing (three drags, no mouse movement) | `none` | no, three times |
| a real mouse move over the window (`mouseMoved`, no button) | `auto` | yes, and three more times |
| a click in the Chat page, or a click on the Chat tab | `auto` | yes |
| a Shift drag over the Chat | `auto` | yes (the Shift drag itself arrives) |
| Esc | `none` | no |
| hiding and showing the Chat tab by keyboard | `none` | no |
