| a synthetic window `blur` + `focus` (dispatched events, not a real focus change) | `none` | no |

The stale block therefore lasts only until the next mouse movement over the window. After a real drop the user moves the mouse, which lifts it; it is not a sticky state of the page or of the extension (the page's reading counters and its listeners settle after a refused 2.6 MB file; see the regression test in `chat-ui.test.ts`). It is met when the Chat is brought back without moving the mouse (a keyboard switch, as measured). Reload Window recreates the iframe and with it the style; that case was not run. The maintainer's report that small files failed after a huge log was the ordinary blocked drag: they confirmed (2026-10-16) that an unshifted drop fails only when the file is carried over another tab or panel on its way to the Chat, and the large file only coincided. The page cannot detect a blocked drag (it gets no event), no API lets a webview receive an unshifted drag, and the host has no drop event for it, so the product answers with Shift in the Attach tooltip, the empty chat's hint and the guide.

**Chat web links.** Web links follow the same convention and request pair, without a validation round trip. `webLinkUrl` (`src/shared/terminal-links.ts`) accepts only an absolute `http:`/`https:` URL with a host and returns its normalized form; `detectWebLinks` (`src/webview/lib/chat-web-links.ts`) finds bare URLs in prose, starting only at a word boundary and stopping before trailing sentence punctuation and closing brackets the URL never opened (GitHub's autolink rule). `Markdown.tsx` links `[label](https://…)`, `<https://…>` autolinks and bare URLs (a bare URL is one inline token, so `_`/`*` inside it never start emphasis); inline and fenced code stay text, and any other destination (`javascript:`, `data:`, `command:`, `mailto:`, …) keeps its label and loses its URL. `WebLinks.tsx` renders a focusable `role="link"` span, never an `<a href>`, with the tooltip "Open in editor · Ctrl+Click to open in browser"; a click or Enter posts `omp:terminal-link-open` with `mode: "editor"`, Ctrl+Click or Ctrl+Enter with `mode: "external"`, and the handler prevents the default action and stops propagation so the page never navigates and Ctrl+Enter never reaches the Send keybinding. Its provider wraps both the session page and the TODO/Agents detail documents, so web links work in the transcript, tool output (web-search and GitHub sources included) and the detail tabs. The wire accepts `mode` only on an open request. The host re-validates with `webLinkUrl` before anything opens (`handleTerminalLink`, and `DetailTabs` for detail documents, which accept no other link request) and opens through `openWebLink` (`src/host/terminal-links.ts`): the built-in Simple Browser extension's `simpleBrowser.api.open` for an editor tab, and `vscode.env.openExternal` for the browser; only the origin is logged. A user link-rule link (`link-rule://…`) takes the same two modes: the host builds the URL from the rule and opens it through `openWebLink`. If the editor command is missing or throws, `openWebLink` falls back to `openExternal` and the host logs a warning with the origin and the reason. In the installed VS Code 1.141 and 1.143.1 the Simple Browser command hands the URL to VS Code's integrated browser (an editor tab with its own address bar), and `openExternal` first shows VS Code's own "Do you want Code to open the external website?" prompt for a domain the user has not trusted. Terminal mode uses the same path: an OSC 8 `http(s)` link OMP draws (Markdown links and autolinks in replies, login and pull-request links) opens in an editor tab on a click and in the browser on Ctrl+Click, with the same tooltip; xterm has no plain-text URL detector here, so a URL OMP prints without OSC 8 is not a link in Terminal.

**Terminal streams and input.** Each pipeline has a fresh random presentation identity, distinct from the unchanged authenticated PTY identity. Healthy probes preserve output, selection and scrollback; ordered missing bytes or another presentation trigger explicit snapshot recovery. Only snapshot-follows adopts a new stream, and only the snapshot resets xterm. Bounded target-frontend buffering maps the broker's UTF-16 snapshot cut to dense output sequences so concurrent post-cut Unicode is not lost. Broker claim/owner events, not DOM focus, authorize input; visible eligible owners retain permission through blur, while hidden/passive/disposed panes release it. Genuine activation/click intent alone permits takeover, including an already-focused textarea click ([ADR-0044](decisions/0044-resynchronize-on-stream-discontinuity-and-retain-broker-ownership.md)). Native Shift+Enter sends OMP's multiline sequence. Single-use activation tokens restore keyboard focus on actual activation/document readiness/window-focus return, never model/probe ticks. Host clipboard copying admits stopped/passive screen reads but requires the latest current-document/generation-bound user nonce; oversized serialized replies refuse rather than truncate.

**Terminal screen restore.** The broker serializes up to about a million characters of screen and scrollback, but one `omp:terminal-snapshot` message carries at most 131,070 decoded bytes. A longer retained screen is trimmed by the host (`fitTerminalSnapshot`): whole oldest lines are dropped, attributes are reset at the cut and the visible rows, cursor and modes (or an alternate screen) are never cut, so a long session reattaches with its recent screen instead of failing. Output that outruns a reading triggers a fresh reading (three attempts) instead of an error; only a visible screen that alone exceeds one frame, or output that never lets a reading settle, shows the plain sentence "The earlier screen of this terminal … new output will still appear".

**Terminal repaint after restore.** A restored screen is replayed into an xterm that must show the grid the PTY really has: the owning pane renders at its own fit (a pane that has not yet been granted input first shows the host's grid, and is refitted once it owns), otherwise OMP's differential renderer draws for one grid while the emulator shows another, leaving stale status lines and a cursor one row off. After a snapshot reaches a visible owning native OMP pane, the host nudges the PTY one column narrower and back once (`TerminalPipeline` `redrawAfterRestore`, native `managed-omp` sessions only; shells are not nudged), so OMP repaints its whole screen from its own state. A surviving broker of an older build is detected and left running (it owns the PTY, and restarting it would end the OMP process); the repaint heals what its older mirror gets wrong. See the [native terminal design](designs/2026-10-03-native-terminal-ux.md).

**Terminal resize after reload.** A revived editor is laid out over several frames, so its first fit (the one sent with `omp:terminal-attach`) is a row or two off the settled one. The owning pane therefore reports its fit to the host only after it has held still (`RESIZE_SETTLE_MS`) and restates it whenever it regains input or asks for a screen again; the host never applies the attach-time fit, runs every grid write (page resizes and the repaint nudge) through one queue, nudges the grid the broker reports at that moment, and sends nothing for a resize to the grid the broker already has. Without this the PTY went 46 -> 44 -> 45 -> 46 rows within 60 ms of each reload and OMP repainted at every value. See the [native terminal design](designs/2026-10-03-native-terminal-ux.md).

Authority-fence changes publish terminal state immediately, independently of the queued broker ownership work. Even a fence that is set and lifted before that queue runs must produce the input-loss and restoration edges: the page clears its last-reported fit on loss and restates the settled grid on restoration. This prevents a resize refused during role reconciliation from remaining cached as delivered when the broker owner itself never changed. Read-only and hidden panes still render the host's grid and cannot resize it.

**Snapshot cursor.** The serialized screen is given an absolute cursor position (`placeSnapshotCursor`, from the mirror's own cursor) after everything the serializer wrote: `SerializeAddon` returns the cursor with relative moves, and after a full-width last row (OMP's footer) xterm.js's cursor-backward starts from the last column, so the restored cursor landed one column left of the prompt's until OMP next moved it.

**Redraw Terminal on demand.** `OMP Desk: Redraw Terminal` (command palette, the editor title overflow beside **Copy screen**, and Ctrl+Alt+Shift+R / Cmd+Alt+Shift+R, a chord no OMP or VS Code default binds) runs the same one-column nudge for the active native OMP pane (`TerminalPipeline.redrawNow`). It is for the moments OMP's own differential renderer and the terminal disagree about rows for a reason outside this extension (the program's output itself overwrites earlier rows); the program repaints from its own state, nothing is hidden or rewritten, and a folder shell, a hidden pane or a pane that does not own input is refused with a message rather than nudged. The terminal swallows the chord so the program never receives it (Ctrl+Shift+L would reach OMP as `^L`, its live-mode key).

**Workbench keys from anywhere.** Ctrl+Shift+L runs `ompDesk.focusComposer` with no `when` clause: the active session editor, else the one showing in the chat group, else the index's active tab, is revived if VS Code has not handed its panel over yet, revealed with focus, and a Chat page gets the `focus-composer` panel action; with no session editor it opens the OMP Desk side bar (`workbench.view.extension.ompDesk`). (It replaced Ctrl+Shift+Q, which took the same command from anywhere; Ctrl+Shift+L is VS Code's Select All Occurrences of Find Match key, which the contributed binding takes over and a user keybinding can take back.) Ctrl+Shift+M runs `ompDesk.moveFileToNewWindow`, which takes every tab of the group next to the chat (`filesGroupColumn`: the active group without a session editor, else the leftmost one, never the file window's own group) into the file window: one auxiliary window, kept on top, that every file opened from a Chat link joins until it is closed (see the chat column below). It is a toggle: while that window exists, the command returns its tabs (`returnFilesFromWindow`) to the existing files group, else to a new group beside the chat group, and forgets the window. It replaces VS Code's default Ctrl+Shift+M (Problems), and Cycle Model has no default key. Between two extensions VS Code picks by load order, so a keymap extension binding the chord under `editorTextFocus` (IntelliJ IDEA Keybindings: Move Caret to Matching Brace) can win in text editors; only a user keybinding outranks it. `contributes.configurationDefaults` lists both commands in `terminal.integrated.commandsToSkipShell`, so the integrated terminal passes the keys to the workbench instead of the shell; a user value for that setting replaces the contributed default and must list the commands itself. A Terminal-mode pane claims both chords, and Ctrl+Alt+Q, like Redraw (`isTerminalWorkbenchChord`): they reach the workbench and are never encoded for the program, which would read them as Ctrl+L (a form feed) and Ctrl+M (a carriage return).

**Focus VS Code takes back from the page.** When a window gains the operating-system focus, VS Code's `WebviewEditor` focuses its active webview again: the workbench runs `contentWindow.focus()` on the webview's outer frame, and 50 ms later the webview host focuses the page's frame. Chromium empties the focused element of a frame that loses the focus that way, so a click into the Chat composer of an unfocused window used to leave the page focused with nothing focused in it. `restoreFocusTakenByHost` (`src/webview/lib/frame-focus.ts`, installed by the Chat page's `boot()`) remembers the element that lost focus when the window's `blur` follows in the same task with the body as `activeElement`, and focuses it again when the window's `focus` returns within 750 ms with nothing focused. The operating system deactivating the window keeps `activeElement` and a later return is outside the window, so neither is touched. Native Terminal mode has its own re-activation (`activateNativeEditor` on window focus). Reproduced in an owned headless Chromium with the same two-level frame nesting (`src/webview/frame-focus-ui.test.ts`) and observed in an isolated VS Code window by replaying the workbench's call; the real operating-system activation order was not observed.

**Focus a disabled composer cannot keep.** The Chat composer's textarea is `disabled` whenever the session is not `live` (`chatWritable`), and a session leaves `live` without the user doing anything: the watchdog's reattach after a silent or dropped connection, and the rebuild after a damaged frame, both pass through `attaching` or `resyncing`. A disabled field cannot hold the focus and the browser does not return it when the field is enabled again, so the user typed into nothing. `useHeldFocus` (`src/webview/lib/use-held-focus.ts`) lets the composer *hold* its place from the moment it gains focus until focus moves for another reason than being disabled (another element takes it, or a press lands on something while the field is disabled), and focuses it again when it is writable and nothing else has focus. The page also keeps an always-on focus trace (`src/webview/lib/focus-trace.ts`, installed by `boot()`): a ring of what it saw (focus moves, window focus and blur, the field disabled or removed, the caller of every `focus()` and `blur()`), posted once as `omp:focus-trace` (rate-limited to one a second and 30 a page) when the composer loses focus without a pointer press, Tab or Escape in the preceding 400 ms. The host writes it to the OMP Desk output as `tab <id>: focus: <reason> | -<age> <event> | …`; nothing acts on it. Typing needs no focus on the composer: `installTypeToFocus` (`src/webview/lib/type-to-focus.ts`, installed by `boot()`) listens for `keydown` on the document (bubble phase, trusted events nobody has `preventDefault`ed) and, when a printable character or a paste chord (Ctrl/Meta+V, Shift+Insert) arrives at something that is not editable, focuses the composer with the caret at the end of its draft so the browser's own handling of that same key lands the text there once. Space, Enter, Tab, Escape, navigation keys, other Ctrl/Meta/Alt chords, IME composition, the Rewind picker's focused bar and a composer that is absent (an ask is open), disabled or read-only are left alone.

**Go to Session.** Ctrl+Alt+Q (`cmd+alt+q` on macOS) runs `ompDesk.goToSession` from anywhere, with no `when` clause and listed in `commandsToSkipShell` like the other workbench keys. It shows a `showQuickPick` over `SessionTreeProvider.listedSessions()`: the rows the flat Sessions list is built from, in its order (`src/views/session-order.ts`), computed for either grouping, each with its folder name (`flatFolderLabels`) and the status text the row carries (including `Unread ·`). Picking a row runs `clickSession`, so it is exactly a click on that row (a stopped row opens its history, a second pick in a short interval resumes it); the last item, New Session…, runs `ompDesk.newSession` with no argument, as the view's New Session row does. The key is `ctrl+alt+q` because Ctrl+Shift+Q already focuses the composer, no VS Code default and no IntelliJ IDEA Keybindings entry uses the chord, and it needs no `when` clause. On a layout whose AltGr is Ctrl+Alt the chord can type a character first (German AltGr+Q is `@`); rebind `ompDesk.goToSession` there.

**New Session and Resume Session keys.** Ctrl+Alt+N runs `ompDesk.newSession` and Ctrl+Alt+R runs `ompDesk.resumeWorkspaceFolder` (`cmd+alt+n` and `cmd+alt+r` on macOS), the commands behind the New Session… and Resume Session… rows of Sessions, with no argument, so the folder is asked for exactly as those rows ask it (`chooseFolder`: a pick when several folders are shown, listed by `LauncherFolders.listForPicker` so this window's folders lead in workspace order and the highlighted first row is the current project; the Add flow when none is). Like Ctrl+Alt+Q they have no `when` clause, both commands (and their `omp.` aliases, which `src/wiring/contributions.test.ts` requires to mirror the current entries) are in `commandsToSkipShell` like the other workbench keys (an end-to-end run without those entries still reached the workbench from the integrated terminal on Windows with VS Code 1.142, because xterm.js leaves a Ctrl+Alt chord alone there as a possible AltGr character; the entries keep the chord from depending on that), and a Terminal-mode pane claims both chords in `isTerminalWorkbenchChord`, so a terminal never encodes them as Escape then Ctrl+N or Ctrl+R for the program (OMP's documented defaults bind neither; Ctrl+R is history search and Alt+R is retry). The Chat page does not handle them, so its webview forwards them to the workbench. The scenario `test/e2e/scenarios/05-session-keys.spec.ts` presses both chords from a text editor, from the focused Chat composer and from the integrated terminal and sees the Resume picker and a new Chat panel each time. A keystroke is a command-origin start: `newSession` marks the operation `row` only for a folder tree item, and the folder-history Resume always begins its `resume` operation with origin `command`, so both show the status-bar progress of the pending-operation registry after its one-second grace (see the click-feedback design). Conflicts, checked on VS Code 1.142 (its bundled defaults and bundled extensions) and the extensions installed on the maintainer's machine: VS Code binds Ctrl+Alt+N only in the web build (New Untitled Text File) and Ctrl+Alt+R only on macOS and Linux (Reveal in File Explorer; Windows uses Shift+Alt+R) and in the chat and terminal find widgets on macOS; on Windows the one default is **Terminal: Run Recent Command** with a terminal focused (`workbench.action.terminal.runRecentCommand`, workbench weight), which the extension's binding (extension weight, no `when`) replaces there, as Ctrl+Shift+L replaces Select All Occurrences of Find Match; bind that command to another key to get it back. IntelliJ IDEA Keybindings binds Ctrl+Alt+R only on macOS, and no other bundled or installed extension and no user keybinding uses either chord. On a layout whose AltGr is Ctrl+Alt a chord can type a character first; rebind there.

**Terminal writes, resets and resizes stay in arrival order.** xterm.js parses what it is given later and in slices; a hidden or minimized window can sit on a large unparsed backlog. The Webview's renderer therefore routes every write, screen reset and refit through `OrderedTerminalSurface` (`src/webview/lib/terminal-ordered-surface.ts`): a reset or resize waits for every earlier byte to be parsed and later bytes wait for it, so a restored screen replaces the old one instead of being parsed after it, and bytes drawn for one grid are never parsed at another. A hidden or zero-sized container is not fitted at all. See the [native terminal design](designs/2026-10-03-native-terminal-ux.md).

**Terminal selection copy.** Native and folder-shell xterm panes copy like the VS Code integrated terminal: Ctrl+C/Ctrl+Insert with a non-empty selection and Ctrl+Shift+C write it to the clipboard from the Webview on the user's keystroke, clear the selection after a successful write and never send `^C`. Without a selection Ctrl+C is the interrupt byte in a folder shell only; a native OMP session never sends Ctrl+C (the key is consumed on every layout, matched by the physical `KeyC`, and a stray `^C` in input is dropped). Paste stays on the browser paste event. See the [native terminal design](designs/2026-10-03-native-terminal-ux.md).

**Keys follow the physical key; Copy of a selection (2026-10-21).** A page reads a shortcut's letter, digit or punctuation key only through `isPhysicalKey` (`src/webview/lib/physical-keys.ts`), which compares `event.code` (`KeyC`, `Digit1`, `Slash`) and falls back to the Latin letter or digit only for a script-built event with no `code`; named keys (`Enter`, `Escape`, `Insert`, `F3`) stay `event.key`. The physical key wins on every layout, a Latin one too (Dvorak's `KeyC` is `Ctrl+C` for the page), so there is no layout list; VS Code's own keybindings follow its keyboard mapper (the virtual key on Windows), which on a Cyrillic layout equals the physical key, and are pressed on a Russian layout in `test/e2e/scenarios/53-chat-russian-layout-keys.spec.ts`. `scripts/check-keys.mjs` (part of `npm run typecheck`) fails on `.key` compared with a character, case-converted, tested with a regular expression or looked up among characters anywhere in `src/webview/` outside that module. The cause of the Copy defect that looked like a layout one: VS Code's webview host (`execCommand` handler in `webview/browser/pre/index.html`) focuses the last element the page focused when `document.activeElement` is the `<body>`, then runs `execCommand('copy')`; a mouse selection in the transcript leaves the body active, the last element was the composer, and the copy ran on the empty composer (every layout, Ctrl+C, Ctrl+Insert, Edit > Copy and the context menu alike). `installSelectionFocusHold` (`src/webview/lib/selection-focus.ts`, installed by the Chat and the three settings pages) gives a non-collapsed selection the page's `#root` as focused element until the selection collapses, nothing is changed in the middle of a pointer press (a focus change ends a drag selection), and the root's own `focusin` is stopped at the window so VS Code keeps remembering the composer for its Paste rescue. Real-window check: `test/e2e/scenarios/52-chat-copy.spec.ts`.

**Add editor context to a session.** `OMP Desk: Add File to Session` (`ompDesk.sendFile`; Explorer incl. multi-select, editor-tab and editor context menus, Command Palette) insert a **reference** at the input of a session the user picks, never file contents and never a submit ([design](designs/2026-10-07-add-editor-context-to-session.md)). Installed OMP expands only `@path` / `@"quoted path"` mentions, resolved against the session cwd, and has no line-range syntax (`@a.ts:12-30` would name a missing file and disable the read), so the text is `@src/a.ts` (a selection's lines travel as a quote and a caret's line as `path:N` inline code, see below; **Add Selection to Session** was removed in 0.4.7, [design](designs/2026-10-18-chat-keys-and-empty-state.md)): `mentionPath` against the *target session's* cwd (absolute, forward-slashed outside it), one mention per Explorer file, one line of at most 900 characters (the native TUI turns a larger paste into a `[Paste #N]` marker and menu). A QuickPick is always shown (`src/wiring/editor-context.ts`): running sessions whose input is free, stopped/draft sessions with an open editor here (choosing one starts it through Open), then `New session in <folder>` (adds that folder to the launcher and opens a draft in the default view); most recently focused editor first and preselected, blocked/held-elsewhere/question/transitional rows and passive editors never offered. Delivery reveals the session and waits (30 s) for its input: Chat gets `omp:insert-text` once its document has sent `omp:ready`, inserted at the textarea caret through the editing command stack (end of draft when no textarea) with a held-until-mounted page queue; Terminal gets `TerminalPipeline.pasteText`, one `ESC[200~ … ESC[201~` write with no CR/LF as the visible input owner, only while the program itself has set DECSET 2004. The status bar names the destination; failure says nothing was added and offers Copy reference.

Terminal ANSI colors retain [ADR-0041](decisions/0041-allow-terminal-style-attributes-under-nonce-stylesheets.md)'s explicit attribute permission: stylesheets/scripts remain nonce/resource-gated and network permissions unchanged. VS Code supplies the 16-color palette; indexed/RGB colors come from terminal data. The earlier installed comparison found Cascadia Code NF lacks model U+EC19; the existing Codicon fallback covers it without overriding OMP's symbol preset. Canonical isolated acceptance exercised the new stack without a font-family override; evidence and limits are recorded in the [native UX design](designs/2026-10-03-native-terminal-ux.md).

**Terminal styling in native text.** One tokenizer (`src/chat/ansi.ts`) serves the model's text bounds and the page. `clampText` removes every escape sequence and control character as a whole (it used to drop only the ESC byte, which left fragments such as `[38;2;0;180;255m` on screen); `command_output` and widget lines keep well-formed SGR through `sanitizeAnsi`, and `AnsiText`/`AnsiLines` draw them as spans: the 16 palette colors are classes over `--vscode-terminal-ansi*`, 256-color and RGB values are set per span through the CSSOM. Non-SGR CSI, OSC (hyperlinks keep their text), DCS/APC/PM/SOS, two-character escapes and C1 controls are removed; a cut-off sequence ends at the byte that broke it. Work is a single bounded pass (256 KiB of input and 20 000 runs per parse; 1 MiB for tool previews). Tool previews (`TextPreview`) and bash executions use the same parser; code, diff and match views strip with the same tokenizer (`displayText`). The `/context` report parser strips with it too, so the colored report, several times longer than its plain text, still parses.

**Windows desktop notifications.** The host-owned causal ledgers and `ompDesk.desktopNotifications` setting replace the former in-VS Code turn information message. Chat actual settle/fresh outcomes and structured RPC dialogs, or native main-TUI public hooks plus later SDK settled facts, submit per-event OS toasts through constant encoded PowerShell WinRT and strict UTF-8 stdin. Exact focused/active/visible suppression consumes events without replay; bounded first-line display text excludes hidden payloads. Capability-gated additive `nativeActivity` preserves existing protocol3 controls and old runtimes. The [desktop design](designs/2026-10-03-windows-desktop-session-notifications.md) and accepted [ADR-0042](decisions/0042-submit-windows-toasts-through-powershell-winrt-and-protocol-activation.md) record installed isolated Chat/Terminal trigger/suppression proof, precise native observation limits and Stable-only AUMID pairing. The unmodified `asExternalUri` result uses `toString(true)` to preserve ordinary query routing syntax. The maintainer accepted public topmost-window/profile routing limits: protocol clicks reveal an exact indexed retained panel, otherwise select/reveal its existing Sessions row or log; they never open/attach/resume/launch or change run intent. Frozen installed proof dispatched the actual corrected toast URI explicitly to the isolated profile, observed exact retained-editor reveal and indexed-row selection without lifecycle effects, and found no tree-reveal errors. Physical/global clicks were not exercised; primary installation remains withheld by the maintainer.

Notification decisions record fixed sent/suppressed/failed categories and bounded Chat/native baseline/settle observations without transcript or capability-bearing payloads. Native control waits sixty seconds for exact-process readiness and retries absent/closed connections later; Chat keeps its fifteen-second deadline. Availability and completion-signal capability are logged explicitly. Older re-adopted children keep their recorded activity capability: no unsupported journal probes or invented completion. Deferred notification results keep the original suppression reason. Canonical isolated proof observed same-process native readiness after 23.9 seconds and real inactive-editor/window-unfocused WinRT entries; successful submission/history still does not guarantee Windows banner presentation.

Canonical isolated proof observed an actual hidden Bash turn's live settle, fixed sent decision and fresh WinRT history entry, followed by healthy editor-only close/reopen and supported **Developer: Reload Window** restoration on the same RPC/file. Parsed guest source matched the built digest; a separate read-only exact-byte cache SHA256 matched built and installed bundles. The earlier malformed served-resource state is recorded in the design as **observed-with-raw-CDP**: raw-reload causality is unproved, and no bundle cache-busting or VS Code core patch was introduced.

**Broker `managed-rpc` kind.** `PtyKind` is `"managed-rpc" | "managed-omp" | "folder-shell" | "stats-dashboard"`. `PTY_PROTOCOL_VERSION` and `PTY_RUNTIME_VERSION` were **not** bumped: the `rpc-*` frames are additive and gated by the record's kind (a `managed-rpc` broker refuses terminal-only frames and other brokers refuse `rpc-*` with `wrong-kind`), so a surviving Collab-era or folder-shell broker of the previous build stays reachable for probing, adoption and stop. `src/broker/rpc-child.ts` spawns the child with pipes (no node-pty and no screen model are loaded for this kind); the broker, not any client, negotiates rpc protocol 2 after the `ready` frame, swallows that response, and returns `ready` and the negotiated version on every attach. stdout is drained continuously into a sequence-numbered ring (`RpcLineRing`, 4 MiB / 2048 lines) whose retention is deliberately shallow: `message_update` frames are superseded per `messageId` and dropped at `message_end`, unanswered `extension_ui_request` dialogs are pinned until answered or cancelled, open `message_start`/`tool_execution_start` lines up to 64 KiB are pinned until their end frame, `response` and `rpc_chunk` frames are not retained (except a small `vsc:` response), and a `pinnedOverflow` flag is reported instead of ever evicting a dialog. Transient `response`/`rpc_chunk` lines wait for attached cursors under their own 128 MiB budget per broker (2026-10-12), so a reattaching client's own `get_entries` answer no longer evicts the replay ahead of it; eviction still walks one oldest-first queue, and a cursor that loses an unsent line reattaches. The ring only lets a (re)attaching client rebuild the in-flight turn; it is not a durability store. Lines cross the authenticated link as base64 fragments (≤ 96 KiB raw), replay is paced by the connection's send-queue bound and only the input owner may write. `RpcHandle` (`src/host/rpc-handle.ts`, on the shared `BrokerHandle`) implements the `RpcChannel` port an `RpcSession` uses. **Stop** closes the child's stdin (OMP aborts the turn, persists it, appends `session_exit` and exits); a force stop kills the retained child handle, never a numeric pid, and its whole-tree result stays `unknown` unless proven (ADR-0029). The broker never auto-stops a `managed-rpc` child: an extension-host or VS Code restart leaves it running, and only a folder shell has an owner-exit grace. A consumer that stays at the pace bound (2 MiB unflushed) for 10 s is closed by the broker with its own reason (`paced`, a launch option `--rpc-paced-close-ms` only for tests), like one whose cursor the ring evicted (`fell-behind`); the next `rpc-attached` carries the optional `lastForcedClose { reason, ageMs }` of the newest such close, broker-wide and diagnostic only ([design](designs/2026-10-24-chat-stream-stall-is-visible-and-ends-fast.md)). A timer, not the ring's eviction, ends a stuck consumer, and each consumer's pump is timed and counted.

**A slow protocol-2 negotiation is waited out, never latched to protocol 1 (2026-10-17).** A `ready` that lists protocol 2 starts the broker's negotiation, and the broker settles on protocol 1 only when the child refuses it, the stdin write fails, or `ready` does not list 2. It used to settle on 1 after 10 s without an answer, and Desk (protocol 2 only) then showed `state-unavailable` until the user killed the process: a child that spent 57 s loading a 41 MB session under a startup stall missed the 10 s. Now the 10 s (`RPC_NEGOTIATE_NOTICE_MS`) only writes one broker log line; the wait ends with the answer (`rpc-ready` is announced once, from the answer), a refusal, or the child's exit (no protocol is announced). Meanwhile `rpc-attached` carries `rpcProtocol: null`, `ready: null` and the additive `negotiating: true` (a broker from an earlier build omits it and parses as `false`; it also keeps the old 10 s latch), and client writes stay held until the negotiation settles, so none is lost or reordered. Lines the child writes while it is still answering go to the ring as usual. `RpcSession` stays in `attaching` for its ready deadline (120 s); a deadline that expires on an attach that said `negotiating` fails as `negotiation-timeout` ("OMP did not finish starting the chat protocol. Reconnect, or restart this session if it keeps failing."), a recoverable failure retried like `ready-timeout` and offered Reconnect, and the retry's attach learns the answer if it came meanwhile. `state-unavailable` is therefore only the sentence for a child that really negotiated protocol 1. The broker changes, so `out/pty-tree.json` records a new digest (`node esbuild.mjs`; the packaging gate `scripts/verify-vsix-tree-record.mjs` compares the record with the packed tree as before). A broker started by the previous build keeps its old behaviour until it is restarted.

**Autonomous Chat exits.** The managed-RPC pipe child strips terminal escape sequences across stderr chunks and retains only the last 4 KiB of UTF-8 plain text, draining both output pipes before publishing exit (under the existing finite drain deadline). Exited broker status optionally includes `stderrTail`; it is validated and survives attach, so a client that missed streamed `rpc-stderr` frames still gets the final diagnostic. The field is additive, absent on native Terminal children, and does not change launches or retry policy. `RpcHandle` suppresses autonomous-exit notification for its own requested Stop; `RpcSession` carries `{exitCode, stderr}` as an optional `exitReason` in Chat state and snapshots. The page renders it as text, not HTML, and the extension logs it to OMP Desk output. The installed OMP 18.8.5's verified "No default model selected" refusal alone offers the existing session-profile-bound **Log In to Provider** path. Other failures have no special action; user Stop/Close retains ordinary stopped presentation. Resume replaces the runtime and diagnostic in the same tab through existing launch admission.
When the installed Bun launcher includes a minified source excerpt before an uncaught startup `error:`, presentation uses the final error and stack text rather than the excerpt; the bounded original tail remains available in the diagnostic payload.

**Launch.** `rpcOmpLaunchSpec` (`src/host/rpc-launch.ts`) builds the Bun runtime flags (where the launcher carries them, below), launcher prefix, `--mode rpc-ui --cwd <dir> --profile <explicit profile> [--session <exact file>] -e <host-control module>`. `nativeOmpLaunchSpec` (`src/host/native-session.ts`) uses the same resolver/bootstrap, without `--mode`, and `--resume <exact file>` for a native resume. Both use the header cwd when enterable and the recorded profile; a fresh launch may use the recorded session directory. No Collab overlay, fork, continue or bare resume is used. The broker/root PID and creation-time tuple is recorded before adoption is reported; an allocated session path does not establish a durable file.

**Bun runtime flags by launcher kind (2026-10-14, [ADR-0075](decisions/0075-carry-bun-runtime-flags-by-launcher-kind-and-probe-before-using-an-environment-carrier.md)).** `--define <name>:<public recipient>` and `--preload <module>` are Bun's flags, not OMP's. The resolver records what the launch is (`OmpBinary.launcher`: `bun-shim` — Bun's own CLI running OMP's entry, the Bun global install — or `program` — OMP's own compiled executable: winget portable, `omp.sh` binary install, mise; absent for a hand-built value). The kind is classified from the OMP shim file; the Bun runtime a shim runs on is the first `bun.exe` beside the shim or on `PATH` whose content is Bun itself ([ADR-0083](decisions/0083-take-the-bun-runtime-of-a-bun-shim-launch-only-when-its-content-is-bun.md): it carries Bun's JavaScript engine, read in process once per file identity by `fileCarriesMarkers`, the rule that already tells OMP's program from a launcher; the extensionless `bun` is not a Windows name). A package manager's forwarding `bun.exe` makes OMP a grandchild of the broker and host control fails with `pid-mismatch` on every launch, so such a file is skipped with an Info line and the real Bun behind it is used, or, with none, the resolution is refused naming the launcher. `planControlLaunch` (`src/host/launch-capability.ts`) then decides the carrier (`src/host/runtime-flags.ts`): a `bun-shim` gets the flags on its command line ahead of the entry, unchanged, with no probe; `program` and an unknown launcher never get them on the command line (OMP's parser would exit with code 2, `unknown flags`) and use `BUN_OPTIONS` only after a harmless `omp --version` run, with the staged probe module `media/runtime-probe.mjs` and a random nonce carried exactly as the launch carries them, has printed that nonce back (on stderr or stdout); the answer is remembered per executable identity (path, size, mtime) for a negative as well, and a probe that could not run is not remembered. A launcher that does not honour `BUN_OPTIONS` runs with no Bun flag: Chat still loads the module with `-e` (Rewind, Clear, the Chat prompt and agent liveness need no control channel), the host does not connect (`runtime.control` is null), the launch notice says once per executable that host control is unavailable, and a Terminal launches without the module. The launcher records why in the control-process record (`RecordedControlProcess.withheld`, `LocalHostControl.withhold`), so a reload, the native watch and a reattach stay quiet instead of reporting a channel that was never offered as missing. A probe that could not answer (timeout, signal, or a non-zero exit, which on Windows is also how an external kill looks) is not remembered and gets its own notice ("could not check") apart from the definitive one. `BUN_OPTIONS` values escape every character that is not `[A-Za-z0-9_./:-]` with a backslash (Bun 1.4 splits on white space, reads a backslash as an escape and silently ignores a quoted `--preload` value); a value the user already had stays in front and is recorded in `OMP_VSCODE_BUN_OPTIONS_BASE`, which the first preloaded Desk module (`restoreInheritedBunOptions`; also `stats-opener.mjs` and the probe) puts back, so no process OMP starts inherits Desk's flags or needs its staged module to still exist; the restore is narrow (an earlier preload, a probe of an executable that ignores `BUN_OPTIONS`, or a stale verdict can leave Desk's options in OMP, and the host still refuses a process other than the launched one; ADR-0075 decision 4). Only the public recipient is carried (ADR-0006). Stats probes its opener preload on its own (installation marker, on every dashboard start, bun shim included) under the same carrier rule (`planStatsPreload`). Observed with OMP 18.10.3 (Bun 1.4.3 inside): its compiled program rejects both flags on the command line and honours both in `BUN_OPTIONS`; ADR-0049's earlier observation that a compiled runtime ignored an environment preload is why this is probed and never assumed.

**OMP resolution (2026-10-12 amendment, [ADR-0011](decisions/0011-use-installed-omp-without-version-gate.md)).** `resolveOmpBinary` (`src/host/native-terminal.ts`) walks PATH in order, then OMP's launcher directory as a last resort, and a candidate's failure is classified (`src/shared/omp-unavailable.ts`). A *definitive* refusal (missing file, not OMP's program, foreign launcher, probe exited non-zero) records its reason and tries the next candidate. A *transient* one (the `--version` probe did not finish within 15 s, spawning or inspecting the candidate failed for lack of resources or because the file was busy, the process was ended by a POSIX signal) stops resolution with an error that names the candidate; a busy machine never starts an older OMP than the one PATH selects, and the next launch is the retry (Windows reports an externally ended process only as an exit code, which stays definitive). The machine-scoped setting `ompDesk.executablePath` replaces the search: when set it is the only candidate and every refusal is an error, never a PATH fallback; its Settings row has a **Test** button that resolves the typed value without saving it. Every caller (launch, native terminal, CLI reads, Settings, Stats, rename, inventory) goes through this one resolver; the package source they read is the package that holds the selected launch's entry script, never a global location. The inventory's runtime resolver keeps its earlier Bun and package root only across a transient failure and drops them when the explicit path changes. When PATH offers a second distinct install, the OMP Desk output logs both paths and releases once per window.

**Install layouts the source-reading features cope with (2026-10-16 amendments, [ADR-0011](decisions/0011-use-installed-omp-without-version-gate.md)).** Several install forms exist and each resolves to a different launch. The Bun global install resolves through `omp.exe` and its `.bunx` metadata to `bun.exe` running the package's entry; the dependency packages sit beside the OMP package. The npm global install resolves through npm's `omp.cmd`, which `pathTarget` recognises (`cmdShimEntry`: it runs `bun` and names an entry inside a package whose manifest names `@oh-my-pi/pi-coding-agent`) and launches as the same `bun-shim` launch, with no shell; the dependency packages sit inside the OMP package. pnpm's and Yarn 1's `omp.cmd` have the same effect in another text (`"%~dp0\bun.exe" "%~dp0\<entry>"`); Yarn's launcher in its prefix folder and Volta's `bin\omp.cmd` (`volta run %~n0 %*`, read with Volta's `tools\user\bins\omp.json` that names the package) only forward to the package launcher they run, which is followed, never started. pnpm's package is a link into its `.pnpm` store, and its dependencies sit beside the real folder, so the layout search also tries the link's real path. mise's `shims\omp.exe` (a copy of `mise-shim.exe` that starts `mise`, which starts OMP: three processes) is never launched: when it is the mise shim (its own marker text) and exactly one release is installed under mise's `installs\github-can1357-oh-my-pi`, the program is that release's `omp.exe`, a standalone program as below. OMP's standalone program is a `program` launch with no entry and no source on disk. The staged workers for Models, Agents, OMP Settings and the inventory find `@oh-my-pi/pi-utils`, `pi-ai`, `pi-catalog` and `pi-tui` through `src/shared/omp-package-layout.ts` (the package's own `node_modules`, then the one it is installed into, only a folder whose manifest names the package), never by assuming the Bun layout. Before a new editor opens, `checkSettingsRuntime` (`src/host/settings-runtime.ts`) says whether Bun or the package source is the missing part; a refusal reaches Chat as the sentence itself and a palette command as an error, and no empty panel opens.

**The layout `omp update` can leave behind (2026-10-29, OMP 18.13.3).** OMP's self-update runs `bun add -g`, then runs the PATH `omp --version`; when Bun's launcher cannot run the release (OMP 18.13 asks for Bun 1.4.4 or newer: its `src/cli.ts` refuses an older Bun) the update installs the standalone binary over `omp.exe`, removes the `.bunx` sidecar and tells the user the install is no longer managed by Bun. Desk resolves that file as the standalone program (a `program` launch, no entry, `resolveOmpPackageRoot` null) although `bun.exe` still sits beside it and Bun's global package folder still holds the same release number, and it does not read that folder: the no-borrow rule of the 2026-10-12 and 2026-10-15 amendments of [ADR-0011](decisions/0011-use-installed-omp-without-version-gate.md) stands, because a later `omp update` of such an install replaces the program only and leaves the package behind. Chat and Terminal run through the `BUN_OPTIONS` carrier after the probe ([ADR-0075](decisions/0075-carry-bun-runtime-flags-by-launcher-kind-and-probe-before-using-an-environment-carrier.md)), the Chat broker runs on `Code.exe` ([ADR-0074](decisions/0074-run-chat-brokers-on-the-sessions-own-bun-runtime-with-code-exe-as-fallback.md)), and the source-reading features report that OMP has no source on disk. The user-facing description is in the troubleshooting guide.

**A session that went on in a newer file is the same session (2026-10-29, OMP 18.13, [design](designs/2026-10-29-session-continuations.md), [ADR-0059](decisions/0059-clear-chat-in-place-through-a-desk-registered-omp-command.md) amendment).** When a session cannot keep writing its own file (another omp process holds it, or its journal changed under it) OMP 18.13 starts the next file `<time>_<newId>.jsonl` in the same directory, with a new session id and a header `continuationOf` (and `parentSession`) naming the old id, and `--session <old>` then opens the newest file of the chain and reports its file and id. `src/host/session-chain.ts` (`proveContinuation`, `groupChains`) proves such a move from the headers alone (`readSessionHeader`, `src/host/session-files.ts`): walking back from the reported file along `continuationOf` reaches the requested file inside one directory, every id agrees with its link and its generated name, and a cycle, a missing or ambiguous file, another directory, a forged link or more than `MAX_CONTINUATION_HOPS` (64, Desk's cap) refuse. Everywhere Desk compares the file and id OMP serves with the recorded ones a proven continuation is accepted and anything else is the divergence it was: `RpcSession` (`#followContinuation`, at the first attach, every resync and every state read of a live session, waiting up to 2 s for the new file's header), the host-control handshake (`ControlClient`) and `LocalNativeWatch`. Acceptance is `SessionIndex.acceptContinuation` (through `LocalIdentity.bindContinuation`, under the tab's lifecycle): it re-proves the chain, takes the new file's claim first, commits `sessionFile`, `sessionId`, `host.sessionId` and `sessionDir` of the one row in one compare-and-swap transaction (refused when another row holds the new file) and then gives up the old claim; a failed write gives the new claim back. `mergeSessionIndexRecords` settles those fields together (`settleIdentity`), so a window that never saw the move cannot take the old file back with a later title or unread write. `SessionIndex.rowOfChain` names the row a file's conversation lives in; `trackSession` stays exact-file, and the History Resume opens that row instead of creating a second one. `scanFolderHistory` hides a member that exactly one other file continues, keeps the head of each chain, an indexed row and every branch of a diverged family (a parent two files continue), and lists one file of a cycle. A bridge document already committed for the old file and id stays write-once: the panel route is unaffected, the bridge route stands by until the editor is re-created (Reload Window, reopening the editor). The end-to-end scenario is `test/e2e/scenarios/75-session-continuation.spec.ts`.

**Same-editor mode replacement.** The profile default is read from `globalState` (`omp.session.defaultMode`, initially Chat) only when opening/creating a tab, including reopening a closed editor whose writer still runs. Ordinary Open/click on an existing tab reveals its actual mode; explicit Open in Chat/Terminal switches that tab. Restore chooses live host transport, saved slot mode, then default, independently of the current default when actual/saved mode is known. Attachment persists the actual transport mode to the durable editor binding rather than leaving a provisional default there. Stop/start actions operate on that actual transport. A stopped single click never starts either mode, including fileless drafts; rapid second click/Open starts.

The lifecycle fences Chat mutations/UI responses and terminal input before inspecting state. It captures exact E/T/root/file/id/epoch under the per-row gate, releases the gate for busy consent, then revalidates. RPC uses fresh `isSettled` plus compaction and rechecks before stdin EOF. Native uses public idle/pending/async-job facts; unknown is busy. Guarded SDK abort/shutdown is not ConPTY close: accepted shutdown is irreversible and may be deferred, so timeout keeps Stopping and never schedules a successor. Both require matched broker exit plus exact creation-time absence before replacement; Windows tree emptiness is not claimed.

Content without a durable file is refused before stopping. Only a confirmed no-turn fileless draft may reset its session identity while preserving row/ordinal/editor/claim; missing durable rows never become empty. A successful replacement keeps the claim and run intent through a durably recorded successor mode/attempt; failure records stopped and releases. Bounded Chat draft text survives on the host until another Chat document is ready, never enters Terminal or saved editor state, and is discarded on dispose/conversation change; attachment loss is disclosed.

**Native autonomous identity.** `/resume`, `/new`, branch/tree and rollback remain unmodified and best effort. Authenticated current file/id observations may move the same E/root from A to B: claim B first, atomically publish row/binding/broker mapping, then release A. A stays stopped; a verified rival demotes only the newcomer. No idle/early event is called a settled identity witness. Before owned known-file launch, fresh own-native readback avoids a positively observed writer hidden by a stale row; failed readback remains best effort. Terminal→Chat continues the best-known file after exact-root exit, accepting speculative/missed native identity. `/restart` can lose bootstrap/control; explain native `/exit` or explicitly confirmed force Stop, tree unknown, no automatic successor, and no successor over an observed survivor.

**`RpcSession` and history** (`src/host/rpc/session.ts`, `history-reader.ts`, `frames.ts`, `protocol.ts`). The session is a state machine `starting → attaching → resyncing → live` (and `stopped`, `failed(code)`) over an `RpcChannel`. Every attach first paints JSONL history through the shared density-independent 100-canonical-card admission rule; each visible non-tool and individually owned call costs one, hidden/internal entries and already-owned results cost zero. Older cards are read by byte range. Replay folds into a shadow model, identity is proved with `get_state`, and `get_entries {since: <disk cursor>}` supplies the durable delta before authoritative publication. Ended live rows retain aliases and unmatched/unsaved notices through durable reconciliation. Mutations retain exactly-once `vsc:` ids and lost-prompt reporting. Identity-changing slash builtins remain refused, including the installed lifecycle operations `wt` and `worktree` and their advertised aliases. One `get_available_commands` query follows verified live attach; a pushed `available_commands_update` advancing the catalogue revision supersedes an older query response. There are no keystroke queries or new discovery processes for stopped/passive history. Protocol mismatch retains disk history and fixed refusal codes. `readViewOnlySnapshot` never claims, launches or appends history.
History reads resolve OMP's externalized images (`blob:sha256:<hash>` references in the JSONL, bytes in `<agent dir>/blobs/`) through `src/host/rpc/history-blobs.ts` before the shared parser runs; an unresolved reference renders an explicit “Image not available in saved history” placeholder instead of a broken image.

**Narration and the active leaf of a saved session (OMP 18.12, adopted 2026-10-23).** Two OMP 18.12 session facts are read in one place each. (1) A `thinking` block with `narration: true` is prose the model addressed to the user between tool calls, which the provider returned as a signed thinking block; OMP's own surfaces show it as answer text (`presentNarrationAsAnswer`). `parseChatContent` (`src/chat/messages.ts`), the single parse of every live frame, history row and page message, turns such a block into a `text` block, so the answer card, copy, Find, the stop-withdraw rule and the thinking clock all treat it as reply text; OMP sets the flag when the block ends, so a streaming block changes from reasoning to text at that update, as in the terminal UI. Desk never sends a message back to OMP, so the dropped signature is not needed. (2) An entry written off the active branch (late usage, a background result routed to the branch it started on) carries `activeLeafId`, the leaf that was active; OMP's loader replays that leaf instead of treating the entry as the leaf (`resolveSessionLeafId`). `HistoryReader` records the key of each indexed line (written last, so the tail of the line is enough), and its default leaf (a view-only open, a resync without a live leaf) follows the same rule, including the fallback to the newest self-selecting entry when the captured leaf is gone and the empty path for a cleared leaf; the `get_entries` cursor stays the last line of the file. A live session still passes the process's own leaf.

**Streaming cost (2026-10-12).** OMP sends one `message_update` per token chunk and each carries the whole partial message. `RpcSession` passes on at most one per message every `MESSAGE_UPDATE_PACE_MS` (33 ms): the latest frame per message is kept, a pending frame is delivered before any other line so order is kept, the first frame after a pause goes out on the next timer turn, and a stop flushes what was pending so a partial reply is shown as far as it got. On the page, `Markdown` keeps the previous parse (`parseMarkdown(text, previous)`, `src/webview/components/Markdown.tsx`): when the text only appends to the previous text, every block that ends before the previous last line keeps its element and only the tail from the last block (or the one before it, which a new line can join) is parsed again; a 20 KB reply rendered at 400 growth steps went from 562 ms of parsing to 16 ms. While the user types in the composer (`src/webview/lib/typing-activity.ts`: a draft edit or an IME step, until 500 ms pass without one) the page yields to the keystrokes: `ChatClient` notifies its subscribers of `message_update`, `tool_execution_update` and `tool_stream_update` frames at most every `LIVE_PACE_WHILE_TYPING_MS` (150 ms) while the model still takes every frame at once, any other change notifies at once and carries what was held, and `PacedMarkdown` shows the text that has arrived instead of revealing it frame by frame, because each reveal frame parses and renders the whole text again. A keystroke itself touches nothing in the transcript (`chat-ui.test.ts` guards it).

**A reply streaming into a long transcript costs what it costs in a short one (2026-10-25).** The page's `Transcript` projects the loaded entries into cards in two steps, `positionTranscript` (display order) and `projectTranscript` (the fold into cards), and both walk every loaded entry. A streaming reply is a new message object on every frame, so projecting everything per frame made a frame cost as much as the history is long, and a snapshot made every row a new object (the measured heights of `MeasuredRows` and the tool metadata are keyed on the row's objects), so one auto-compaction on a long session redrew everything and the frames behind it queued into a run of long tasks. The rules now: (1) `src/chat/live-tail.ts` projects the transcript without the live reply once (`projectStable`, memoized on the entries, pending rows, notices, active tools and options) and, per frame, only the reply (`appendLive`); `appendLive` answers `null`, and the whole is projected (`projectWhole`), when the reply could change a card before it: it is not attached to the last row, something is anchored to it, a tool is running (its card follows the reply's), the reply calls `wait` or `todo` (which retire an earlier card) or calls a tool that already has a card; `live-tail.test.ts` compares the two projections over random sessions. A reply that is still streaming has no footer, so the turn totals and served-model checks are read from the stable entries. (2) `applyChatSnapshot` keeps the page's own object for every row whose content is equal (`src/chat/entry-sharing.ts`), so row identity, and with it the measured heights and the memoized `toolPresentation` (remembered per call block), survives a snapshot. (3) `ChatClient` measures what drawing a live frame costs (the time from the notification to the microtask queued after the subscribers, in which React has drawn) and does not notify again for a live frame before `(1 + LIVE_LOAD_FACTOR)` times that cost has passed, at most `LIVE_PACE_MAX_MS`; typing keeps its own 150 ms pace, the larger of the two wins, and every other change still notifies at once and carries the held frames. (4) Hot render paths format counts and clock times with formatters built once (`groupedCount`, `clockTime` in `src/shared/format.ts`); `toLocaleString` builds a new `Intl` formatter per call. `src/webview/compaction-ui.test.ts` streams a reply into 4,500 loaded entries on a CPU slowed four times, with and without a resync or a compaction that drops 480 entries, and bounds the long tasks.

**Host tool channel (2026-10-11, [design](designs/2026-10-10-agent-debugger-tools.md) §1, [ADR-0055](decisions/0055-expose-vscode-capabilities-as-rpc-host-tools-run-by-the-owning-window.md)).** Desk can register VS Code capabilities with OMP as RPC host tools (`set_host_tools`) and run them in the window that holds the session's broker input. The broker advertises `hostToolRetention` in `rpc-attached`: its ring pins a `host_tool_call` line until the matching `host_tool_result` is written to OMP or a `host_tool_cancel` for it is seen, and reports `hostCallOverflow` when pinned calls pass soft limits (32 lines, 1 MiB) without evicting them. A broker without the capability gets no tools and one Chat notice that the session must be restarted. `RpcChannel` exposes live `inputOwned` with an `input-owner` change event. `src/host/tools/host-tool-calls.ts` is the per-`RpcSession` call ledger: calls execute only while the session has verified identity and holds input (re-checked before each side effect), a call not seen live by this session (an earlier attachment, or a reconnect replay) is answered with an error and never executed, an OMP cancel aborts the tool and writes nothing, a local cancellation or lost ownership still writes exactly one error result, and a written result is retried under its id until the broker accepts it. `src/host/tools/host-tool-registration.ts` serializes and coalesces registration after identity verification and again when the provider's tool set changes. `src/host/tools/host-tool.ts` is the contract tools implement; the debugger tools live under `src/host/debug/`.

**Leaf moves and branches (2026-10-09, [design](designs/2026-10-09-chat-rewind.md), [ADR-0051](decisions/0051-rewind-chat-in-place-through-a-desk-registered-omp-command.md)).** The active branch can move without an append from this host: OMP's own turn recovery, or a Rewind. `#reconcileEntries` therefore rebuilds the window at the live leaf when a `get_entries {since}` delta is empty but reports another `leafId`, or does not extend the known leaf (the parent of its branch root is not the model's leaf). `#rebuild(live)` refreshes the `HistoryReader` and takes `snapshot(rows, leafId)`, the tail window of the path to that leaf, only when the disk holds exactly what the process just reported (its last complete entry is the live `lastId` and the live leaf is indexed), because OMP's write queue can trail the process. Otherwise it looks once more after 150 ms (`REBUILD_RETRY_MS`), then falls back to a full `get_entries`, which `#applyFull` trims to the same tail window (`transcriptWindow`) with an older-row count. The cursor becomes the live `lastId`. `HistoryReader.loadOlder(beforeId)` walks the ancestors of `beforeId`, so paging up follows the active branch even when its leaf is not the file's last line. The model's `branches` (`computeBranchPoints` over the reader's index or the full read, with first-prompt previews from the reader) list only divergences the user made: an off-path subtree whose first message, past metadata and markers, is a user prompt. OMP's retry and checkpoint pruning leaves subtrees rooted at an assistant or tool-result message, which are not listed. A branch's switch target is its newest user, assistant or tool-result message. A point is anchored at the nearest message at or before the divergence on the path, because a rewind usually lands below OMP's `model_usage` bookkeeping, which Desk does not load; points sharing an anchor merge. A reattach replays the broker's ring, which can hold turns of a branch the user rewound away from; on the first unmatched reconcile pass of a settled session with branches, one full `get_entries` drops the pending rows saved off the active path (`dropPendingSavedElsewhere`) instead of flagging them *not yet saved*.

**In-place navigation** (`RpcSession.navigate`, `src/omp/navigate-command.ts`, `src/chat/rewind.ts`; ADR-0051). The `-e` host-control module calls `registerNavigateCommand`, which registers the extension command `omp-desk-navigate` at the first `session_start` whose `ctx.mode` is `rpc` (the native TUI loads the same module and lists nothing). It needs only that module loaded with a non-absent bootstrap, not the host-control pipe. `RpcSession.navigate` runs once per 32-hex `requestId`. `#navigationRefusal` first refuses on host-known state: a fence or turn, compaction, a dialog, queued input, an unsettled or async-paused session, an unconfirmed prompt or unreconciled pending row, a catalogue without the command at `source: "extension"`, a leaf other than `expectedLeafId` (past which only OMP's `model_usage` bookkeeping may follow, `leafReaches`), or a target that is not in `rewindTargets`, a durable user message or a skill invocation the user typed (rewind), the Undo offer's `from` (`undoOffer`) or a listed branch tip (switch). The request then goes out as `/omp-desk-navigate <json>` (`encodeNavigateCommand`, a compare-and-swap of session id, expected leaf and target) through an ordinary `prompt` without `streamingBehavior`. Inside OMP, `runNavigateCommand` re-checks session, leaf (with the same `leafReaches` rule) and target, calls `navigateTree`, and appends one `custom` entry `omp-desk/navigation` `{v, requestId, kind, from, target, to, summarized, raced?}` whenever the leaf moved, taking `from` and `to` from OMP's `session_tree` event and flagging `raced` when they differ from what was checked or no event was observed. Refusals throw `omp-desk-navigate:<code>`, which OMP reports as an `extension_error` with `extensionPath` `command:omp-desk-navigate`. `#onFrame` keeps that command's `prompt_result` and coded errors out of the transcript; `agentInvoked: true` in its acknowledgement or result, or the command text echoed as a user message, means it reached the model, which is aborted. `#runReconcile` defers while the command runs; afterwards one re-sync runs through the same serializer, and the marker with this `requestId` on the durable active path (the leaf or an ancestor) is the only proof of success. The outcome carries `summarized`, `raced` and, for a rewind, the target prompt as a draft taken from the entry the host holds, with `blob:` images resolved. A command whose answer never came (30 s, 180 s when summarizing) or was lost leaves `#navigationFence`: every mutation is refused as busy until its late `prompt_result`, its marker on the path or the end of this `RpcSession` (process exit, or a reattach after a window reload), and the page is told `unconfirmed`. `ChatRuntime.navigate` answers `omp:chat-navigate-result` to the asking page, or, for **OMP Desk: Rewind Conversation…** (`ompDesk.rewindConversation`, no origin), to every writable route, refusing `not-owner` when there is none. Read-only, view-only and not-live routes are refused before the session is asked, and a draft too large for its route is resent without images, counted as unavailable. `/rewind` and `/branch` typed alone open the page's Rewind; with arguments they are refused with `REWIND_ARGUMENTS_SENTENCE`. Files are never restored; the page and the QuickPick name the files changed after the target, including the paths of OMP's hashline `edit` (`toolPresentation` reads its `[PATH#TAG]` headers and the result's `path`).

**In-place clear and Desk session commands** (`RpcSession.clear`, `src/omp/clear-command.ts`, `src/chat/clear.ts`, `src/chat/desk-commands.ts`; proposed ADR-0059, round 4 review pending). One shared mutation fence covers boundary + public `ctx.reload()`, supported RPC `prompt("/fresh")`, provider-id readback and durable acceptance, then the confirmation-phase command's durable `omp-desk/clear` marker. The only private OMP dependency is `SessionManager.appendResetBoundary`. Missing work observations refuse; host and handler check async delivery and registry-only liveness as well as running jobs. Native fresh's no-data success and true/aborted/settled admission result qualify only in fresh phase with same-file rotated-id readback and durable acceptance. A latest-committed-row catalog transaction checks previous provider id, file, host/RPC generation and ownership. Tentative identity is confined to its desired snapshot, published only after commit; queued ordinary snapshots are captured at their serialized turn, preserving concurrent title/unread/other-tab edits without leaking a refused identity. A detected competing committed runtime remains authoritative in the shared row. Durable row/header identity serves liveness, bridge/navigation and stopped-file operations; provider identity serves host readback/reattach. Restart validates row/header/bridge against durable identity and host/live RPC against captured provider identity throughout. Ordinary divergence still fails. Detach results never prove prepare/confirm completion; all phases retain late-response correlation and abort model fall-through. Unknown completion remains fenced. Only host-known incomplete Desk boundaries show the retry warning; unknown/native boundaries remain neutral and visible. No automatic retry occurs. ADR-0059 lists retained checkpoint/tool, plan, capability, memory, TODO, advisor, goal/side-thread, async and foreground-output differences. Settled readback does not prove foreground bash/eval idleness. Host exit after rotation but before durable acceptance fails reattach closed: recover by explicitly stopping the exact child and reopening the saved conversation, not Chat Restart (which requires unanswered native state). Main must confirm that policy before acceptance. `/fresh` preserves local messages; `/new` fences before replacement and rechecks observed settled/exact identity before stopping, retaining the old editor on partial outcomes; `/delete` creates a successor only after confirmed deletion.

**Named refusals and a deferred `/clear` (2026-10-13 amendment to ADR-0059).** `RpcSession.#clearBlock` names the first thing in the way: `busy` (a response streams, or compaction/another Desk operation runs: OMP's own sentence), `dialog`, `queued` (a message waits in OMP's queue), `jobs`, `unsettled` (OMP is finishing the interrupted turn), `unconfirmed-send` (a sent message is not reconciled yet). When the only reasons are the transient `queued`, `unsettled` and `unconfirmed-send`, the chat runtime passes `RpcSession.clear` a callback and the clear is deferred instead of refused: nothing is written to OMP, the page gets one notice, `model.clearPending` (reason, carried in snapshots and the `clear_pending` host frame, shown as a footer row) marks the wait, and `handleMessage` answers at once so the composer is not held. At most one clear waits per session (a second says so). Every model change re-checks the block; when it clears, the deferred clear calls the same `#clear` as an immediate one, so ADR-0059's fence, phases and confirmation are unchanged. It ends, without writing, when the user sends a message, starts another command (`/new`, `/delete`, `/fresh`, a rewind, compaction, a context report), the session stops or changes identity, or after 60 s, which shows the then-current precise refusal; an abort, a queue removal or a streaming turn that begins while it waits does not end it. A streaming response, an open dialog, jobs and the other refusals are never deferred.

**Fork, Branch and Clear from the Sessions view (2026-10-18, [design](designs/2026-10-18-fork-branch-clear-session-actions.md), accepted after independent review).** A fork never touches the original's process: `deskSessionCommand` answers typed `/fork` (no arguments) and **Fork Session** runs the same `liveChatTarget` path in `extension.ts`. `RpcSession.forkSource()` checks the live conversation (idle, settled, nothing queued, identity verified, a session file with saved messages), `awaitForkDisk` (`src/host/fork-source.ts`) waits up to 2 s until `HistoryReader` reads the file at the live leaf, and the window then creates a new draft row whose first launch carries `forkFrom` (`PendingForks`, `src/host/pending-forks.ts`, registered per tab; every launch of the fileless draft passes it, so a fork process that dies early is retried, and a launch of a materialized or native row forgets it). `rpcOmpLaunchSpec` adds `--fork <file>` only with `forkFrom`, never together with a session file: OMP writes the copy and its `parentSession` link, as it does for its own pane/window fork, and restores model and thinking from the copied entries (ADR-0038 §1 narrowly amended). The RPC `fork` command is not used, because it moves the asking process onto the new file. **Branch…** posts the panel action `open-rewind` once the page announced ready, and the composer's handler calls the function typed `/branch` calls. **Clear Context…** shows a modal and then `ChatRuntime.clearConversation`, which shares `#startClear` (refusals and deferral) with typed `/clear`.

**`/review` and commands that ask (2026-10-18, [design](designs/2026-10-18-chat-review-command.md)).** OMP's bundled `/review` is a `custom` command that runs inside the `prompt` command and asks through `extension_ui_request` (`select`, `input`, `editor`) with `setStatus` and `notify`, all rendered by the existing composer dialog; OMP acknowledges the `prompt` only when the command returns. A pending command's deadline (`RpcSession.#send`, 30 s) and the page's send-result deadline (`ChatClient.#sendText`, 45 s) therefore do not run while `model.uiRequest` is set and restart when a dialog opens (`#onUiRequest`) or settles (`#dropDialog`, `ChatClient.#commit`). `deskListed` (`slash-completion.ts`) gives the bundled entry a Desk description and hint.

Installed-OMP isolated no-model RPC evidence established that prepare/confirm return early no-data admission acknowledgements and finish later with correlated `agentInvoked:false/status:completed` results; Desk waits for those handler completions before advancing. Two repeated native sequences retained the exact file/durable id and prior journal history, rotated provider ids and wrote final markers. This is not installed-window or real-provider teardown evidence.

**Lost-connection recovery and package drift** (`src/host/rpc/session.ts`, `src/host/package-drift.ts`, `src/wire/pty-ipc.ts`). A session never stays frozen on a connection that stopped delivering. A watchdog armed while `live` probes a silent connection with `get_state` (after 45 s of silence while a turn shows as working, 90 s while idle); a probe that times out replaces the connection through the ordinary reattach, and a probe that answers “idle” while the page shows working clears the stale working state (`recovery` output, reason `probe-timeout` / `stale-working`). A frame that cannot be folded, or a frame listener that throws in `pty-ipc` (which now closes the connection instead of abandoning the buffered lines behind it), schedules an authoritative resync/reattach (`frame-failure`). Entering `failed` clears working, async-paused and active-tool state, and a *recoverable* failure (`attach-failed`, `state-failed`, `ready-timeout`, `protocol-error`, `write-refused`; `isRecoverableRpcFailure`) is retried automatically with growing delays (3 s, 10 s, 30 s, then 60 s); the failed banner offers **Reconnect** (`omp:chat-reconnect` → `RpcSession.reconnect()`), which reattaches to the same process and proves identity again, and is refused for identity divergence or an exited process. Drafts live in the page and are untouched by any of this. Beside it a 1 s stall monitor (same file) looks at a command that has waited 5 s and a working session that has sent nothing for 5 s: it logs what the link delivered, asks the broker for one `status` round trip (separating a stalled link from an OMP that is busy), shows `linkStalled` to the page only when the broker does not answer (or has not within 2 s) and reads the session file for a prompt whose answer did not come; an unanswered probe never replaces a connection (the broker ends a consumer that stays at its pacing bound for 10 s, the watchdog ends every other silent link; design Risks).

**A reattach always ends, and a gap in the ring is read from the file (2026-10-29, [ADR-0038](decisions/0038-host-chat-over-rpc-ui-on-a-broker-pipe-child.md) amendment, [design](designs/2026-10-24-chat-stream-stall-is-visible-and-ends-fast.md)).** Every wait of the path from `RpcSession.#reattach` to the socket has a bound, because an unbounded one left a tab on "Connecting to the session…" for 44 minutes with nothing in the log. `connectPtyBroker` (`src/wire/pty-ipc.ts`) races the broker's `hello-ok` against its handshake failure (the 10 s deadline, a socket error or close), destroys the socket of a failed dial, and gives a deadline that fired 250 ms or more late (this process had not run, so the broker was not silent: a blocked event loop runs its due timers before it reads a socket) one fresh bound. The dial of `RpcHandle.reconnect()` (one shared flight) is bounded to 30 s, a connection that arrives after that is closed, never adopted, and the status round trip that follows the dial keeps the handle's 15 s request bound. `RpcSession` cuts one attach request at 45 s (`attachTimeoutMs`; the rest of an attempt keeps its own deadlines, such as the command timeout of its resync) and starts no new attempt of a reattach once 120 s have passed (`reattachBudgetMs`, checked when an attempt ends: it bounds the re-attempts, not one attempt, which since the 2026-10-30 amendment below may last up to `starvedReadMs`, 5 min, while its state read waits patiently for a heavy stream); an attempt that fails inside the bounds logs `reattach attempt N failed after X ms (<cause>); trying again in D ms` and retries with the attempt list's delays, and the end of the budget (or of the list) is the ordinary `failed` state with the automatic retries and **Reconnect**. The cause of a failure is in the log only; no page receives it. When a reattach finds that the ring no longer holds the lines after the window's cursor (`truncated`), `#reconcileAfterAttach` re-reads the transcript from the session file (`#rebuildFromDisk`) and asks OMP only for what the file trails by, instead of one full `get_entries` queued through the consumer the broker just closed as too slow; the log says `the broker's lines in between are gone: the transcript was re-read from the session file (last entry …)`. A reattach whose ring still holds the cursor's lines is unchanged: `get_entries { since }`. The event loop monitor (`src/wire/loop-lag.ts`) attributes per-frame work it cannot mark one by one: a stall line names `metered Desk work in that time` with, per name (`broker socket read`, `chat reply flush`, `chat output delivery`, `chat publish`, `chat history apply`, `chat transcript rebuild`), how many times it ran, its total and its longest run; the totals are inclusive, so a read contains the delivery it caused and a publish the delivery of its snapshot.

**A reattach under a heavy stream is shown before OMP confirms it (2026-10-30, [ADR-0038](decisions/0038-host-chat-over-rpc-ui-on-a-broker-pipe-child.md) amendment, [design](designs/2026-10-24-chat-stream-stall-is-visible-and-ends-fast.md)).** OMP writes the answer to a command in the order of its output, so while it streams hard (7–20 MB a minute made one Chat tab fail every reattach for about forty minutes) the answer to the attach's `get_state` queues behind the flood and comes minutes after the 30 s command deadline, while lines keep arriving on a connection that works. The old rule read that as a lost child (`state-failed`), the automatic retry queued a new `get_state` behind the same backlog, and the tab never recovered. For a **reattach** only, `RpcSession.#send` gives the reads of the resync (`get_state`, the subagent reads, the ask-dialog opt-in) a *patient* deadline: it runs from the newest line that arrived, so a link that delivers keeps the read alive, and it ends at `starvedReadMs` (5 min) whatever arrives; a link that goes quiet still fails the read at the command deadline, as before. A first attach and the heal resync of a frame that could not be folded (`#scheduleResync`) keep the plain deadline, and so does every other command. A reattach whose state answer is still out after `resyncLiveAfterMs` (2 s) with lines still arriving does not wait on "Connecting": a gap in the broker's ring is read from the session file first (local I/O, never OMP), then the tab is published `live` with the read-only reason `STATE_UNCONFIRMED_REASON` ("Waiting for OMP to confirm this session; sending is paused until it does."). The session proves nothing yet: `#identityVerified` is reset by every reattach, so `#controlRefusal` answers `not-live` to every send, Stop, navigation and tool read, `respondUi` writes no dialog answer, the host-tool gate (`HostToolCalls.open`) and the registrations stay closed, and the watchdog is not armed. When the answer comes, the ordinary tail of the resync runs unchanged (continuation, identity check, registrations, agents, the delta; the ring-gap read already done is not repeated) and lifts the reason; an answer that names another session still ends the tab as `identity-diverged`. A connection that closes while the tab is shown this way ends the wait at once as an attach failure (retried on the reattach schedule), not as `state-failed`. A reattach whose link is quiet behaves as before. The `chat connection restored` log line waits for the state that lifts `STATE_UNCONFIRMED_REASON`.

**Unanswered native dispatcher recovery.** `RpcSession.nativeStateUnanswered` remembers an unconfirmed state read for one native session; attach readiness and transcript events do not clear it, while a newer authoritative state answer does. A state-failed Chat notice offers Reconnect and explicit Restart, retaining the latter through same-native resynchronization. `omp:chat-restart` carries the native epoch and follows `ChatRuntime`'s lifecycle-event route. The host requires the controlling guest document, committed binding, positively owned runtime generation and exact saved header before and after consent. It uses authenticated force stop and matched broker/root-creation exit evidence without another native state or abort prerequisite, then reuses `SessionIndex.replaceHost` under the same claim and per-tab gate. No positive exit proof means no successor.
