Consent discloses uncertain admission, unsaved effects, image loss and independently surviving tools that can still modify files; ordinary Stop, Close and native Terminal behavior are unchanged ([ADR-0050](decisions/0050-retain-uncertain-input-and-confirm-exact-file-restart.md)).

**Restart after an identity failure (2026-10-13 amendment to [ADR-0050](decisions/0050-retain-uncertain-input-and-confirm-exact-file-restart.md)).** `RpcSession.restartOffered` adds a failed `identity-mismatch` or `identity-diverged` session to the unanswered-state case; `chatBanner` gives those codes a `restart` action (no Reconnect, which stays refused for both). `ChatRuntime` and `restartChat` gate on `restartOffered` with the checks above. A session that failed before it went live in this host has no committed bridge document, so `restartBinding` derives the same binding fields from the row and the runtime instead, before and after consent; a committed binding is used whenever one exists. The successor resumes the row's own file. Consent adds that the exact recorded process now reports another conversation or identity. `#identityMismatch` logs, per tab, the file and id OMP reported and the ones the tab expected.

`code --install-extension --force` of a same-version build replaces the installed folder in place under a running window. The host (loaded `out/extension.js`) keeps running its old code, but every *new* webview document, child entry or helper read loads the new files. `PackageDrift` (`src/host/package-drift.ts`) fingerprints the shipped entry bundles at activation and every 10 s; on a change it logs once, shows a warning with **Reload Window**, withholds a freshly built guest document from the old host (an already-loaded guest keeps working; mode switches show an “updated on disk” notice instead) and refuses to stage new child entries. Reload the window right after any in-place install.

**A drifted window starts nothing (2026-10-19).** The staging check above (`RuntimeEntries.refusal`) runs only while the window has not yet staged its entries, and the staged copies are cached for the window's life, so a window that staged before the reinstall still launched: its broker and child came from the replaced bundle, the page said “updated on disk”, and a reload then landed in the middle of the launch. `LocalLauncher.launch` (`src/runtime/local-launcher.ts`, `launchRefusal` wired to `packageChangedOnDisk` in `extension.ts`) now asks the same drift check as its first step on every launch and answers `not-started` with the fixed Reload sentence before the precheck, the staging or the broker slot is touched (the only thing it does first is discard the run's launch timeline, so no measurement of a launch that never ran is kept). An attach of a host that already runs starts nothing and is never refused by it.

**Host-owned conversations** (`src/host/chat-runtime.ts`). A conversation is `live` (an `RpcSession`), `view-only` (a stopped session's history, composer disabled, one explicit Open) or `legacy` (a row an earlier Collab build's host owns: a banner-only page with no composer and no transport). Pages are a separate axis: a page is one route (an editor's `postMessage` channel or its authenticated bridge) that receives the conversation's `omp:chat-*` messages, comes and goes without touching the conversation, and every replacement (view-only to live on Open, relaunch after Reload) uses a fresh epoch nonce so a surviving page accepts the new snapshot. A snapshot is chunked to the route's size ceiling; a route that refuses a size gets an authoritative re-send. The runtime reports model changes (turn activity, notifications), phase changes, identity and `open_url` to the extension; `open_url` becomes an extension-owned confirm naming the URL and is opened with `vscode.env.openExternal` only after an explicit click.
The bridge wire acknowledgement is available before the document's accepted-readiness callback attaches and resends the chat route; rejected acknowledgements restore the previous wire gate. A bridge acknowledgement is connection-scoped: losing that socket clears the document's bridge acknowledgement, so replacement authentication alone cannot publish readiness or lose the resend against an unacknowledged wire. The new connection's acknowledgement triggers the authoritative snapshot again, even after an interrupted first live snapshot; the document generation, sequence ledger and any panel acknowledgement survive. Headless coverage follows the actual Close, stopped-row Open, page Open and first host restart through this interruption. Installed-window verification remains separate. See the [surviving-page ordering invariant](designs/2026-09-29-rpc-ui-chat-and-sessions.md#3-webview-contract).

Large authoritative snapshots use ordered bridge backpressure, not a synchronous burst of encrypted-frame promises. The writer produces one sealed frame at a time and releases its plaintext byte reservation only after the socket write callback, under the unchanged 256 KiB frame and 1 MiB outbound queue ceilings. A stalled write closes after five seconds, independent of inbound heartbeats. The earliest admitted train finishes to preserve disk-first paint; only later wholly unsent trains are superseded, at their actual positions among live events. Every train ends with its epoch-bound display preferences. Large rows/DTOs use authenticated, sequenced fragment payloads; at most one bounded DTO is encoded/reassembled, with 128 MiB total, 128 KiB decoded parts and a 15-second absolute reassembly deadline. The guest discards partial data on refusal, route change or disconnect and exposes only complete DTOs. The panel path remains preferred where acknowledged, but an orphan has no guaranteed panel handle. Three early authenticated recovery failures (or repeated deterministic fragment refusals/timeouts) stop retries and explain Reload Window or copying the draft before reopening; neither OMP nor input is replayed. The [bounded snapshot design](designs/2026-10-09-bounded-bridge-snapshot-delivery.md) and ADR-0023 amendment were independently accepted; installed verification is recorded separately.
Before a bridge Chat page attaches or sends any snapshot, a sealed read-only `guest-version` announcement must prove guest protocol version 9 with fragment support on this exact authenticated connection and acknowledged route. Reoffer, disconnect or document replacement clears that proof. A fixed five-second post-ACK deadline cannot be extended by duplicate ACKs; missing, malformed, wrong-version or late proof gets one small existing unfragmented blocked chat-state with Reload Window guidance, no ChatRuntime attachment, no native call and no reconnect loop. The existing v8 page retains its transcript and composer-local draft.

Panel route ACKs are repeatable but quiescent: only the first valid current-generation ACK publishes the ready-status offer. Later ACKs retain route authority without another identical offer, preventing the panel offer/ACK feedback loop. Lost-ACK re-delivery and fresh-generation writer fences remain intact; this does not change connection-scoped bridge acknowledgement.

**Chat page** (`src/webview/main.tsx`, `chat-messages.ts`, `lib/chat-client.ts`, `App.tsx` and components). Epoch-bound shared snapshots feed chronological canonical cards, then density and measured visible-window projection. [Paseo-style Chat](designs/2026-10-04-paseo-style-chat.md) replaces read-only groups and collapsed body previews with flat, lazy individual details and Overview groups of two or more tool calls. `projectToolOverview` emits a group only for a run of at least two calls; a lone call is the ordinary flat row (it keeps its reserved run key, so growing to two calls becomes a group under the same key with anchors, scroll and focus preserved, and shrinking restores the plain row). A settled successful Bash/Read call whose expanded details would be empty (`(no output)`, no error, diagnostics or artifacts) renders as one static, non-expandable line with its muted intent and “no output”. Shared `tool-presentation.ts` owns route/category/error/live-edit semantics; only ask and approval cards are never grouped, and every other settled call (task, wait, IRC writes, unknown tools) joins the adjacent Overview group and is counted as agents, jobs, messages or tool calls in its summary, while a running or queued task or wait keeps its own live row until it settles. A Wait is one header line in both densities (job name, outcome and clock, or a count and a truncated name list; the full list is in the tooltip and the expanded body). An actual live file edit/write stays expanded until terminal outcome, then returns to its run unless native focus/selection holds the detail. Late diagnostics stay attached to their file tool; unmatched diagnostics and native notices remain boundaries. Advisor notes validate severity/nonempty content, keep blockers discoverable and fall back to actual message content. Only proven visible user-attributed skills become user rows; exact prompts remain exact, legacy labels are explicitly reconstructed and expanded content/images stay disclosed. Goal/retry/fallback rows preserve native chronology; hidden data is excluded before counting, grouping, copying or announcements.

**Wait row and running badge (2026-10-08).** `wait()` in `tool-renderers.tsx` decides from the data whether expanding adds anything the one-line face and tooltip lack: several entries (the header ellipsizes names), a job's result, error, extra label lines or output notices, cancelled rows, an IRC message body, or the text of a rowless result. When it adds nothing (a lone job with a plain label, a lone agent, an empty progress), `ToolCard` renders a plain non-interactive row (`omp-tool-head--static`: no chevron, no button, nothing focusable) whose tooltip still lists every job's name, type, status, clock, exit code and first label line. A failed wait, diagnostics or attachments always stay expandable. The live clock is the longest awaited job's duration, ticking locally, in the right-aligned `omp-tool-elapsed` slot and kept after settling. No tool row shows a `running` badge next to its spinner any more; `error` and `skipped` badges stay (a failed call that is still running keeps its error badge).

**MCP, Submit Result and autoresearch cards (2026-10-19).** Three families have a renderer whose one-line face is built while the card is closed, as for Wait and Debug (`rendererFace` in `tool-renderers.tsx`; the card then shows the view's title, target and facts in the head and suppresses the generic gist for `yield` and the autoresearch tools). An MCP call is identified by its minted name alone, `mcp__<server>_<tool>` (OMP's `mintMCPToolName`; no native tool starts with `mcp__`, and `details.serverName`/`mcpToolName` alone never make a tool MCP); the title is `details.serverName/mcpToolName` once there is a result and the name split at its first underscore before that. The row's target is OMP's inline argument summary (`src/chat/mcp-tool.ts`: 24 characters per value, 160 in all, the intent field dropped; a null argument is already removed by `presentArgs`), the open card shows the arguments as a tree and a JSON output through the card's own detected-format tree. A `write` to an `xd://mcp__…` device resolves to the same renderer. `yield` is read by `src/chat/yield-submission.ts` the way OMP's terminal UI reads it (the accepted `details.data` over the data as sent, a rejection from the result text, an abort from `details.error`), and the task row's reviewer reads `extractedToolData.yield` through the same `yieldItems`/`yieldLabels`. The autoresearch details (`LogDetails`, `RunDetails`, `RunExperimentProgressDetails`) are read defensively in `src/chat/autoresearch.ts`; a result without the expected shape is shown as its text.

**Chat presentation and reading.** Application-scoped `ompDesk.toolCallDetail` defaults to Overview and is changed in Settings (the composer has no control for it); host readback of the setting updates every panel/bridge route live without an OMP mutation, including read-only chats. The look is derived from the theme: `--omp-muted` and `--omp-border` are `color-mix` of the foreground over the page (72 % and 18 %; the theme's own `descriptionForeground` and `contrastBorder` in High Contrast), so Light Modern really dims, and radii come from a three-step scale (`--omp-radius` 4 px for controls, `--omp-radius-card` 6 px for cards, `--omp-radius-composer` 10 px). A tool row is one 26 px line: a settled call shows no mark (its status stays in the head's sr-only `role="status"`), the name is regular and muted, the digest neutral, an edit shows `+N −N` in colour, a TODO call its operation and `done/total`, a Task `N agents: A, B` with the per-agent list as its tooltip. An empty conversation shows the folder, model, key hints and up to three recent sessions of the same folder: the page sends `omp:recent-sessions-request` (panel route only), the host answers `omp:recent-sessions` from the rows the Sessions view already lists and keeps that answer per editor document, and a click sends `omp:open-recent` with the answer's id and an ordinal, which the host resolves only against its last answer and opens through the Sessions click (`src/wiring/recent-sessions.ts`). An aborted reply is one muted line, a provider error one line, `Handed off` sans and muted, and the working row carries a spinner and a text shimmer that stop under reduced motion (and, for the shimmer, in High Contrast and forced colors). Manual disclosures, draft, source anchor, focused tool and native text selection survive both density directions. `TranscriptScrollController` is the sole vertical scroll writer for the outer transcript: upward wheel detaches immediately before measurement can repin, and programmatic geometry changes cannot rearm following. Source aliases preserve viewport offsets through prepend, grouping, measurements and same-conversation rejoin. `MeasuredRows` uses measured prefix heights, roughly one viewport overscan and bounded focus/selection islands; expanded giant runs flatten their members into this same outer window. Groups, individual detail bodies and read-only child content grow in transcript flow without inner vertical scrollers. Long source/output/diffs initially render 200 head lines and 20 tail lines with in-flow incremental/all-line disclosure; wide code retains horizontal scrolling. Live edits/output follow only through the outer controller. Typography, width, font and theme changes invalidate layout, not semantic data. The normal 100-card admission window can grow on explicit older-history reveal without growing mounted DOM. Paged history has no in-chat control: it is on exactly when VS Code's `editor.accessibilitySupport` reads `on` (`auto` keeps normal virtualization), bypasses pacing, and preserves 100-card earlier pages during append/prepend without separate inner tool pages. Latest-page replacement waits for active focus/selection release. Browser Find is explicitly limited to mounted history; there is no hidden full-history DOM.

**HTML pages in Chat (2026-10-19).** ([design](designs/2026-10-19-chat-html-pages.md), [ADR-0079](decisions/0079-serve-reply-html-pages-from-a-constant-loopback-frame-document-with-its-own-policy.md).) `ompDesk.chatHtmlPages` (default on) is read three times: on launch for the Chat prompt module (`OMP_VSCODE_HTML_PAGES`, `HTML_PAGE_INJECTION`), live as `htmlPages` of `ChatDisplayPreferences` (`lib/html-pages-enabled.ts`) and at activation to bind the frame server. `Markdown.tsx` hands a closed top-level `html` fence of the assistant reply text to `HtmlPageBlock`, which embeds `<iframe sandbox="allow-scripts" src>` of `host/html-page-server.ts`: a one-route (`GET /page`) loopback listener serving the constant frame document of `shared/html-page-frame.ts` with its own policy header (a hash for its one bootstrap, `unsafe-eval`, no nonce, no network, no frames, forms or navigation). The Chat document names the listener only as `frame-src` and `<meta name="omp-html-page-frame">` (`createGuestHtml`'s last parameter, from `EditorPanelsDeps.htmlPageFramePort`). The page source reaches the frame by `postMessage` after its `ready`; the bootstrap rebuilds it (`DOMParser`, indirect `eval`, `new Function` handlers); the Chat takes size reports only from the block's own `contentWindow`, and `bridge.ts` ignores host-channel messages whose source is an embedded frame (`lib/embedded-frame.ts`).

**Intent-first tool heads (2026-10-19).** `ompDesk.toolIntentDescriptions` is read twice: on launch for the Chat prompt module (`OMP_VSCODE_TOOL_INTENT`) and, live, as `toolIntent` of `ChatDisplayPreferences` (`readChatDisplayPreferences`, refreshed when the setting changes; absent = off), which `App` hands to a module store (`lib/tool-intent-first.ts`, like the screen-reader mode) that `ToolCard` reads. While it is on, a call that has an intent renders its collapsed head as icon · name · intent · target · facts (`omp-tool-head--intent-first`; the DOM order changes, not just CSS `order`, so Find and a screen reader read why before what). The intent is `flex: 0 1 auto` in the foreground colour; the target digest is `flex: 1 1 0; min-width: 0` in the muted colour, so it gets only the space the intent and the gist (`flex: none`, at most half the head) leave and is the only item that shrinks until it is empty, after which the intent ellipsizes. A read's or edit's single path (`isPathTarget` in `FileLinks.tsx`, shared with `ToolTargetText`) is laid out as `.omp-path-dir` and `.omp-path-file` inside the same file link: the directories sit in a right-to-left box holding a left-to-right `<bdi>`, so `text-overflow: ellipsis` cuts them at the start (character by character, not at a `/`) and the file name with its line selector keeps its width. There is deliberately no minimum width for the target: at about 320 px with a typical ten-word intent it disappears (its full text is the digest's tooltip). A command or any other target keeps the end ellipsis. A call without an intent, and the setting off, render the head exactly as before; the Overview group summaries are another component and unchanged.

**Send key in Chat (2026-10-21).** `ompDesk.chatSendWith` (`enter` | `shiftEnter`, default `enter`) is read once, live, as `sendWith` of `ChatDisplayPreferences` (`readChatDisplayPreferences`, refreshed on a change of the setting; parsed by `parseChatHostMessage`, absent = `enter`); `App` hands it to a module store (`lib/send-with.ts`, like the tool-intent switch) that every Enter-sends input reads with `useSendWith()`: the composer's keydown (`isComposerSendKey`), the `/review` editor and input dialogs (`AskEditor`), an ask's "Other" field (`AskDialog`) and a side question's follow-up row (`BtwPanel`). The chord rule is one pure function, `isSendChord` of `src/chat/send-with.ts`: `enter` sends on Enter without Shift, `shiftEnter` on Enter with Shift (and Ctrl or Meta in both). Callers keep the rules around it: an IME composition never sends, an open completion takes a plain Enter first (so `Shift+Enter` still sends with the list open), and in a fenced code block Enter and Shift+Enter are newlines in both modes. Alt turns a send into a queued follow-up (`Alt+Enter`; `Alt+Shift+Enter` in `shiftEnter`). Tooltips, `aria-keyshortcuts`, the empty-state hints and the quote hint read `SEND_KEY_LABELS`. Find, Rewind and the terminal keep their own Enter chords.

Long lines never widen the transcript (2026-10-13). Inline code, file, symbol and web links, URLs, long words, headings and list items in Markdown wrap at the block's edge (`overflow-wrap: anywhere` on `.omp-md` and on inline code, `.omp-md :not(pre) > code`); a block that cannot wrap scrolls inside a block of its own: a fenced code block's `pre`, a diagram's frame, a tool card's `.omp-pre`, and a table through its `.omp-table-scroll` wrapper. **Code is never wrapped (2026-10-14).** Wrapping was asked for inline code, where a path must break, and not for code blocks: every code block (a fenced block, a tool card's rows in the preview and the full view, a command's output, a Bash or Python execution's command and output, the native message blocks) is `white-space: pre; overflow-wrap: normal` with `overflow-x: auto; overflow-y: hidden`, so a long line stays one row, the block scrolls sideways, a vertical wheel scrolls the transcript and Shift+wheel or a horizontal wheel scrolls the block. A tool card's `.omp-pre` is a grid of one column, so every row, a highlighted match or a diff line included, is as wide as the longest line or the block, whichever is wider. The only wrap is the explicit **Wrap** switch of a card's full-view toolbar (per card, off by default whatever the size of the output; `.omp-rows--wrap`). Inline code and link text in Markdown carry a `<wbr>` after every `/` and `\` (and after `.`, `_`, `-` only inside a segment longer than 20 characters), inserted by `breakable` in `components/BreakPoints.tsx` from `lib/break-points.ts`, so a long path wraps at a separator and only a segment still too long breaks by the `anywhere` fallback; a `<wbr>` adds no character, so copying, selection, link targets and symbol lookups see the text as written. The transcript is `overflow-x: hidden` only as a last guard; `chat-ui.test.ts` (and `mermaid-ui.test.ts` for diagrams) assert at 360, 480 and 900 px that its `scrollWidth` never exceeds its `clientWidth`; `tool-output-ui.test.ts` asserts that a 200-character line of a grep, a read, a command, a fenced block and a command output is one row that scrolls, and that long inline code still wraps.

**Wrap lines on output panes (2026-10-24).** `WrapPane` and `WrapButton` (`components/OutputWrap.tsx`) are the one implementation of the per-pane Wrap lines switch, shared by Markdown code blocks (`CodeBlock.tsx`) and by tool output: `OutputRows` with `fixed` rows, `MatchOutput`, and the native Bash/Python execution and file-mention panes. The pane's choice is kept per page in `lib/code-wrap.ts` under the row scope plus the Section titles and the pane name, so it outlives card and virtual-row remounts; the button shows only while a `pre` or windowed list in the pane overflows (ResizeObserver plus MutationObserver, one measure per frame) or while the pane wraps. A wrapped pane gets `data-wrapped` (CSS `pre-wrap`), its rows are not windowed (as with the card's own Wrap), and code and diff rows hang their text under the first visual line so numbers and markers stay on it.

Detached reading never restores an anchor while wheel/touch/scrollbar intent is active; native compositor movement owns that interval, then the controller captures its settled position. The intent window is only a 250 ms heuristic, so it is not what keeps the anchor current: while detached, any `scroll` event the controller did not write itself (`#written`) and that moved `scrollTop` re-captures the anchor, so native movement delivered after the window ended (a compositor scroll reaching a main thread busy with live re-renders, a long glide, a browser scroll-into-view) can never be undone by the next layout restoring the line captured before it. A scroll alone still never detaches a following reader. Tool-call and content-slot aliases precede shared entry aliases so a multi-call reply cannot redirect an anchor to a sibling. A reading source remains a bounded mount exception when its old offset exceeds an invalidated height estimate. Mounted rows are synchronously remeasured after revision or layout-cache invalidation, even when their size is unchanged and `ResizeObserver` has no new notification. Only jump-to-latest or actual downward user movement to the bottom resumes following.

Explicit Latest or a manual arrival at the bottom overrides pre-existing held focus/selection while tail measurements settle. A new native body interaction may hold content again, and upward input still detaches immediately. Focused headers remain bounded DOM exceptions without pausing following; only body focus or native body selection holds live content.

**Bottom anchoring and following (2026-10-06).** The bottom edge of the transcript viewport is the top edge of the composer dock, and the line a reader sees there stays fixed relative to it. Following pins the tail synchronously: `TranscriptScrollController` runs its pin from `ResizeObserver` callbacks (after layout, before paint) and from layout effects, so streaming growth and dock resizes are absorbed in the frame they appear in, never one `requestAnimationFrame` later. Detached anchors record the viewport height they were captured at and compensate when it changes, so a growing or shrinking dock (typing, paste, attachments, image tokens, the working chip, feedback lines, the ask card) moves the reading offset instead of the bottom line. Only upward input (upward wheel, PageUp/Home/ArrowUp) or a touch/scrollbar drag is intent to leave the tail; downward wheel, Space or End while following neither pauses the pin nor can detach, so jump-to-latest appears only when the controller is detached by the reader. Rows for assistant text keep their last measured height across revisions (`MeasuredRow.growing`) so a streaming reply never snaps back to a text-length estimate and churns mounts. The composer's `autosize` freezes the dock height while it measures, so no transient dock height is ever laid out and clamps the transcript.

**Queued messages (2026-10-07).** Chat lists OMP's own queue above the composer (`QueuedMessages`, in `.omp-dock`): the host reads `get_state.queuedMessages` and `queue_update` into `ChatLiteState.queuedMessages` (steering, then follow-up; no ids, so a message is named by its exact chip text). Remove and Edit send `omp:chat-queue-remove`; the host runs one `remove_queued_message` per item in order, stops at the first unconfirmed command, and answers only the asking page, by request id, with one `removed | gone | unknown | failed` entry per item and no message text. Edit appends the removed messages (and their images, renumbered) after the composer's draft and sends nothing; `gone` is reported as already sent or no longer queued, never dropped. OMP has no dequeue-all that leaves the run alone, so Edit all is a sequence of removals. See [Edit queued messages](designs/2026-10-07-edit-queued-messages.md).

**`/btw` side questions (2026-10-19, [design](designs/2026-10-19-chat-btw-side-questions.md), accepted after two independent review passes).** OMP's `/btw` has a terminal handler only, so a typed one would reach the model as a prompt; `ChatRuntime.handleMessage` therefore tests `btwCommand` (`src/chat/btw.ts`, a leading token) first on `prompt`, `steer` and `follow_up`, before the Desk commands and the terminal-only answers, and serves it once per request id (`#serveBtwCommand`): a question is asked as a side question (`explained`: no turn state, the panel shows the answer), a bare `/btw` reads the history, and a refusal (busy, no model, history unsaved, an OMP without the commands, images attached, over 8,000 characters) is one fixed sentence (`BTW_REFUSALS`, chosen by matching OMP 18.11.1's wording, never displayed) that keeps the draft. `RpcSession` owns a `BtwChannel` (`src/host/rpc/btw.ts`): `btw_record`, `btw_delta` and the `notice` OMP raises for a failed history save are consumed in `#onFrame` before the chat reducer, so a side answer is never a transcript row, a turn, a queue entry or a context/cost figure, and the cache (a bounded copy of OMP's sidecar) is sent to pages only after `get_btw_history` was read once per session (`loaded`), which is what keeps a running answer through an extension-host restart (a new `RpcSession` for the surviving child). Pages get `omp:chat-btw` (records, `replace` after a snapshot or a history read), `omp:chat-btw-delta` (`at` is the answer length it extends; applied only to a running turn of exactly that length) and `omp:chat-btw-result` (`src/protocol/btw-messages.ts`), posted directly and never through `#deliver` (a `too-large` answer there is another snapshot); `fitBtwRecord` cuts a record that alone exceeds the bridge's frame, counted in the bridge's own measure (`btwWireBytes`, six bytes per non-ASCII unit). The panel's own actions are `omp:chat-btw-request` (`ask`, `cancel`, `history`), answered at every gate with `omp:chat-btw-result` by request id. The page keeps the store outside `ChatModel` (`BtwClient`, owned by `ChatClient`, reset on a new epoch) and renders `BtwPanel` in the dock between the queue and Rewind; it reports the `btw` owner of the Escape signal only while the focus is inside, so `Esc` there cancels or closes the panel and never also stops the turn.

**Stop, queue authority and TUI controls in Chat (2026-10-09, [ADR-0052](decisions/0052-answer-terminal-only-commands-and-restore-the-queue-on-stop.md)).** Guest protocol version 8: a page of an older build cannot receive the queue a Stop withdraws, so the host refuses it. Every Stop path sends `abort_and_restore_queue` once per request id (`RpcSession.abortAndRestore`). OMP withdraws the queue before it waits for the turn to stop, and the command waits behind any serial command, so its answer is awaited past the manual-compaction deadline rather than the ordinary 30-second command deadline. An OMP that answers `Unknown command: abort_and_restore_queue` gets a plain `abort`; its queue stays in OMP and in the dock. `ChatRuntime` answers only the stopping page with `omp:chat-abort-result`, carrying the withdrawn steering and follow-up messages, oldest first. The composer appends them to its draft and never sends them; entries past the host's cap of 64 and images it cannot hand back are flagged as truncated or dropped and reported. A queued steer therefore never runs as a new turn after Stop. Plain `abort` would let OMP's post-abort drain deliver it. The dock follows OMP's queue under one ordering rule. `RpcSession` counts `queue_update` frames and records the count on every command it writes. A `get_state` answer to a read sent before a later `queue_update` keeps the model's queue and applies the rest of the state; OMP 18.8.5 was observed writing such an answer after a `queue_update` that had already emptied the queue. A user `message_end` while the dock lists anything triggers a state readback, because OMP does not report every delivery of a live-steered message. **Send now** promotes a follow-up with `promote_queued_message` through the queue-request path (`purpose: "promote"`), and `Alt+↑` is an Edit of the newest queued message. Terminal-only builtins are classified from the installed OMP's registry, read by the staged `media/slash-registry.mjs` (`src/host/slash-registry.ts`); a new install root or version drops the previous list, and a failed read is not repeated for the same install. They are answered in the transcript with result `explained`, and a mapped Desk action runs. Compact, shake, model and thinking cycling, HTML export and share reach the host only as fixed `omp:chat-command` names; `runChatAction` asks for consent and chooses every argument. **Compact…** and **Shake…** (context popover, editor title menu, Command Palette) first ask for OMP's mode in a QuickPick: `/compact` `snapcompact` (preselected, no model call), `soft` or `remote` (these two then ask for optional focus instructions), and `/shake` `elide` (preselected, OMP's own default), `images` or `thinking`. OMP's RPC `compact` takes no mode and there is no RPC `shake`, so `RpcSession.maintain` sends the slash command as a `prompt` with an internal id and no `streamingBehavior`: OMP runs a builtin before any turn logic (a compaction during a turn aborts it and resumes it afterwards). A manual pass emits no maintenance event; observed with installed OMP 18.8.5, `/shake` reports one `command_output` line before its acknowledgement and `/compact` acknowledges at once and reports from the background (`Compaction complete…` / `Compaction failed: …`). The session therefore shows its own progress line from the send until that report, fails it on a `… failed` report or on an acknowledgement saying the text reached the model, and refuses a second pass meanwhile; the report itself still renders as the notice it always is. Retry re-sends `/retry` from the composer. `↑`/`↓` history is derived from the loaded user prompts (`src/chat/prompt-history.ts`), and `Ctrl+Alt+Y` (was `Ctrl+R`) adds remembered prompts from other conversations (`globalState`). The thinking and tool-call defaults (`Ctrl+Alt+T`, was `Ctrl+T`, and `Ctrl+O`) are `globalState` values pushed with the display preferences (`lib/transcript-toggles.ts`). OMP extension widgets and info or warning notices render around the composer, and errors become VS Code error messages.

**Served-model verdicts (2026-10-19).** Whether a reply was served by a different model than requested is decided by the installed OMP's own `classifyModel` (the rule of the terminal UI's served-model marker), never by comparing ids as text. The page asks only about replies whose served id differs textually (`omp:served-models-request`, at most 8 provider/requested/served triples of at most 256 characters); the host runs the staged `media/served-model.mjs` (`src/host/served-model.ts`) under OMP's Bun against the selected install, which resolves the catalog package from that install's root, and answers one verdict per pair (`omp:served-models`: `mismatch`, `same` or `unknown` when an id cannot be classified). Verdicts are remembered per install root and version; a failed read answers `unknown` for its pairs and is not remembered, so the next ask reads again. Nothing is read until a page asks, and only the panel that currently serves the slot is answered.

**Stop takes the message back (2026-10-21, [ADR-0082](decisions/0082-stop-takes-a-message-back-out-of-the-conversation-when-nothing-was-answered-or-done.md); [design](designs/2026-10-21-stop-returns-a-fresh-message.md)).** The same `abortAndRestore` also removes the message that started the turn when the Stop interrupted a turn that has neither acted nor, beyond 5 seconds after the send, answered. `RpcSession` records the `prompt` it sends to start a turn as an **anchor** in its ledger entry (text, images, send time and a baseline: the newest durable entry then held), only when no turn, pending row, queue or other ledger entry exists; a prompt sent during a turn is never one, and only text OMP turns into plain user input (or a listed skill invocation, or a file or prompt template) qualifies (`anchorKind`). The anchor is **armed** by the acknowledgement of its own `prompt` with `agentInvoked: true` and disarmed by a refusal, an `agentInvoked: false` answer, a `tool_execution_start` or a second user message. A `takeBack: true` flag on `omp:chat-abort` (a page that omits it gets the old Stop) makes the Stop take the anchor before its first await and, unless the live model already shows it cannot qualify (`mayStillWithdraw`, so a long-turn Stop waits no longer), raise a **withdrawal fence** that answers every mutation `busy` until the verdict. After `abort_and_restore_queue` (the plain `abort` fallback never withdraws) it waits, bounded, for the anchor's `prompt_result` and a settled session, re-reads the branch and asks the pure `withdrawVerdict` (`src/chat/stop-withdraw.ts`, `WITHDRAW_GRACE_MS` = 5 s): `remove` only when the tail since the baseline is bookkeeping, the one request, and at most one assistant message with `stopReason: "aborted"`, with no tool call and no text after the grace; `dropped` when only bookkeeping follows and the `prompt_result` says `aborted` with `agentInvoked: true` (OMP never dispatched the prompt, so there is nothing to remove); otherwise `keep`. `remove` is `RpcSession.navigate` with `kind: "rewind"` on the request entry (ADR-0051: the same CAS, fence, marker and re-sync; `omp-desk-navigate` also accepts a user-invoked skill invocation as a rewind target). The draft is the anchor's typed text and images from the ledger, not the stored entry. `omp:chat-abort-result` carries it as `withdrawn` (`unconfirmed` when OMP did not answer the navigation in time) or `kept: true` when the navigation was refused, and `ChatRuntime.#answerAbort` never drops it. `Composer.stop` merges `[withdrawn, ...queued]` through `offerQueuedForEditing`, after what the composer holds, and posts the `stop-withdraw` notice; Undo of the move clears an unedited refill only when the composer was empty and the withdrawn message was the whole hand-over (`rewoundRef`). Hidden notices written before the message and the memory recall stay on the branch, as after Rewind.

**Reply meta on the page (2026-10-19; [design](designs/2026-10-19-chat-reply-meta.md)).** `turnMeta` (`src/chat/reply-turns.ts`) sums each turn's usage (`src/chat/reply-usage.ts`, the terminal UI's `TurnUsageTally` sums) over all loaded entries, tool-only requests included, so the footer's `19K tok · $0.02 this turn` does not depend on which cards the window holds, and picks the first mismatching `(provider, requested, served)` pair for the footer of the reply that holds it (`announcedMismatches`, `src/chat/served-model.ts`). `lib/served-models.ts` asks the host once per pair and keeps the answer for the life of the page, so a failed host read hides a substitution until the Chat is reopened. A thinking block's time is the page's own measurement (`src/chat/thinking-clock.ts`, `lib/thinking-clock-store.ts`): none for a block it did not see begin. A failed request's frame (`ProviderError.tsx`) reads the recorded `errorStatus` and reaches the composer footer's model picker through `lib/model-picker.ts`.

**Host signals (2026-10-19; [design](designs/2026-10-19-chat-host-signals.md)).** Frames and `get_state` fields OMP's terminal UI shows and `toChatEventFrame` used to drop. `src/protocol/frame-notices.ts` maps `extension_error` and `rpc_frame_error` to `notice` frames (fixed `source` headings, text stripped and bounded, drawn as plain text by `NativeMessage`); Desk's own `/rewind` and `/clear` error paths stay swallowed. `RpcSession#onDroppedFrame` logs a dropped frame, and for a type that leaves the model behind the session (`message_end`, `tool_execution_end`, turn and queue frames) asks for the disk reconcile an `agent_end` asks for; a lost `message_end` first finalizes the streamed reply into its pending row (`finalizeDroppedStream`). `src/chat/host-signals.ts` parses `goal` (shown while goal mode is on or paused, as the status line's goal segment), `fastMode*`, `slowMode*`, `usageLimit` and `prompt_result.error` for host and page alike; `ChatLiteState` carries the first four into `HostSignalChips` in the footer, `goal_updated` rewrites the goal, the rest are as current as the last `get_state`. An `ask` option's `preview` and the request's `timeoutMs` reach `AskDialog`; the host stamps `expiresAt` when the frame arrives. `get_state.tokensPerSecond` is deliberately not read: it is one sample taken at read time.

**Tool display mode (2026-10-14).** The Chat has one global tool display mode of three values (`src/chat/tools-mode.ts`): `collapsed` (every Overview group closed), `groups` (every group open, every call in it one collapsed line) and `all` (groups open, calls expanded, the TUI's `Ctrl+O`). Two commands move between them from a Chat only: `ompDesk.toggleToolOutput` (`Ctrl+O`, Expand/Collapse All Tool Calls) and `ompDesk.toggleToolGroups` (`Ctrl+Shift+O`, Expand/Collapse Tool Groups), by `nextToolsMode`:

| From | `Ctrl+O` | `Ctrl+Shift+O` |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `collapsed` | `all` | `groups` |
| `groups` | `all` | `collapsed` |
| `all` | `collapsed` | `groups` |

`Ctrl+O` expands whatever is not fully expanded and collapses only from `all`; the groups key closes the groups from `groups` and lands on `groups` from anywhere else. `Ctrl+Shift+O` replaces VS Code's Go to Symbol in Editor only under the Chat `when` clause (the IntelliJ IDEA keymap binds neither it nor `Ctrl+O` outside Java editors; its `Ctrl+Alt+O`, Optimize imports, is why that key was not chosen, and `Ctrl+Alt` is AltGr on many layouts). The host keeps the mode in `globalState` under the key that always held the `Ctrl+O` boolean, `omp.chat.toolsExpanded`: `true` is `all` (what every earlier build stored), the string `"groups"` is `groups`, anything else `collapsed`; an older build reads `"groups"` as collapsed. It is pushed with the display preferences as `toolsMode`, so it survives reload and webview restore and applies to every Chat of the profile. In the page, `transcript-toggles.ts` keeps the mode and a generation; `Transcript` forgets every tool block's own state on a change (`clearToolDisclosures`) and reads the disclosure map through `withToolDefault`: an unset `run:` key (group) reads open in `groups` and `all`, an unset `call:` key reads open only in `all`. So a group that arrives later follows the mode, a group or call toggled by hand keeps its own state until the next key press, and the mode only changes the fold state; `ompDesk.toolCallDetail` still decides whether calls are grouped (Detailed has no groups, so `groups` looks like `collapsed` there).

**Text admission and original recovery.** `omp:chat-send-result` reports accepted, refused or unconfirmed admission to the submitting page, correlated by request id independently of later epoch reconciliation. Transport acknowledgement and eventual native `prompt_result` are separate events. `ChatClient` registers before posting, bounds a missing receipt and never resends. `Composer` retains editable text/images while awaiting admission, blocks duplicate submit routes without blocking Stop, and clears only an accepted unchanged revision. Unconfirmed originals and refused edited originals remain in explicitly dismissible recovery cards; Add to draft uses the existing append-and-renumber merge and does not duplicate an identical still-held message. Native command feedback remains until dismissal; model/thinking control feedback retains its existing transient lifecycle. This does not create a local queue or repair an unresponsive native dispatcher.

**A prompt OMP accepted and then failed on.** OMP acknowledges a `prompt` before it resolves the model's API key; a failure there (no key, an OAuth refresh the network refused) writes no user message and is reported only by a late error response and `prompt_result` `status: "error"`. The composer has already cleared on the acknowledgement, so `RpcSession` keeps each failed plain or skill prompt (`LedgerEntry.verbatim`, with its images) and, after the next reconcile pass, emits `prompt-failed` when no row with its text is on the branch or in OMP's queue. `ChatRuntime` posts `omp:chat-prompt-failed` (text, images or the count that the route could not carry, OMP's reason) to every page of the tab; `ChatClient.subscribePromptFailures` hands it to the `Composer`, which keeps it as an *Unsent message* recovery card (`keepUnsent`). A failure after the message was saved, a Stop, a template and a local or slash command are not reported this way.

**Complete composer handoff.** Guest protocol version 7 (now 8, see above) carries current text, attachment counts and bounded original/recovery records. A matching capture request freezes composer-local mutation through replacement, including delayed admission, image edits and programmatic insertion/prefill; in-flight image reads, IME composition and queue edits refuse capture. Cancellation/failure releases only that capture token. The host binds replies to the exact source document and retains the transfer until the new guest document acknowledges it; repeated Ready cannot restore into the frozen source, and a wrong document or request cannot consume it. Restored originals are recovery cards, not submitted messages. Missing image bytes retain explicit reattachment warnings and reserved marker numbers. No text or images are added to saved editor state, logs or disk.

**IRC rows.** `irc:incoming`, `irc:autoreply`, `irc:relay` and `irc:workpool` custom messages are one flat, collapsed-by-default tool-style row (`omp-tool omp-native-irc`: chevron, `comment-discussion` icon, bold `IRC`, `from → to`, kind and a muted one-line truncating preview). Expanding shows pool/mode/reply-to and the full body as Markdown; disclosure is controlled by `Transcript` (`irc:<conversation>:<row id>`), so it survives virtualization. In Overview, these native custom-message cards join consecutive eligible tool cards in a counted summary run; expanding renders the individual messages with their existing disclosures. Incoming and outgoing messages are counted as messages, not all described as sent. Detailed keeps the individual rows. Run members retain source aliases and native focus/selection across density changes; storage and call/result ownership remain unchanged.

Combined ComposerDock/FlatGroups installed acceptance exercised the whole native 120-read history with real wheel input over an expanded Read body: no detached repin, zero source-anchor drift through expand/font/resize, at most 57 mounted rows and no nested vertical overflow owner. Explicit Latest and manual-bottom following both reached the measured bottom. The same artifact proved the rounded metadata dock, quota reset countdowns and slash badge spacing; canonical checks and packaged/built/installed bundle equality are recorded in the [design evidence](designs/2026-10-04-paseo-style-chat.md).

**Paced replies and actions.** Canonical text remains complete in the shared model. Mounted append-only live prose/reasoning uses grapheme-safe rAF reveal; historical/remounted/replaced/terminal/hidden/reduced-motion/paged content flushes without replay animation. Native selected Markdown DOM is retained until release. One finished-reply footer copies canonical prose only and reports clipboard failure. Live timing follows one invocation through retries/steers/async resume; historical timing is explicitly user→reply, or final-model-response timing when only that field exists. Missing/invalid timing is unavailable, never an estimated turn duration. No Fork control exists. Only reveal policy was adapted from Paseo revision `5285b7e502714f2d08a220c328aedca3b9637b94`; modified-source attribution and full Apache-2.0 license/NOTICE ship in `licenses/paseo/`.

**Bottom-block HUD rows and detail tabs.** Two flat tool-style rows sit at the bottom of the chat column, directly above the composer card and below the transcript: `TodoHud` then `RunningAgents` (the TUI's order), rendered by `HudDock` at the top of `.omp-dock` (the rows plus `Composer`). They are in normal flex flow at the transcript's column width (about 840 px, aligned with messages and the dock), with no panel background or full-width band, and never inside or scrolling with the transcript, whose viewport ends above them. The rows are part of the measured bottom block: expanding, collapsing, appearing or disappearing one resizes the transcript viewport exactly like a growing composer, and `TranscriptScrollController` keeps the bottom visible line fixed against the block's top (following stays pinned, detached holds its line). Both start collapsed, AGENTS exists only while native registry members remain and TODO whenever tasks exist. Collapsed, each is one line: TODO shows the current task and one counter with an accessible name, Agents shows counts by state and who is doing what. Expanded they show the TUI's density, at most eight rows and a `+N more` button, with no scroll region and no status chips: TODO is a walking viewport (the phase holding the actionable task, closed lead-in, later phases as headers only), Agents is one line per agent (`id ⟨type⟩: description  tool · intent  elapsed`). The rows have fixed heights, so when both are open in a short window their combined height is capped at 40 % of the viewport (`hudSectionLines`, `todoWindowWithin`, `agentWindowWithin`) by showing fewer rows plus `+N more`, never a scroll region; the actionable task always stays. Transport identifiers, timestamps, source/index, raw progress records and statistics never render in the pinned rows. Todo phases come from dispatch-time state cuts and later successful non-view live updates; old disk/RPC rows cannot overwrite live authority. Closed means completed or abandoned. The projections are in `src/chat/hud-summary.ts`. The full content opens in editor tabs ([design](designs/2026-10-06-todo-agents-rows.md)): the TODO tab (phases, blockers, details, notes), the Agents roster tab and one tab per agent with a read-only child transcript that re-reads the newest rows every two seconds only while the agent is `running` (a failed refresh keeps the rows already read and says so). A detail tab is a presentation page of an existing conversation, not an editor slot: `omp:open-detail` is accepted only from a panel route, the host builds it with `createDetailHtml` (same CSP as chat, `connect-src 'none'`, no identity or bridge meta, so VS Code has nothing to restore) under the view type `omp.detail`, and `DetailTabs` (`src/host/detail-tabs.ts`) keeps one tab per conversation, kind and agent id, forwards to the conversation only `omp:ready` and the child read, and closes the tabs when the conversation is forgotten or its terminal mode takes over. Stop and Chat Reload keep them; a surviving bridge page keeps its fixed allow-list, so its open buttons are disabled with the reason. Leftover tabs from before a window reload are closed at activation (there is no serializer). Child history is lazy and read-only, native-id gated, limited to 200 retained rows per page and two nested levels, with explicit unavailable/reset/paging states and no composer or lifecycle controls. The bottom Tasks/Plan panel and all exclusive projection/staleness machinery are removed; plan content stays in the transcript.

**A finished TODO list leaves the pinned row (2026-10-19).** OMP's terminal UI hides a TODO list whose tasks are all `completed` or `abandoned` after `tasks.todoClearDelay` seconds and records the choice as a `todo_hud_state` custom entry (`dismissed`/`revealed`, with the `sourceEntryId` and the fingerprint of the list it was written for; `getTodoHudVisibility`). OMP 18.11.1 declares `todo_auto_clear` but never sends it over RPC, so Desk keeps the clock itself, host-side. `TodoHudClock` (`src/host/rpc/todo-hud.ts`, owned by `RpcSession`, synchronised from `#applyFrame`, `#publish` and every history cut) reads the model's list; while every task is closed it arms one timer for the delay (`todoClearDelayMs` in `src/chat/todo-hud.ts`: undefined means OMP's default of 60 s, a negative or non-finite value or one beyond a timer's range means never), asks `options.readTodoClearDelay(tabId, cwd)` for the delay only when a timer is needed (`ChatRuntime` forwards it per tab; `extension.ts` implements it with `omp config get tasks.todoClearDelay --json` through `readOmpSetting`, `src/host/omp-setting-read.ts`, in the session's profile and folder, so a failed read logs a warning and falls back to the default), and drops a delay that was read for a list that has since changed. At expiry it applies the host-synthetic frame `todo_hud_hide`, which sets `todo.hidden` in the shared reducer (`projectTodo`, `src/chat/todos.ts`: the same list keeps the flag, any other list clears it) and travels in snapshots as `todoHidden`; `HudDock` and `TodoHud` draw the row only when `todoRowShown` says so. The flag is presentation only: the TODO tab, the TODO tool cards and `model.todo.phases` are unchanged, and nothing is written to the session file. The history reader (`HistoryReader.#readTodo`) also returns the newest `todo_hud_state` whose `sourceEntryId` and fingerprint match the latest list snapshot: `dismissed` hides the row at once, `revealed` cancels the clock. A session opened after the list closed counts the delay from the timestamp of the entry that holds the latest list (`changedAt`), so a list closed for longer than the delay is hidden at once; a list with no date, or one that only the live stream knows, counts from the moment Desk learned of it. There is no control to reveal the row: the terminal UI's expand key and `/todo expand` are unavailable over RPC, and the row returns when the list changes.

**Jobs row, card chips and `proc://` links (2026-10-19, [design](designs/2026-10-19-chat-background-jobs.md); accepted).** `JobsHud`, the third row of `HudDock`, lists OMP's background jobs. OMP has no job list or job event over RPC (only `get_state.hasPendingAsyncWork`), so the inference lives in pure modules: `src/chat/background-jobs.ts` (`scanJobs`, `jobsViewOf`, `JobRecord`) builds one record per latest start of a job id (ids restart per OMP process) from start results, polls, cancel results, `async-result` deliveries and the live job frames; `src/chat/jobs-hud.ts` holds the summary, the bounded window (`jobsWindow`, counted in the shared 40 % cap), the honesty note, the cancel guard `jobsCancelBlocked` and the reading of OMP's `/jobs kill` answers; `src/shared/proc-links.ts` parses `proc://` names; `src/webview/lib/jobs-ui.ts` (`JobsUi`, one per page) and `jobs-context.ts` share the focus request, the open cancel, the last outcome and the `gone` tombstones with the row, the working row (`WorkingStatus`), the card chip and Cancel (`JobCard.tsx`) and the links. Data flow: `tool_execution_update` frames whose `partialResult.details.async` names a job, and the start result at `tool_execution_end`, are kept by the model as `jobFrames` (the newest 200, same Map while nothing changed) -> carried in the snapshot -> read by `jobsViewOf` on the page, with the transcript scan cached per entries array. A task frame is call-level evidence (a running frame ends nothing; an aggregate terminal frame settles the primary). Cancel is not a new RPC command: the page sends the text `/jobs kill <id|all>` through the existing `omp:chat-prompt`, which OMP's RPC mode runs as its builtin before queuing anything (so it works mid-turn), relying on the installed OMP without a version gate (ADR-0011); the buttons are enabled only when the session's command list holds `/jobs` with a `kill` subcommand, an ack that is not `explained` is shown as not cancelled, and the answer is read from the new ephemeral `command_output` row whose sentence answers that command. A send is an ordinary prompt, so it also clears a pending `/clear` wait and arms the identity check. A `proc://` link only focuses a job in the row and never sends. Limits: the list is a lower bound (older history, a task's later spawns, cancels the transcript never saw), a job frame OMP dropped is never made up and its job stays running until a Cancel answered `No running background job` retires it, `pending` can include queued deliveries, and `kill all` reaches only the jobs OMP owns for this session.

**Roster identity for revived agents.** The native roster drops an agent when it parks, and the frames of an IRC wake carry the message as `task` with no label (a cold revive even reports its own id as the type), so the chat model keeps each agent's type, label and assignment in `agentIdentity` (snapshot-carried, bounded to 256) and re-applies them to a woken agent; with nothing remembered it reads the type and assignment from the transcript's `task` call, and otherwise shows the id alone. A detail tab may ask the host for a sibling detail tab (`omp:open-detail`), which `DetailTabs` answers itself without reaching the runtime, so a roster row opens or reveals that agent's own tab. An expanded Agents row is glyph, id, muted type, current activity and time (the one-line shape in the paragraph above no longer includes the description): only the activity truncates, the id, badge and time wrap at extreme widths instead, and the spawn description and full activity are the row's tooltip.

**Registry liveness rows (2026-10-09, [ADR-0053](decisions/0053-merge-a-desk-published-registry-liveness-signal-into-the-agents-row.md)).**

- **The gap.** OMP 18.8.5's RPC roster hears only task-executor monitors. A finished subagent that a parent's `write agent://` steers into another run therefore never appears in it. Its registry ref also stays `idle` while it works.
- **Publisher.** `registerAgentLiveness` (`src/omp/agent-liveness.ts`) runs in every binding of the `-e` host-control module.
  - Subagent bindings record their own `agent_start`, `turn_start`, `tool_execution_start` (tool and intent) and `agent_end` in a process-global store.
  - The first `rpc`-mode main binding subscribes to `AgentRegistry.global().onChange`. It publishes `{v, instance, seq, sessionId, agents[{id, agent, state, sessionFile?, tool?, intent?}]}` through its own `ctx.ui.setStatus("omp-desk.agent-liveness", …)`, with at most 32 agents and 16 KiB, shedding detail rather than skipping.
  - It coalesces at 150 ms for edges and 1 s for activity. It re-checks every 2 s only while an agent is live as `idle`, and repeats a non-empty set every 10 s. Nothing runs while no subagent is live.
- **Shared derivation.** `subagentLiveState` is also the rule for `hasLiveSubagents`. It counts `running` (not a stale accepted run), `idle` with queued messages, and `idle` with a streaming session whose own binding observed new work after `lifecycle.terminalAt`.
- **Host and page.**
  - `RpcSession` takes that status key out of the status line. `AgentLivenessGuard` (`src/chat/agent-liveness.ts`) accepts only the bound session id, with a newer `seq` per publisher `instance`. It keeps the newest list, re-applied after a resync reset unless the replay was truncated.
  - The shared reducer folds the accepted list as `agents_liveness` (`reduceAgentLiveness`, `src/chat/agents.ts`). RPC rows stay authoritative and are only annotated with `liveState`. Unlisted ids become `origin: "registry"` rows, running with the published activity and the remembered spawn identity. The next publication that omits a registry row removes it.
  - Any RPC frame for an id takes its row over. A terminal frame removes it, unless the newest publication reported the agent `idle`-live, in which case the row is kept as a registry row. `get_subagents` snapshots keep registry rows.
- **Installed check (2026-10-09).** In owned isolated windows with real OMP 18.8.5, a parent spawned a subagent that finished, then sent it a `sleep 45` follow-up (receipt "Delivered").
  - The pre-fix build showed no Agents row for the whole 45 s.
  - The fixed build showed "Agents 1 running · <id>: bash · Sleeping forty-five seconds" throughout, and removed the row within about a second of the subagent's final reply.

**Footer and composer.** `Composer` owns one rounded, theme-variable-only card outside transcript scrolling: multiline input above a toolbar (left: host-readback model/thinking QuickPicks and Git branch; right: context usage, Attach and prompt actions). The toolbar controls are quiet, text and icons in `--omp-muted` that take the toolbar hover surface (High Contrast keeps `contrastBorder`); Send is the one filled control, a pill with its label (an icon below 460 px), neutral when disabled; focus shows one ring, on the card. Notices and queued messages have their own rows, without duplicate footer chips. Model/thinking triggers have non-shrinking minimum widths and aria-hidden Codicon chevrons. The Codicon Git branch disappears first at 860 px; Stop/Queue follow-up become icons at 720 px and Send/Steer at 460 px. Below 380 px actions move to their own row, rather than squeezing the pickers. The tool-call density is the `ompDesk.toolCallDetail` setting only; the toolbar has no control for it. The context ring opens passive hover/focus details for native usage, cost and quotas using contrast-aware VS Code hover tokens; Escape dismisses it without stopping a turn. Prompt placeholders are short sentence-case text; stopped sessions retain their explanation and Resume action. Enter sends/steers, Alt+Enter queues follow-up and Shift+Enter inserts a newline, with shortcuts in action tooltips and `aria-keyshortcuts`; refusals preserve drafts/images. Pending asks keep the toolbar visible below their answer/cancel actions. On live attach the session sends `set_ask_dialog`; a multi-question ask receives one ordered answer array. Ask cards use widget/focus borders and UI-font option labels with Codicon radio/checkbox markers using checkbox tokens; options, review rows and tabs are flat rows (only the chosen option and the active tab keep a `focusBorder` edge; High Contrast keeps `contrastBorder`), and Cancel and Stop are text buttons beside the one primary Next/Submit. Full question titles remain visible in review; Left/Right cycle tabs and answers survive switches. The actionable id-bound ask stays at dock top, outside virtualization, and answers once; its chronological transcript boundary is independent. Slash and path completion use pushed catalogues, insert without executing, preserve drafts on Escape and never accept/send during IME composition. Their popups may extend above the card without clipping; completion and context popup owners independently reserve Escape. No settings, tool-inventory or usage panel is introduced.

**The composer's editing surface and code blocks** (`src/webview/lib/draft-surface.ts` with `draft-dom.ts`, `draft-text.ts`, `draft-history.ts`, `draft-write.ts`; `components/DraftEditor.tsx`, `Composer.tsx`, `lib/draft/cell.ts`, `lib/code-fence.ts`; designs `docs/designs/2026-10-15-composer-contenteditable-surface.md` and `2026-10-15-composer-code-blocks.md`; [ADR-0077](decisions/0077-edit-the-chat-draft-in-a-contenteditable-surface-rendered-from-the-markdown-string.md)). The draft is one Markdown string (the `text` of the Draft value that the cell holds, `lib/draft/cell.ts`), and the field is a `contenteditable` `div` (`role="textbox"`, `aria-multiline`) with one `div.omp-line` per line that `DraftSurface` renders from it: only the lines of a fenced block (CommonMark's fence grammar, unclosed to the end of the draft) take the editor font on a full-width band, prose keeps the UI font, and a `[Image #N, WxH]` marker of an attached image is an atomic inline `span.omp-chip` holding the literal marker text. React gives `DraftEditor` no children and no changing attribute; the surface owns the element's attributes (`configure`), cancels every cancelable `beforeinput`, applies it to the string (`apply`: markers only ever go whole, one undo step recorded, lines re-rendered, selection placed, text reported through `onEdit`) and keeps its own undo stack (`Ctrl+Z`, `Ctrl+Y`, `Ctrl+Shift+Z`; `Edit > Undo` is inert) and a retained selection like a textarea's, so `focus()` returns the caret where it was. Every write from outside is a `Write` of the Draft (`lib/draft/field.ts`) that the cell performs through the `FieldPort` of `lib/draft-write.ts`: a `text` write is `setText(text, {caret?, history: "record" | "reset"})` (send, Rewind and the hand-off restore reset the stack; history recall, queue restore, `set_editor_text`, accepted completions and Commands inserts are one undo step), and an `edit` or `remove` write is `apply`/`removeRanges`, which serve inserted markers, `@path` references and the thumbnail ×. An IME composition opens one *composition window*: the surface writes nothing to the DOM or selection until a settle with no composition active has read the text back and re-rendered the touched lines, the port reads that window (`composing()`), and work that arrives meanwhile (marker inserts, restores, Rewind, paste, cut, the ×) waits in the cell or is skipped, as each writer's `Timing` says, and runs from `settled()`, which `onSettled` calls. The hand-off capture `freeze()`s the surface (`contenteditable="false"`, `aria-readonly`), stronger than a textarea's `readOnly` so no composition can change the captured draft. Paste is plain text (an image attaches), copy and cut write `text/plain` from the model, and a plain-text drop inserts at the pointer. While the caret or selection sits inside a block, `Enter`, `Shift+Enter` and `Alt+Enter` insert a newline with the line's indentation (and at the end of an unclosed opening fence also the closing fence, in one edit), `Tab` and `Shift+Tab` indent and outdent (four spaces, previous tab stop; several lines for a multi-line selection; fence lines untouched), and `Ctrl+Enter` falls through to the normal send rules; `Shift+Tab` with nothing to outdent is left to the browser and moves the focus back, the keyboard's way out because VS Code's workbench receives every key the page sees (so `Escape` would also stop a running turn and `Ctrl+M` is the workbench's global Tab-Moves-Focus toggle). No completion is asked while the caret is in a block (`completionsEnabled`), an IME composition, a held draft and a modified `Tab` are never handled, and the text sent is unchanged. A status row under the input reserves its height while a block exists and only its words follow the caret; the completion list is announced through `aria-controls`, `aria-activedescendant` and a debounced visually hidden `role="status"` ("N suggestions").

**The Draft module** (`src/webview/lib/draft/`, entry `draft.ts`; `lib/draft-write.ts`, `lib/use-draft.ts`, `lib/attach-reader.ts`, `lib/attach-dom.ts`; design [`docs/designs/2026-10-23-composer-draft-module.md`](designs/2026-10-23-composer-draft-module.md), vocabulary in [`docs/GLOSSARY.md`](GLOSSARY.md)). One module owns every rule of the Chat composer's draft: the immutable `Draft` value (text, attachments, counters, reads in flight, revision, recovery cards, the message awaiting admission), the pure operations that change it (`attach`, `removeAttachment`, `keyEdit`, `completionAt`/`acceptCompletion`, `insertCommand`, `recall`, `setEditorText`, `insertText`, `restoreQueued`, `rewind`, `quote`, `removeQuote`, `restoreHandoff`, the cards), the send composition (`compose` in two phases, `beginSend`, `settleSend`, `addToDraft`) and the **cell**, which holds the value, decides when a change may be written (an IME composition window, the hand-off of ADR-0050) and writes it through a `FieldPort`; the real port (`draft-write.ts`) calls the editing surface of ADR-0077, which stays the field adapter (the string in the DOM, the selection, the text-only undo). `Composer.tsx` translates events into operations and renders the value (`useDraft` gives the cell a lifetime and subscribes React with `useSyncExternalStore`); it composes no send and imports no draft helper. The module has no React and no DOM, so its rules are node tests (`lib/draft/*.test.ts`); Chromium keeps layout, IME, focus, DOM paste and drop and the end-to-end checks. Two import rules in `scripts/check-imports.mjs` keep it so: **R-draft-1** a file outside the directory may import only `draft/draft.ts`; **R-draft-2** a file inside may import only the directory, `src/shared`, `src/protocol`, `src/chat`, `slash-completion.ts`, `code-fence.ts` and type-only `@oh-my-pi/pi-wire`, never `react`, `react-dom` or a `.tsx` file.

**Command and skill chips** (`src/webview/lib/draft/command-chips.ts`; `draft-dom.ts`, `draft-surface.ts`, `draft/image-references.ts`, `draft/field-rules.ts`, `Composer.tsx`, `styles.ts`; design `docs/designs/2026-10-18-composer-command-skill-chips.md`). A command or a skill that the draft names is the third and fourth kind of atomic chip beside the image and file markers, found by the same kind of pure scan, so nothing is stored and the sent text is the draft unchanged. `commandResolver(snapshot.commands, working)` maps a token to `command`, `skill` or nothing from `slashCatalogue` (a denied builtin is not in it; Desk's own commands also match in any case; chips are the same during a turn: OMP's rpc `steer` and `follow_up` do not act on a command or a skill (they queue the text, and `steer` refuses an extension command), so `RpcSession.steer`/`followUp` send a message that invokes a listed skill or starts with a listed command (`routesAsPrompt`, `src/chat/prompt-routing.ts`, the catalogue rule `findCommandChips` shares) as a `prompt` with `streamingBehavior` `steer`/`followUp`, as OMP's terminal UI does, and OMP decides what it does mid-turn; a denied command is refused as a prompt would be, and the message is accounted like any prompt (ledger entry and `prompt_result`)). `findCommandChips` mirrors what OMP 18.11.1 acts on in a `prompt`: the leading token of the draft after the blanks the send trims, or the first `/skill:<name>` token elsewhere (`SKILL_TOKEN_RE`, not when the draft starts with another slash command or a `!`/`$ ` sigil), and only once whitespace follows the name; the chip covers the token and the one space after it. `findChips` merges them with `findReferences` (a token inside a marker's label is not a chip), and `fieldRules(known, resolve)` (`draft/field-rules.ts`) is the surface's one question about them: `DraftSurface` asks it for `chips` (render), `snap` (selection snapping), `deletion` (deletion widening), `atomic` and `bareAt` and `repair` (IME repair), so Backspace, Delete, the arrows, selection and Undo need no code of their own (`ReferenceSpan`'s helpers take a plain `SpanRange`). `Composer` builds the rules over the cell's value and a resolver ref, and calls `surface.refresh()` when the catalogue or the turn state changes; `refresh()` writes the selection back snapped off any chip that now covers it. The chip is a `span.omp-chip.omp-chip--command|--skill` with `data-kind`, `role="group"`, an `aria-label` (`Command compact`, `Skill review`) and a codicon before it (terminal, book).

**Magic keywords (2026-10-19, [design](designs/2026-10-19-chat-magic-keywords.md); accepted, implemented, unreleased).** OMP's standalone prompt words (`ultrathink`, `orchestrate`, `workflowz`, `jevify`) are painted with OMP's hue gradient in the composer and in the user's sent messages. The matcher is pure and shared (`src/shared/magic-keywords.ts`, adapted from `pi-tui`'s `magic-keywords.ts` and `markdown-prose.ts`; `src/shared/magic-colour.ts` solves each gradient stop's lightness per theme kind), and `src/omp/magic-keywords.test.ts` replays a recording of the installed OMP's own answers (`src/omp/magic-keywords-recorder.ts`, `fixtures/omp-magic-keywords.json`; `magic-keywords.live.test.ts` re-records and compares). Which keywords OMP acts on is one epoch-free exchange: the page posts the field-less `omp:chat-magic-keywords-read` (only while a keyword word is on screen, at most once per 30 s but at once when the composer or the window gets the focus, `lib/magic-keyword-settings.ts`) and `ChatRuntime` answers that page alone with `omp:chat-magic-keywords { keywords }` before its no-session and read-only checks, from `readMagicKeywords` in `extension.ts` (one `omp config list --json` in the tab's folder and profile through `readOmpSettings`; concurrent askers share a read and a success is reused for 5 s; a failed read answers nothing). The question is also on the surviving-bridge allow-list, the detail-tab gate and the guest dispatcher. The paint is the CSS Custom Highlight API (`lib/magic-keyword-paint.ts`: highlights `omp-magic-<id>-<stop>` named by the leaf `lib/magic-keyword-name.ts`, priority -1 so Find wins, styles generated in `magic-keyword-styles.ts`), driven by `lib/magic-keyword-views.ts` from `MutationObserver` rescans of the composer (`scanComposer`, chips as one space) and of a sent message's rendered Markdown (`scanBubble`, code and controls as one space); no DOM is added to the contenteditable surface or to a message, and the composer shimmer is a 70 ms timer only while the field has the focus in a window that has it and neither reduced motion nor forced colours is on.

**Quoting Chat text** (`src/webview/lib/markdown-leaves.ts`, `markdown-inline.ts`, `markdown-table.ts`, `quote-capture.ts`, `quote-selection.ts`, `draft/quote.ts`, `draft/quote-draft.ts`, `draft/quote-lines.ts`, `draft/quote-edits.ts`, `quote-sink.ts`, `use-quote-draft.ts`; `components/Markdown.tsx`, `QuoteRemoveButtons.tsx`; design `docs/designs/2026-10-15-quote-chat-selection.md`). A quotable `Markdown` instance (assistant text, thinking, a user message) keeps, for every leaf block it draws (paragraph, heading, rule, code block, table row), the source lines it came from and the columns the parse stripped from each (`LineOrigin`), stamps the element `data-md-leaf` and registers the records for its `div.omp-md` in a page-local `WeakMap` after commit; streaming reuse keeps the leaf numbers of reused blocks. `captureSelection` measures the Range per leaf and `quoteSurface` rebuilds the source of exactly the selection with the inline tokenizer the renderer shares (`markdown-inline.ts`): a token covered whole is source, a cut one is its shown text, a marker comes back only when the selection starts at the leaf's start, quote prefixes always, code is re-fenced longer than any backtick run in it; a leaf whose records disagree with its DOM text degrades to the shown text and the notice says so. **A table is cut to what the selection touches (design amendment 2026-10-17).** A row leaf's pick carries the cells the Range has text in (`CellCover`: the Nth `td`/`th` is column N, with the part of its text covered) and whether the Range reaches outside the table (text before or after it, `reachesOutside`; a range that only ends at the start of the next block does not). One touched cell quotes its selected text alone, with the inline rules of a paragraph. Several touched cells quote a valid table of the header, the separator and the touched rows, cut to the columns between the start cell and the end cell (`tableSlice`): every cell is carried as written (`splitTableCells` returns a cell's `source` beside the `text` the renderer draws from, so escapes such as `\|` survive), the two edge cells are cut to the selection and written back with `\` and `|` escaped, and the separator's own cells keep their alignment. A selection that starts or ends outside the table takes the touched rows whole with every column. Both triggers (`Alt+Q` and the `webview/context` item gated by `ompChatText` on the transcript region, which run `ompDesk.quoteChatSelection`, which posts the `quote-selection` panel action) call `quoteSelectionIntoDraft`, which asks the composer through `quote-sink.ts`, whose `useQuoteDraft` applies the refusals (held draft hand-off, size, empty) and the one edit: `placeQuote` appends `Q + "\n"` after a blank line, closing an unclosed fence first. In the draft a quote is a per-line band (`findQuoteRuns`), with its own keys and a Remove quote button per run; the sent text is the draft verbatim, and the transcript ends a quote at the first line without `>`.

**Find in Chat** (`src/webview/lib/transcript-find.ts`, `find-dom.ts`; `components/TranscriptFind.tsx`, `find-styles.ts`; design `docs/designs/2026-10-17-chat-find.md`). Two paths open the bar, and both end in the same `openFind`. The contributed keybinding `Ctrl+F` (`Cmd+F` on macOS) under the Chat-only `when` runs `ompDesk.findInChat`, which posts the `find-in-chat` panel action; `provideFindAction` in `lib/panel-actions.ts` hands it to the hook even when the Chat refuses other panel actions, and the command is also in the Command Palette and `editor/title`. VS Code resolves the keybinding from the physical key, so it works on any keyboard layout. `useTranscriptFind` (called by `Transcript`) also listens for the key on `document` in the capture phase, so it works from the composer, in a view-only Chat and while the host restarts; it reads the letter with `isPhysicalKey(event, "KeyF")` (the physical key on every layout: a Cyrillic layout reports "а" for F and the earlier character test ignored the press in a real window; the 2026-10-21 rule below replaced the Latin-character shortcut `isLetterKey` took). VS Code forwards every key the page handled to the keybinding service too, so one press arrives twice, and `openFind` returns at once when the open bar's field already has the focus: it never reselects what is being typed. VS Code's `enableFindWidget` is not used: it can only search mounted DOM. `searchUnits` searches the loaded transcript model once typing has paused for 150 ms (user prose, assistant text and thinking, tool head and output text; never notices, summaries, attachments, history before a reset or the reply still streaming, whose row carries no find id), with the shown-text cache bounded by characters and cleared when the bar closes; `reveal` pins the window or sets the page, opens the overview run, the call and the thinking block, then scrolls through `transcript-scroll`'s `reveal(sources, offset)` below the bar. Matches in mounted rows are painted with the CSS Custom Highlight API (`omp-find`, `omp-find-current`, repainted at most every 200 ms on mutations) and a `data-find-current` row marker when a range cannot be made. `Esc` is handled on the bar container, and while focus is anywhere inside the open bar it reports the `find` owner to `composer-overlay`, so the host's stop-turn binding stays disarmed for that press.

**Quoting an editor selection** (`src/extension.ts` `quoteEditorSelectionToSession` and `pickSendTarget`, `src/wiring/editor-context.ts` `composeEditorQuotes`, `src/shared/quote-limit.ts`, `src/webview/lib/editor-quote.ts`, `insert-text.ts`, `quote-sink.ts`; design `docs/designs/2026-10-15-quote-chat-selection.md`, decision 9). `ompDesk.quoteEditorSelection` (`Alt+Q` under `editorTextFocus`, and the `editor/context` menu) reads the active editor's non-empty selections (with none, the primary caret's line becomes a `path:N` reference, `composeLineReference`, sent as `omp:insert-text`; an untitled document is refused) and the session picker offers only Chat sessions (a new session is opened as a Chat). The host composes one `GuestEditorQuote` per selection (`location` as `path:line` or `path:start-end` through `mentionPath` against the target session's cwd, `fileName`, the editor's `languageId` and the text with LF endings) after refusing what cannot be quoted (over 20 000 characters in all, a control character, more than 64 selections), and posts `omp:insert-quote` once the chat page has announced itself. The page, not the host, writes the Markdown (`editorQuoteMarkdown`: the location as inline code, never an `@` mention; a fenced block with `fenceFor` for code, plain quote lines for Markdown and plain text), hands the sources to the quote sink (`offerQuote` holds them until a composer mounts), and `useQuoteDraft` places them as one insertion joined by blank lines, exactly as a Chat quote is placed.

**Quoting from a diff (2026-10-18; decision 10 of the quote design).** `quoteEditorSelectionToSession` reads the focused side of a diff (VS Code's `activeTextEditor` is the side that has the focus, in a side-by-side, an inline and a multi-file diff editor alike) through `quoteSourceOf` (`src/wiring/quote-source.ts`). A `file:` document is the file on disk. A `git:` document (the Git extension's `{"path","ref"}` query, parsed by `parseGitDocumentUri`, `src/wiring/quote-revision.ts`) is one version of a file: `revisionVersion` names it (`index` for the empty ref, a `~` ref resolved as the Git extension does (the index when the file is among the repository's `state.indexChanges`, else HEAD), the merge stages, a hash as its short form, a symbolic ref as `HEAD (3f2a9c1)` through `repository.getCommit`, bounded to 3 s) or answers `null`, and then the quote has no location. The words travel as part of `GuestEditorQuote.location` (`path:42-50 @ HEAD (3f2a9c1)`; grammar in `src/shared/quote-location.ts`, which the page also reads: `Markdown.tsx` draws a code span that is such a location as plain code, since a file link would open the file on disk at other lines). `src/wiring/git-api.ts` holds the Git extension API accessor `commit-links.ts` and `quote-source.ts` both use.
