**Quoting a hunk of a diff (2026-10-19; decision 11 of the quote design).** With no selection, `quoteEditorSelectionToSession` first asks `quoteHunkOf` (`src/wiring/quote-hunk.ts`): the visible tab of the editor's own group must hold a diff (a `TabInputTextDiff`, or a multi-file diff's `textDiffs`, read structurally), the other side is read with `openTextDocument`, and `hunkAtLine` (`src/shared/line-diff.ts`, a bounded Myers line diff; VS Code has no hunk API, so its blocks can differ from the editor's) finds the block that has the caret's line among its changed lines on the caret's side. The result is a `hunk` request: `composeHunkQuote` (`src/wiring/editor-context.ts`) writes the unified-diff rows as one quote (`languageId: "diff"`, `fileName: ""`) under `hunkLocationText` (`src/shared/quote-location.ts`: `<modified location> was <original location>`, each side by the rule of decision 10), and the same `omp:insert-quote` message carries it. `Markdown.tsx` `codeSpan` draws a span that `isHunkLocation` accepts as plain code. With no hunk under the caret the line reference of decision 3 of the chat keys design is what happens.

**Quote target without a picker.** `sendToSession` for a quote first asks `visibleQuoteTarget(sendCandidates(...), editorRecency.order())` (`src/wiring/editor-context.ts`): a `SendCandidate` now carries `visible` (`tab.panel.visible`, so the front tab of its group in any editor group), and the first candidate that is visible, a Chat, `live` and has an editor, in recency order, is the target with no QuickPick; with none, `pickSendTarget` runs as before. Add File to Session never takes this path (a line reference does). Delivery is the same `openSession` and `deliverInsertion` for both.

**Rewind in the page** (`src/webview/components/Rewind.tsx`, `src/chat/rewind-mode.ts`; ADR-0051). `ChatView` owns the `reduceRewind` state (idle, picking, pending) and the Undo notice; `Esc` `Esc` within 500 ms in an empty idle composer, `/rewind` or `/branch` typed alone (intercepted in `Composer`'s `send()`, never sent) and a prompt's hover **Rewind to here** in `.omp-row-actions` start picking, which marks the chosen prompt and dims the rows that would leave the branch, while `RewindBar` above the composer names what leaves and the files changed after the target; a `done` `omp:chat-navigate-result` puts the rewound prompt in the composer (a draft already there moves to a recovery card first), Undo takes it back out while still unedited, and `BranchPointMarker` after the card where branches leave the path switches to one of them.

**Collapsed history after confirmed context clear** (`src/chat/cleared-history.ts`, `components/Transcript.tsx`; proposed ADR-0059). Only a `reset_boundary` named by a final `omp-desk/clear` marker can collapse display history. A boundary from a known incomplete Desk clear during this attach renders **Context clear did not finish; the next message may still include earlier history. Retry /clear.** A markerless native/unknown boundary renders **Context reset**, never an inferred failure. Earlier messages remain visible in both cases. A confirmed divider expands read-only history with no inline Rewind, Retry or reply footers; its count is user/assistant messages in the latest cleared segment, possibly a lower bound, not OMP's native dropped count. Paging, earlier loading and disclosure persistence remain unchanged. All Desk Rewind target lists and host target admission filter before the last confirmed boundary. Full branches remain recoverable separately; model-derived TODO and disk todo seeds do not cross reset boundaries, while OMP's canonical TODO may persist.

**Slash argument completion.** The existing catalogue carries subcommand usage through both bounded parsers (500 characters per usage). After an allowed command or alias and whitespace, the composer filters first-argument variants locally; later arguments stay normal text. Muted nonselectable rows show input hints, with `[...]` interpreted as optional and a leading `<...>` as required; unknown formats remain raw text. Empty arguments have no forced selection, Tab or navigation chooses a variant, and Enter without a selection retains normal sending except for an empty required argument. The shared submit handler refuses that empty required argument for keyboard, button and host actions, preserves the draft and reopens its hint. Selection never executes, IME composition never accepts, and existing lifecycle deny policy remains unchanged (including denied-command hints).

**Tool and child-detail presentation.** Failed tool rows have one `error` Codicon in `testing.iconFailed`; titles, tool icons and chevrons retain normal foreground, exit codes stay muted metadata, and no error badge repeats the status. Native output owns result text, so expanded stderr is not repeated in a generic error section; a distinct structured error remains available. Very narrow live Wait headers retain a short Wait title. Single-agent tabs use the shared icon-button Refresh action and a tool-style chevron Assignment disclosure, omitted when the first user transcript card already contains the assignment. Child paging, serialized refreshes and read-only semantics are unchanged.

**Ask answers.** The dialog keeps answer state as well as editor text: opening Other marks that row and clears a single-choice option, and typing immediately updates the custom answer used by question tabs, review and Submit. Enter or Use answer advances rather than being required to record text. RPC sends one ordered `{id, selectedOptions, customInput?}` per question; a custom single-choice answer carries no selected options. Settled cards prefer native `details.results` or singular `details.question` answers over original input questions, with filled radios and checked boxes only for selected options. Empty multi-selection is valid, not cancellation.

**Ask repair acceptance (2026-10-08).** A private baseline of the initial commit plus the Ask repair passed typecheck and 224 targeted browser, renderer, host and model tests, then build, x64 packaging and isolated installation. A real agent's native three-question ask retained custom text entered before Next without Use answer, selected multi-choice and single-choice answers through review and native settlement. Real lone selected and lone custom asks also retained their answers in the transcript. Screenshots and native saved results were inspected; the owned session, broker and window were stopped and the isolated profile removed. This does not claim a main-profile installation or a combined build of concurrent features.

**Chat status and statistics.** `chatBanner` projects phases into one fixed notice and Codicon without exposing raw phase labels. A stopped or history-only session always explains Resume, ignoring host launcher details; host disconnection takes precedence over the session notice. Pending questions and approvals replace execution status with a question icon and “Waiting for your answer”, without an execution spinner or pill; the composer progress overlay still marks the unfinished turn. Canonical projection omits initial root model/thinking settings and repeated unchanged values, but keeps real changes and cropped-page changes. A matching known model display name replaces its identifier. TODO reminders remain neutral chronological snapshots with readable statuses and blockers; the pinned TODO header carries its count once. Existing shared format helpers provide spaced durations and compact token units across reply, agent, Wait/Task and context displays; reply timing attribution is a tooltip, and agent tabs show elapsed only in their heading.

**Steady Chat turn progress.** The shared reducer retains the latest streamed tool-call `i` or execution-start intent, trimmed with trailing dots removed, across stream/tool gaps and snapshot reattachment. A fresh display turn resets it. `WorkingStatus` is always the final transcript-content child at a fixed 24px height, outside measured source rows; it combines the former working and compaction lines without changing scroll anchors or bottom geometry when its text changes. Idle is empty. The existing display-turn start and shared second tick supply elapsed time through `fmtDuration`; clocks are excluded from polite announcements, and intent announcements coalesce at a one-second minimum interval. A 2px `progressBar.background` overlay on the composer spans send to observed settle, including async engine gaps and asks: a question pauses, but does not finish, the turn. Reduced motion uses a static bar. Lost/recovering host observations suppress progress. OMP 18.6.3's RPC `setWorkingMessage` is unsupported, so extension-authored working messages cannot be shown; only tool intents and RPC maintenance facts are available.

**Commands button** (`components/CommandsMenu.tsx`, `lib/command-list.ts`; composer toolbar, between the context ring and the prompt actions). A `terminal-cmd` icon button labelled "Commands" (tooltip: "Commands (or type / in the message box)"; no keyboard shortcut is bound) opens a popup anchored to the composer card, upward and at most 460 px wide, so it cannot overflow a 360 px panel. The search box takes the focus; ↑/↓ (wrapping), Home/End on an empty search, Enter, Tab (closes), the mouse and Escape (closes and returns focus to the button) drive it, and the popup reports itself to the host as composer popup owner `commands`, so Escape never also stops a running turn. The data is the slash completion's own catalogue (`slashCatalogue`: Desk's commands, then what the session advertises, identity-changing builtins removed), one row per command with its name, description, a source chip (OMP, Skill, Prompt for file/custom/MCP prompt commands, Extension, Desk) and the marker. **GUI support** means Desk handles the command with a native VS Code surface instead of passing text to OMP or refusing it as terminal-only. Those rows come first, alphabetical or by relevance within the group (name prefix, alias prefix, name or alias substring, then description), carry a `window` codicon whose tooltip and accessible name say what opens, and are: `/models` (alias `/model`) and `/agents` (the native Models and Agents settings editors), `/settings` (the OMP Settings editor), `/rewind` (alias `/branch`; the Rewind picker over the transcript), `/clear` and `/fresh` (in-place page actions), `/new` (a new session in place of the tab), `/delete` (a confirmation dialog, then a new session), `/compact` and `/shake` (VS Code mode pickers), `/export` (a Save dialog) and `/share` (a confirmation dialog) when the session advertises OMP's builtin of that name, and the terminal-only `/hotkeys` (VS Code Keyboard Shortcuts filtered to OMP Desk), `/git` (Source Control), `/skills` and `/extensions` (the Skills and Tools view) `/setup` and `/login` (the Log In to Provider picker) and `/logout` (the Log Out of Provider picker and its confirmation). A same-named extension, skill or prompt command is an ordinary row, never the GUI one. The ordinary group is every other command OMP runs from chat text. **Terminal-only commands without a Desk equivalent and the identity-changing commands Chat refuses are not listed**: Chat cannot run them, the typed `/` completion already hides them, and about thirty inert rows would bury the runnable ones; a one-line note under the list says so. **A pick:** a GUI command runs its action directly (Rewind starts the page's Rewind; `/compact`, `/shake`, `/export`, `/share`, `/hotkeys`, `/git`, `/skills`, `/extensions`, `/setup`, `/login` and `/logout` post the fixed `omp:chat-command` actions `compact`, `shake`, `export-html`, `share`, `keyboard-shortcuts`, `source-control`, `tools-view`, `provider-login` and `provider-logout`, the last five needing no live conversation; the two provider actions run in the profile and folder of the session whose Chat asked, as the Chat footer's Log In to Provider does, while a typed `/login` or `/logout` runs the palette command and so follows the active session; the settings, session and agent commands are sent as their bare text, which the host answers before anything reaches OMP); any other command that takes arguments (an input hint or subcommands) is put in the draft as `/name ` with the caret after it, replacing a leading command token as the typed completion does and otherwise leaving all the draft's text after it; a command with no arguments is sent as its own message by the Send button's route (steer while a turn runs, otherwise a prompt), and **the draft, its images and its caret are never touched**. A row is disabled with its reason shown under it when it cannot run: no live session or a read-only page, an open dialog, a draft message awaiting confirmation, a running turn for a plain command (OMP runs builtins, skills and extension commands only from a prompt, and the composer steers during a turn), `/new`, `/clear` and `/fresh` during a response, Rewind's own blocked reasons, and a maintenance pass for `/compact` and `/shake`. Completing the draft is allowed during a turn.

**Waiting for background work (2026-10-13).** While OMP's `get_state.hasPendingAsyncWork` keeps the turn open but the agent is not working (`asyncPaused`, no stream, no dialog, no compaction), `src/chat/background-wait.ts` replaces the row's intent and whole-turn timer with “Waiting for background job: <name>”, “Waiting for N background jobs” or “Waiting for background work”, timed from the newest transcript message (when the agent last did something). OMP 18.8.5 reports background work over RPC only as that boolean: there is no job list, no job start/end event and no RPC command to cancel a bash job, so the names are inferred from the transcript (a started job is a tool result with `details.async.jobId` and the call's `i` or command; `job`/`wait` listings update it; an `async-result` custom message ends it). Jobs older than the loaded window, extra task spawns and jobs cancelled outside the transcript are not seen, so a count can be low and the generic sentence is used when none can be named. No cancel control is offered: the only cancel command OMP exposes over RPC is `cancel_subagent`, which covers subagents, not bash jobs, and Desk does not call it. When the agent works again the row returns to its intent and the turn timer.

**A compaction that closes without a frame (2026-10-16).** OMP sends `auto_compaction_end` to the client before its extensions handled the event and before the pass releases `get_state.isCompacting`, so the `get_state` read Desk takes at that frame can still report a compaction, and no later frame follows (`chatTurnInProgress` reads `state.isCompacting`, so the composer would keep the working animation). `RpcSession` therefore keeps the one-second `get_state` readback it already runs for `asyncPaused` going while the state reports a compaction that no running maintenance pass of the session accounts for (`#compactionOpen`), and stops when OMP says the compaction is over. The same readback ends a state taken from any read during a manual `/compact` typed in the composer, which has no start or end frame and, when aborted, no report line either; Desk does not poll for such a compaction before a read has seen it. It never ends the busy state on its own.

**A stopped manual compaction (2026-10-16).** A `/compact` started from the compact button waits for OMP's one `command_output` report, and a pass the user interrupts prints none (`slash-commands/builtin-lifecycle.ts` returns silently on a user-interrupt cancellation), nor does OMP emit an `auto_compaction_end` for a manual pass. OMP answers `abort` and `abort_and_restore_queue` only after `AgentSession.abort` awaited the manual pass's cleanup, which releases `isCompacting`. `RpcSession` therefore ends an acknowledged manual `/compact` as cancelled when OMP answers Stop (`#endStoppedPass`), not by a shorter timeout; the 15-minute report wait stays only for a pass that ends without any signal. `/shake` is not affected: it reports before it acknowledges and has no pass to close.

**A command OMP runs itself, and a session's name (2026-10-16).** A typed builtin such as `/rename` is answered by OMP with `command_output` and an acknowledgement of `agentInvoked: false`; the RPC layer discards its prompt ticket, so no `prompt_result` follows (`rpc-mode.ts`, local outcome). `ChatRuntime` reports such an acknowledgement as the `handled` outcome and the guest dispatcher tells the page `explained`, so the composer starts no turn state for it (the progress bar is otherwise cleared only by a working change, a result or feedback). OMP announces a new name with `session_info_update`; `ChatRuntime` forwards it as the `title` event, and `adoptSessionTitle` (`src/wiring/session-title.ts`) records it as the row's cached title, repaints Sessions (the tree re-reads the file's title when the cached one changes) and renames the editors. A fresh session without a file keeps the name in that cache and in OMP's memory until the file exists; an unchanged name writes nothing, so a rename made from Desk and OMP's echo of it agree.

**Steady progress installed evidence (2026-10-08).** A frozen x64 VSIX was installed into an owned isolated window and exercised with two real five-tool turns in Dark and Light. All 665 sampled final status rows stayed 24px; nine unchanged-body intent transitions had zero scroll, tail and composer movement, and every sampled working state had the 2px overlay. A real-wheel detached source anchor stayed fixed through 233 samples. Idle/working screenshots in both themes were image-inspected. One 27px content-growth follow gap at settle corrected after 16ms, so this is not an all-frame zero-gap claim. The owned session, broker, window and listeners were stopped; targeted checks, exact artifact identity and remaining limits are recorded in the [Paseo design](designs/2026-10-04-paseo-style-chat.md#steady-working-status-2026-10-08).

**Theme and contrast.** Inline Markdown code uses the paired `textPreformat` foreground/background tokens; fenced and tool code pair the normal foreground with `textCodeBlock.background`. Action buttons use primary or secondary button tokens and the button-border/contrast-border fallback, including icon-only controls, neutral Stop and ask Cancel. Warning notices use input-validation warning background/border with normal text and a warning-colored icon; warning chips keep neutral text. Closed TODO rows and detail text use `descriptionForeground` with a strike-through, with only the completed check in the passed-icon color and cancelled circle-slash muted. The context ring uses butt caps and a solid, stronger track, shows its percentage beside the ring, and retains its contrast-aware hover card.

Model and thinking triggers explicitly use normal foreground on a transparent background, toolbar-hover tokens and button/contrast borders rather than inheriting browser-native button backgrounds from chip styling. Regression tests require readable enabled normal and hover states across Dark, Light and both High Contrast themes.

**One profile-wide catalog (implemented; installed catalog acceptance remains separate).** [ADR-0034](decisions/0034-store-sessions-in-one-profile-catalog.md) uses one versioned document under global storage, short cross-process locking and per-record read/modify/write merges. Unreadable catalog integrity still refuses catalog mutation rather than replacing it with an empty snapshot. A one-time, ledger-bound import copies only the registered folder list from the predecessor extension id's catalog (`src/wiring/predecessor-import.ts`); it never writes the predecessor's files and imports no session rows, claims or broker slots. The earlier per-workspace Memento importer was removed before the first release. Shared facts remain distinct from window-local availability/editor/reply observations. [ADR-0039](decisions/0039-refuse-only-on-a-verified-live-writer-the-extension-owns.md) narrows only ownership: sticky import disagreements do not veto operations, and an unowned editor can control its surface without a writer lease. Catalog/import evidence remains in `profile-catalog.test.ts` and `predecessor-import.test.ts`; this policy cutover does not claim new installed-window evidence.

**Catalog I/O and ledger size.** No catalog read, lock, write, `fsync` or rename retry runs synchronously on the extension host's thread (`src/host/profile-catalog.ts`), and the conflict ledger keeps the newest three records per `(key, identity, reason)` with an `earlier` count of the dropped ones, applied on the next committing transaction ([ADR-0034](decisions/0034-store-sessions-in-one-profile-catalog.md) amendment, 2026-10-12). `src/wire/loop-lag.ts` reports every extension-host stall of 500 ms or more to the OMP Desk log with the marked Desk operations that overlapped it: a `warn` from 1 s, a `debug` below it.

**A visible Chat page reports that it is alive (2026-10-23, [design](designs/2026-10-23-page-heartbeat.md)).** `src/webview/lib/page-health.ts` (installed by `boot()` for session documents, not for detail tabs) posts `omp:page-health` (`src/protocol/page-health.ts`, part of the guest vocabulary, panel route only, `GUEST_PROTOCOL_VERSION` unchanged) from a `setTimeout` chain every 2 s while `document.visibilityState` is `visible`, and sooner (at most every 500 ms) when an event happens: a host message type (a guarded tap in `src/webview/bridge.ts`, `guestTransport.observeInbound`, called before any listener handles the message), a commit of the conversation view, an input kind. A hidden page stops its beat timer after one `visibility` report. The report holds a ring of 12 event kinds, `lateMs` (how overdue the pending beat is) and, from a `longtask` observer joined with a `long-animation-frame` one, a task of 1 s or more with the browser's attribution; every string comes from a closed vocabulary the parser enforces (no id, class, folder, URL or text). `src/host/page-health.ts` (`PageStallDetector`, pure; `PageHealthMonitor`, one per window, wired in `registerPanelLifecycle` so every editor's page is watched) logs at Info a page's silence of 10 s with its last events, its end, the close or replacement of a stalled page and long tasks (one a second, 50 per page; 20 stalls per page; the bounds belong to the document the host loaded, so a page cannot reset them by restarting its counter). A page is held to the threshold only while the host sees the reporting document, the very `PanelDocument` the extension loaded, as the visible one of its panel. A page whose last report said hidden is held to it only after the host has seen the user away (window unfocused or tab not in front) at some check since that report and both are true again, so a hidden report made while the user was in the window does not become a false stall. The clock restarts after a hidden spell and after a host gap above 6 s, and a `seq` that did not increase is a page that reloaded in place.

**The Chat page's motion is a low-rate timer, not CSS animations (2026-10-23).** A running CSS animation makes the browser produce a frame on every display refresh for as long as it lives, whatever it changes, and a text-clipped gradient is repainted by the main thread each time. Measured in Chromium (`SystemInfo.getProcessInfo` over 20 s, a fixture page with nothing else going on): a working page with the working row's glyph and sweep and the progress line animated cost 89 ms of renderer and 100 ms of GPU time per second; the same page without the animations 0 and 3; one lone `codicon-modifier-spin` icon alone 10 and 36. A page in a retained tab that VS Code hides with CSS costs 0.2 and 0 (the browser stops its animation frames, its `visibilityState` stays `visible`, its timers run once a second or faster). `src/webview/lib/page-motion.ts` (installed by `boot()`) therefore writes the custom properties `--omp-spin`, `--omp-sweep` and `--omp-progress` on the glyphs (`.codicon-modifier-spin`, a non-empty `.omp-working-status-text`, `.omp-turn-progress`) every 83 ms from one timer (how a step is taken is amended in the next paragraph), and the stylesheet (`styles.ts`) turns them into `transform` and `background-position` inside `prefers-reduced-motion: no-preference`; the spinner keeps its eight steps a second. The module has no timer while nothing moves: each glyph's rule carries a one-millisecond `omp-motion-present` animation whose `animationstart` starts the loop (and paints the new glyph at once), and the first step that finds no glyph ends it. The same fixture then costs 14 ms of renderer and 21 ms of GPU time (35 ms). The one-second clocks (`useSecondTick`, the thinking seconds, the question countdown) share `src/webview/lib/second-tick.ts`, whose interval stops while `document.visibilityState` is `hidden`. The Sessions view's working and background rows no longer use VS Code's `sync~spin` animation (36–46 ms per second per window that showed one) but a static green `circle-filled`; the transient starting, stopping and checking icons still spin.
**The motion loop writes its own steps and does not wait on a frame (2026-10-24).** Three faults made the spinners of a working Chat tab freeze, crawl or turn unevenly; all were measured in the installed build in a real VS Code 1.143 webview (Playwright over CDP, a hidden desktop, the long page below mounted 7,900 DOM nodes from 40 turns of 80 KB of Markdown, with a turn streaming into it) and are fixed together. (1) **Frozen.** The first loop treated an awaited frame as proof that it lived, so a glyph that appeared could not restart it and a frame the browser never delivered froze every spinner at one angle (a Chromium page test with a swallowed `requestAnimationFrame` reproduced it). (2) **Uneven.** A step was written in the frame that followed a timer, so an 8-a-second spinner (125 ms) and the 12-a-second sweep (83 ms) took steps 100 and 200 ms apart on an idle page (median 100, p90 200). `PageMotion` now sets one timer, due just after the next boundary of the 125 ms grid of the spinners (the working row's glyph included) or the 83 ms grid of the sweep and the bar, whichever are on screen, and writes the step the clock reads (a late timer skips a step instead of drifting); a style write costs the same in a timer as in a frame. (3) **Crawling on a long conversation.** The page's animation frames did not stop (21 a second while saturated) and timers ran 25–50 ms late: the main thread was busy 7.9 s of every 8 (Layout 4.5 s in 178 runs of 24 ms median, one dirty node among 18,208 layout objects, `getBoundingClientRect` in `MeasuredRows` 4.5 s of a 15 s CPU profile, 127 long tasks in 31 s) and the spinner took 5.2–5.6 steps a second with 190 ms between them. The cause is `.omp-transcript { flex: 1 1 auto }`: the scroll box is a flex item of the column-flex `.omp-transcript-frame`, a basis of `auto` makes the browser measure its content height in a pass before the final layout, the two passes differ in constraints so the layout cache misses, and one changed word laid out every mounted row (24–31 ms on that page, 0.2 ms with `flex: 1 1 0px`; `overflow: hidden` or `contain: strict` on the box gave the same 0.2 ms, `contain: layout` alone was worse). With `flex: 1 1 0px` the same page has no long task, timers at most 10 ms late and 8.0 steps a second, and a Chromium subtest of `page-motion-ui.test.ts` (400 paragraphs, one changed word: 0.1 ms against 9.7 ms with `auto`) defends the basis. **A page that is not drawn.** A Chat tab behind another tab of its editor group keeps `visibilityState` `visible` and its timers at full rate (a 50 ms interval 12 ms late at p95) but gets no animation frame at all (`hasFocus()` false, 0 frames a second, nothing from `IntersectionObserver` or `ResizeObserver`), while a page the reader sees gets at least one every 180 ms even when saturated. So every step asks for a frame (`MotionHost.frame`), and a page that got none in `FRAME_STALE_MS` (1 s) while its timer ran is taken as not drawn: the loop then wakes every `DORMANT_TICK_MS` (1.1 s, deliberately not a multiple of the spinner's 1 s period, or every look would find the glyph at the position it already shows) to ask again and to write the step, so a page wrongly taken as hidden crawls instead of freezing, and it is back at its own rate with the first frame, or at once on `MotionHost.onWake` (`visibilitychange` to visible, window `focus`, `pageshow`, the reduced-motion query changing) or when a glyph announces itself (the browser runs an animation only in a frame it draws). `visibilityState` `hidden` and reduced motion stop the loop. The old frame-gated loop wrote no step at all in a tab behind another one, and the long page, streaming there, ran 79 long tasks (35 s in 39 s) with the same layout cost. The stylesheet turns a spinner icon inside its own box: the element is `overflow: clip` and only its `::before` (the glyph) is rotated, because a square rotated by 45° reaches 3 px past its layout box and as a `transform` of the element itself that grew the scrollable overflow of `.omp-notices` (`overflow-y: auto`, `max-height: 112px`) at the four diagonal steps, which showed as a vertical scrollbar flickering on the starting banner (`.omp-notice` with `loading codicon-modifier-spin`, `scrollHeight` 22 over `clientHeight` 21). `src/webview/lib/page-motion.test.ts` (fake clock: steady rate, grids, busy page, dormant page, wake-ups, a throwing glyph, a randomised long run) and `src/webview/page-motion-ui.test.ts` (Chromium: step gaps, lost frames, focus, the layout cost, the banner) defend it.
**The OMP Desk log is a levelled `LogOutputChannel` (2026-10-14).** `createOutputChannel("OMP Desk", { log: true })` replaces the plain channel, which VS Code wrote to its log file only while the channel was shown in the Output panel or when the window closed (a Reload left an empty file). The log channel writes continuously, adds its own time and level, and its file is `<logs>/<session>/window<N>/exthost/hmmvot.omp-desk/OMP Desk.log`; a message carries neither. There is one logger interface, `Logger` in `src/shared/logger.ts` (`error`, `warn`, `info`, `debug`, `trace`; `silentLogger`, `prefixedLogger` and the test `recordingLogger` beside it), `log` in `src/extension.ts` is a `Logger` over the window's channel (silent before activation), and every module that logs takes `log: Logger`; there is no plain-string sink. Levels: `error` is a failure that broke a user action (a launch, restore, Resume, view switch, rename or Kill that did not happen); `warn` is degraded-but-continuing (`host control unavailable`, a failed or timed-out probe, a refused request, an event-loop stall of 1 s or more); `info` is lifecycle and summaries (`extension activated`, `restore: …`, `window timing: startup restore timeline`, one `window timing: <new session | resume | open> <tab> start timeline, ms from the command: …` line per session start (or `… start ended after N ms, failed or cancelled at <step>; …` for one that did not complete), one `window timing: restore tab …` line per restored tab, attach and launch outcomes, `host control verified`, settings moves, stop and kill outcomes); `debug` is the high-frequency diagnostics (`tool spawn: …`, the hand-over `window timing:` steps, `code symbol …`, `file name …`, bridge document states, focus traces, notification observation, event-loop stalls under 1 s); `trace` is anything noisier. The default level is Info, so a Reload leaves its activation, restore summary and timeline readable without any setting; the level is raised with the gear of the Output panel or **Developer: Set Log Level…** → **OMP Desk**, and a test window starts at one with `scripts/test-vscode.ps1 -DeskLogLevel debug` (VS Code's `--log=hmmvot.omp-desk:debug`; the end-to-end suite passes `debug`, `E2E_DESK_LOG_LEVEL=default` leaves Info). Nothing reads the log text back: **Show Diagnostics** renders from the window's recorder. The PTY broker is another process and keeps its own file log. A session start the user makes is reported in ONE `info` line (`src/host/launch-timeline.ts`, 2026-10-21), every step as milliseconds from the command, in the order reached: the Desk's laps (draft (New Session only), panel, admission, stage, host control keys, runtime proof, editor document, broker slot), the phases the broker launch timed itself (resolution, launcher probe, bun attempt, runtime check, verify, broker spawn, handshake, child identity), `omp spawned`, conversation, run intent, and the steps OMP and the Chat reach later: `omp control published` (the `startedAt` OMP stamped in its control record), `omp ready` (the `ready` frame, first seen by the conversation: `RpcSession`'s `onReady`), `host control verified` and `chat live`. The line is written when the start is complete: at `host control verified` for a Terminal, at `chat live` for a Chat (after host control has verified, or has failed or was never carried: `host control unavailable`). A start that fails or is cancelled writes the steps reached so far and where it ended (`launch refused`, `not started`, `unconfirmed at the broker launch`, `chat failed (<code>)` or a Chat process that exited before it was live, the Cancel of the external-writer question, an error before the launch). The run begins when the command handler is entered, so the times are from the click: New Session (`new session`), Resume from the composer, a folder's history or Resume Session (`resume`), and Open of a stopped row, in either view (`open`), plus Restart (`restart`) and a view switch of a live session (`view switch`). A command whose launch never reaches the launcher (the session already runs, the admission refused it, another window runs it) reports nothing; the startup restore begins no run. The per-step `launch started N ms after the command` line stays at Debug.

**Levels of the Chat broker runtime line (canonical mapping, 2026-10-14).** `PtyBrokerClient` writes one `Chat broker runtime: …` line per launch that has to start a broker, once the outcome is known: a slot that holds a record logs none, a launch without a runtime policy or without the OMP launch logs none, a Bun start that left no broker logs nothing about Bun, and a refused Bun start logs only the fallback line (the time that attempt took is the `bun attempt` phase of a new session's timing line). The level follows the reason, chosen by `chatBrokerChoiceLevel` in `src/host/chat-broker-runtime.ts`, which is the code side of this paragraph. **`info`:** a Bun broker exists, or it is not on Bun because of the user's configuration or how OMP is installed: `setting` (`ompDesk.chatBrokerRuntime` is `vscode`), `platform` (not Windows x64), `launcher` (OMP's own compiled program, or a hand-built launch with no launcher kind: there is no Bun to share) and `executable` (the launch's command is not an absolute `bun.exe`, cannot be resolved or read, or resolves to a file that is not `bun.exe`). **`warn`:** a Bun that qualified in principle was not used: `trust` (the file or a folder above it can be changed by another account), `proof` (the pipe-only self-check refused it or did not finish), `runtime-mismatch` (the self-check ran as another image than the judged `bun.exe`, for example a forwarding shim first on PATH), `version` (older than 1.4.0), `changed` (the file or the staged tree changed after it was proven) and `start` (the Bun broker could not be spawned, ended before it published a record, could not be attached, or the file is held off the Bun path after such a failure on an earlier launch).

**Session ownership and witnesses (`session-availability.ts`, `session-index.ts`, `rpc-reconcile.ts`, `session-claim.ts`).** [ADR-0039](decisions/0039-refuse-only-on-a-verified-live-writer-the-extension-owns.md) governs every user action: only a local running runtime, an exact live child authenticated under the extension's own broker, or another verified-live extension-window holder may block or require stopping. A recorded RPC tuple `{slot, brokerId, brokerGeneration, childPid, childCreationTime}` is re-proven by the authenticated broker handshake and, for a current-build broker that holds its pipe child's handle, the broker's own attestation of that child ([ADR-0035](decisions/0035-distinguish-a-recorded-process-from-a-later-occupant-of-its-pid.md) amendment of 2026-10-13, accepted after independent architecture review); whatever is not attested is proved by this window's own kernel reading taken for that proof. Legacy broker children use their authenticated slot plus child generation. Unknown, missing, dead, mismatched and unanswerable observations are unowned, without a release step. No PID-only kill exists. Claims replace dead/unverifiable records under the identity mutex; unusable storage/mutex gives a process-local lease without namespace mutation. This accepts unrecorded-launch/external-writer races, not global exclusion. Accepted absence evidence still gates **automatic** running-intent relaunch only.

Concurrent RPC ownership reads for the same client, slot, recorded broker/child identity and kernel reader share only their in-flight proof. Settled and failed proofs are discarded, so later reads revalidate the live identity; the kernel creation-time readings under them are synchronous in-process calls through the staged native module (`src/wire/pty-identity.ts`, [ADR-0076](decisions/0076-call-win32-in-process-through-a-staged-koffi-module-instead-of-starting-powershell.md)) and keep nothing at all. Native proofs remain independent. This prevents simultaneous Sessions/Open checks from consuming redundant bounded broker connections and turning a live writer into an unowned observation.

**Legacy rows.** A missing managed `transport` discriminator denotes an earlier-build record; explicit `rpc` and `native` are managed transports. A positively verified historical `managed-omp` child is Running (legacy) and is not silently migrated. Its explicit confirmed stop-and-continue and truthful broker stop remain; unverifiable legacy rows are usable stopped/draft rows. Ordinary Open on any existing managed editor only reveals it. Reload requires an actual reattached runtime and preserves its mode.
Explicit Stop verifies that legacy broker's recorded child gone, then requests the existing protocol-v2 `shutdown` with `requireStopped:true`. It disconnects, forgets the conversation's broker mapping and clears its cached restore conflict; the broker's own record stays as recovery metadata. A shutdown refusal is disclosed without undoing the writer-gone proof.
A fresh fileless draft launch clears the previous draft host's session id before recording its new attempt, so the first current-runtime identity can be accepted and its file promoted. Existing-file resume IDs stay pinned; a confirmed not-started launch restores the prior draft identity. Current-runtime and differing-ID fences are unchanged.
A live host whose session id the row never recorded (a reload right after New Session, before the first `get_state` answered) is attached without an expected id: the session learns it from the process and records it through the ordinary identity path; a recorded id that differs from the host's is still refused, and that refusal says to Stop or Kill the process in Sessions ([ADR-0021](decisions/0021-reattach-a-verified-live-draft-on-activation.md) amendment).

**A launch whose window ended before it read the outcome, and a re-adopted host's session id (2026-10-19, [ADR-0031](decisions/0031-reattach-a-surviving-managed-host-through-its-broker.md) amendment).** The launch attempt `SessionIndex.#openEntry` records before it asks the launcher (`pid`, `rpc` and every identity `null`, recognised by `isUnreadLaunchAttempt`) now carries `runIntent: running` with it, and the previous intent is put back when the launcher reports that nothing started; a window that ends inside the launch therefore leaves a row the next restore re-adopts instead of one that reads as the user's stop beside a running writer. `reconcileOwner` resolves such a row through the slot its launch reserved for the conversation: `probeBrokerSlotForRunningChild` returns the pin the broker's own record and the child's creation time prove (and the kind of host the slot holds), and a running child of the same transport (`rpc` ↔ `managed-rpc`, `native` ↔ `managed-omp`) becomes `attachable` with that pin, which the attach re-proves. Only the conversation's own slot mapping counts (`OwnerReconcilePorts.conversationBrokerSlot`, `BrokerSlotStore.forConversation`): a slot reached through a controlling editor's mapping alone, which `brokerSlot()` and the row's provenance fall back to, is not evidence about this row's launch, and its child stays `live`. `describeAttachMismatch` compares only the transport for such an attempt, there being no recorded process or broker identity to compare; the session id is fenced where a process can report it, because the attach hands the recorded id to the Chat as its expected id. A row that recorded a process, a legacy row and a slot holding another kind of host stay `live` (a conflict, as before). Separately, `LocalLauncher.attach` returns the session id recorded for the host (`recordedHost.sessionId`, the row's own id only when none was recorded), not the row's file id: a Chat `/clear` rotates the process's id (ADR-0059) and `SessionIndex` records the handle's id after every attach, so returning the file id replaced the rotated id at the first reload after a clear and the second reload failed `identity-mismatch` against the same process and file.

An allocated native session path need not exist before the first assistant response. Repeated observations of the same runtime/file/session identity keep one pending promotion retry rather than restarting it or invalidating the Sessions projection. Materialization still requires the exact header and claim; a changed promotion failure remains diagnostic.

**Folder navigation and history query.** Folders are navigation only; they never change VS Code workspace roots or session ownership. Sessions shows one per-window list (`LauncherFolders`, `src/views/launcher-folders.ts`, [design](designs/2026-10-07-window-folders-and-other-window-state.md), [ADR-0045](decisions/0045-derive-window-folders-per-window-and-pin-with-identity-ids.md)): the folders VS Code has open in this window (local `file` roots, VS Code order, live on `onDidChangeWorkspaceFolders`, hidden by `ompDesk.showWorkspaceFolders: false`), then the other **pinned** folders (the profile-wide `WorkspaceFolderRegistry`, catalog key `omp.workspaceFolders.v1`, stored order), then folders kept visible only by a session running or launching in this window. Entries are deduplicated by `folderIdentityKey`; a folder both open and pinned appears once with the pin icon. Window folders are never persisted. A folder's id is `windowFolderId(path)` (a hash of its identity key), and Pin stores the record under that id, so Pin and Unpin never change a row's identity or a folder command's argument; records from earlier builds keep their ids. Collapsed state is in the pinned record for pinned folders and in this window's workspace state (`omp.windowFolderCollapsed.v1`) for the others. Pin and Unpin replace the old Remove; Unpin is metadata-only and never refused by a running session: a folder this window has open or runs a session in stays visible, unpinned. Every consumer (New Session, Resume Session, Open Terminal, `folderArgument`, the Add File picker, the welcome context) reads this one list. On-demand Resume Session scans readable exact files and canonical indexed paths, deduplicates them and exposes the same exact-path Delete transaction regardless of provenance. Header/path integrity is revalidated; ownership uncertainty is not a refusal.

**Session folder.** Sessions runs at the root of the version-control checkout an opened folder lies in, or in the folder itself when it lies in none or `ompDesk.runSessionsAtRepositoryRoot` is off for it (setting `resource`-scoped, default on; [design](designs/2026-10-14-run-sessions-at-the-repository-root.md), [ADR-0073](decisions/0073-run-sessions-at-the-checkout-root-and-let-the-editors-follow.md), which replaced the agent-root substitution). `findCheckoutRoot` (`src/wire/checkout-root.ts`) walks up from the folder, the folder included, and takes the nearest directory that holds a root marker of any system: `.git` (directory or worktree/submodule file), `.hg`, `.sl`, `.jj`, `.svn`, `.plastic`, `.bzr`, `.fslckout`/`_FOSSIL_`, `.pijul`, `_darcs`, and the weak markers `CVS/Root` (topmost of a run) and a Perforce `P4CONFIG` file (`.p4config` or the plain name in `P4CONFIG`); weak markers are not taken in a home folder or drive root, strong ones are, the walk never climbs above a home folder it started in, and it costs at most 15 `stat` calls a directory with no VCS program run. `sessionFolderFor` (`src/wire/session-folder.ts`) is the one function; the synchronous Sessions code reads the answers remembered by `SessionFolderResolver`, which is probed at activation, on `onDidChangeWorkspaceFolders`, on window focus, on a change of the setting, on registry changes and on the heartbeat, so an unresolved folder shows as itself and the tree repaints when an answer differs. The shown folder carries the opened roots as `openedPaths` and its tooltip says `Sessions run at the repository root <root>, not in the opened "<folder>".`; every consumer of a folder's path (New Session, the folder pickers, folder Resume and history, shells) therefore uses the root. Sessions already recorded keep their cwd: rows are filed by recorded cwd, and `folderOwnsCwd` also files sessions recorded in an opened subfolder under the root, unless another shown folder names that subfolder itself (a pinned one), so such sessions never disappear and never appear twice. The Settings, Models and Agents editors bind the folder sessions run in: an editor with no session to follow takes `editorProjectFolder` (`src/host/session-folder-setting.ts`) of the first workspace folder, the same resolver, so it writes `<root>/.omp`; one opened for a session takes that session's own cwd, and the scope control's Sessions rows and a folder chosen with the dialog are used as they are.

**Sessions state and actions.** Fresh positive ownership facts override cached availability for RPC, explicit native and legacy children. A verified rival-window holder has the `otherWindow` state (its row shows the holder's published status and that status's icon, plus a `⧉` file-decoration badge after it in the windows that do not run the session; the unread dot stays), with Stop available for the rival's recorded host. Open and a click switch to the owning window at once, with no notification or confirmation, and never launch; the claim is re-read first, so a released claim opens normally ([ADR-0056](decisions/0056-publish-window-state-in-a-leased-registry-and-switch-windows-by-request.md)). The state is an observation, not an authority; all launch paths still go through claim admission. It updates without a manual refresh when the other window opens or stops a session: `src/host/claim-watch.ts` watches the claims directory (`fs.watch`, non-recursive, not persistent, 150 ms debounce, spawns nothing), and changed claim names map only to affected rows for re-observation. A normal window close or crash releases no claim (children and claims outlive the extension host), so the row is rechecked on focus, visibility change or click. Processes uses the same displayed ownership state when it refreshes. Unowned records are Stopped/Draft. Native lifecycle reattaches rather than launching a process just to stop it, uses guarded SDK grace, and requires separate force consent when grace/control is unavailable. A missing transcript offers Forget. Stopped Chat paints history; stopped Terminal keeps an attachable broker screen or a recovery placeholder, never silently spawning.

Other-window rows show the status the holder publishes for that exact run (`sessionIncarnation`: owner generation plus host start and pid; Running when none is published or the run differs), with the unread mark derived from the shared reply markers like everywhere else; they are ordered, and their age shown, from the owner's published `lastActivityAt` (a current publication's `null` means no activity; only a row with no current publication falls back to this window's own reading of the same session file); the tooltip says `Open in another window: <label>`. Claims carry an optional display-only owning-window URI, separate from all ownership evidence: the saved local workspace file, or the single local folder when there is no workspace file. A click or Open on a row this window does not run, while a fresh claim read shows a live claim of another window, **switches** (`switchToSessionWindow`, `src/host/window-requests.ts`): it re-reads the claim, writes `window-requests/<holder id>.<id>.request` (naming the run and the editor binding (slot and generation) the holder published), arms a withdrawal timer for 5 s from that moment and calls `vscode.openFolder(uri, { forceNewWindow: true })`, which focuses the window that already has the folder or workspace and cannot replace this window when the owner has just closed (observed on VS Code 1.141.0). The owner serves a selection-only request (`selectHeldSessionTab`; one queue, four at a time, sixty-four waiting; rescanned on focus and heartbeat): only while the requester still waits and, read together after a fresh claim read, the claim names it as holder under its catalog's owner generation, its catalog records the requested run, it has a live host for the session and the editor binding is the slot and generation the request names (checked again after selecting; the serializer's restore of that tab is deferred until its revival, however late) does it select the editor it already has (a not-yet-revived editor is shown by VS Code, whose serializer path starts nothing while a live host exists); it never launches, stops, takes a claim, changes a mode or forwards a request, and answers in a correlated, bounded `.response` file that also says whether its window reported focus. A message appears only when switching is impossible or incomplete and says why: no usable URI (an unsaved multi-root window, whose record says `windowUri: null` authoritatively), two windows with one URI identity (`openFolder` cannot address a holder id), no answer within 5 s of posting, an owner without the editor or the run, or an owner that could not confirm its window came forward. Only sessions held through a claim are routed; a history-only editor in another window has no claim and is not.

**Window registry** (`src/host/window-registry.ts`, `src/wire/atomic-file.ts`, `src/host/directory-watch.ts`; [design](designs/2026-10-10-multi-window-sessions.md), [ADR-0056](decisions/0056-publish-window-state-in-a-leased-registry-and-switch-windows-by-request.md)). Each extension host writes `<global storage>/window-registry/<holder id>.window` (versioned, bounded: at most 512 KiB, 64 folders, 256 live cwds, 512 rows; rows are budgeted first, an entry that does not fit is dropped whole, never truncated; reads are bounded): pid, start time, lease stamp, window URI and label, the `file` folders open as opened, the cwds of its live sessions and `{status, incarnation, binding, lastActivityAt}` per session it runs. Writes are serialized and atomic (exclusive stage, rename with the Windows retry of claims), on change (250 ms debounce) and every 15 s; a normal close deletes the record and cancels an in-flight write. A record of another window counts while its stamp is within 60 s and not more than 5 s ahead of the reader's clock **and** its pid exists; unchanged expired files are swept once their modification time is a lease old, whatever the pid. A directory watch, focus, view visibility and the heartbeat re-read it, repainting only when the shown content (not freshness) changed; the heartbeat also re-probes the session folders for the complete live root set, rescans the request directory and, while the Sessions view is visible, runs the launcher's own recovery (the rows' files, the rows whose ownership no event announces and, every 3 minutes, a sweep of every row; see "Row ownership is observed again on events" below), while a hidden view does nothing there. A change in which windows are open, which the re-read finds, re-observes the rows the ended or new window's claims can decide. A window publishes its snapshot before repainting its own tree. Bounds, scan included: about 0.4 s for a published change; 15 s for a crash plus one observation (the re-read finds the window gone and re-observes the rows it held); 75 s for a hung host; 3 minutes plus one observation for a claim change whose notification the platform dropped, while the view stays visible; a hidden view converges when it is shown. `LauncherFolders` shows the union of every live window's folders (session folders applied by the reader unless the publishing window listed the opened folder in its optional `staysInFolder`, deduplicated by identity), the pinned folders and live-session folders, in window-start order; `ompDesk.showWorkspaceFolders` is `application` scoped, and `ompDesk.runSessionsAtRepositoryRoot` is `resource` scoped (ADR-0073 replaced ADR-0056's application-scope decision for it), which is why a window publishes the opened folders where it is off (`staysInFolder`, absent in an earlier build's record and read as none). The registry is advisory: it never admits or refuses a launch, and expiry never frees a claim. A session whose host outlived its window is listed again in an unpinned folder only when that folder is open somewhere or pinned. Which row shows another window as holder, and which window a click goes to, use a reading of the holder's process start time taken asynchronously by an in-process call ([ADR-0076](decisions/0076-call-win32-in-process-through-a-staged-koffi-module-instead-of-starting-powershell.md)), kept for 20 s and valid only for claims that existed when it was taken (`claimHolderSeemsAlive` in `session-claim.ts`); launches, stops and deletions still read it at their own verdict (`claimHolderMayBeAlive`). The same reading used to be a synchronous command per verdict on the extension host's event loop, which froze every window for seconds on every switch.

**Activation and the window's start time.** The claim holder's process start time (the start-time rule of [ADR-0035](decisions/0035-distinguish-a-recorded-process-from-a-later-occupant-of-its-pid.md)) is read in process on Windows since [ADR-0076](decisions/0076-call-win32-in-process-through-a-staged-koffi-module-instead-of-starting-powershell.md) (`readNativeCreationTime`, through the staged native module; `ps` on other platforms), so no command is spawned; the reading is still asynchronous because the module may not be loaded yet. `activate` starts it (`readProcessCreationTime`, `session-claim.ts`) and does not wait: the `SessionIndex` is built with the holder without a start time and with the pending reading (`claimHolderCreationTime`), and only `acquireClaim` waits for it (`claimHolderReady()`), so what a claim files is still verified while the Sessions view, the commands and the editors register at once; everything that only compares holder ids never waits. The installed Nerd Font list (a PowerShell command, loading System.Drawing, and still one) is read when the first terminal pane asks for its font, not at activation.

**Startup restore concurrency (2026-10-13).** `SessionIndex.restoreAll` restores the activation cohort with at most four rows in flight (`RestoreAllOptions.concurrency`, pool in `src/host/session-restore-trace.ts`), starting rows in cohort order. Each row is still one restore under its own tab-lifecycle gate; catalog writes stay on the index's single persist chain. The "one writable session per pass" rule is decided in cohort order among rows that name the same identity (`IdentityTurns`), so the first row of the cohort to reach the check keeps it. The report is folded in cohort order, not completion order, and a row whose restore throws becomes that row's `failed` outcome. Controller elections after the pass remain sequential: `bindEditorSlot` rolls back to a pre-await snapshot of the bindings, so concurrent elections could undo each other. Each row logs, at `info`, `window timing: restore tab …` with its admission/reconcile/attach|launch/persist durations (attach split into connect and trusted or probed identity), and the pass logs one activation → catalog adopted → restore started → restore finished timeline line.

**The restore pass does not wait for its saves, and its file calls are synchronous on a verified local volume (2026-10-15).** A row of `SessionIndex.restoreAll` ends with an ordinary save of the index (a catalog transaction under the lock other windows contend for); the row's outcome cannot change by then, so the row queues the save on the same persist chain, behind the earlier saves and ahead of the later ones, and returns (`#persist(trace)`). `RestoreReport.saved` settles to the failure that stands once those saves have run, and the startup pass reports a failure from there (`persistError` is only what the pass knew when it returned). Writes that decide something (the launch attempt's record, a conditional host record) are still awaited. The claims and the catalog use `localFs` (`src/wire/local-fs.ts`): synchronous steps below the directories `synchronousLocalPaths` accepted at activation, asynchronous calls elsewhere ([ADR-0076](decisions/0076-call-win32-in-process-through-a-staged-koffi-module-instead-of-starting-powershell.md), decision 13). The restore line names the pieces of `admission` (`session file`, `workspace directory`, `identity turn`, `claim read`, `claim holder alive`, `claim holder`, `claim take`), and the activation line has `catalog read`.

**The broker tree digest is recorded at build time (2026-10-15).** `esbuild.mjs` writes `out/pty-tree.json` (format `omp-runtime-tree/v2`, files ordered by code unit; `scripts/pty-tree-digest.mjs`) after the broker tree and `out/native/koffi.node` are in place, and `scripts/package-vsix.mjs` fails the package when the record inside the VSIX is not the digest of the tree the VSIX carries (`scripts/verify-vsix-tree-record.mjs`). In a packaged install (`extensionMode` Production) `PtyBrokerClient.attachIdentity` takes that digest as this build's (`ptyRecordedTreeDigest`, one bounded synchronous read) instead of hashing 2.9 MB at every activation, unless the runtime proof has finished (its own digest then takes precedence), and `verifyPackagedTree` hashes the installed tree once, 10 s after the startup restore, warns and switches to the hash when the record does not describe it (`unchecked` when the tree cannot be hashed). The digest only labels and decides whether a live broker of another build is adopted as this one's; no trust decision reads it. No record (a build from source, a watch build, a development host): the tree is hashed as before. [ADR-0076](decisions/0076-call-win32-in-process-through-a-staged-koffi-module-instead-of-starting-powershell.md), decision 14.

**The restore's own file reads are synchronous on a verified local volume (2026-10-15).** `readPtyRecord` (at most 64 KiB) and `ptyRecordedTreeDigest` (`out/pty-tree.json`, at most 4 KiB) go through `localFs`: below the broker state directory (in the activation verdict with the claims and the catalog) and below `<extension>/out` (its own verdict, whose target is the record file, asked before `attachIdentity` starts) the calls are synchronous steps; `useSynchronousLocalFs` refuses a drive root or share root as a root. The session-file `stat` of a restore row's admission (`fileExists` in `session-index.ts`) is its own `lstat`, taken only after `synchronousLocalPaths([file])` said yes on that call: every folder on the way, the session folder included, a plain directory of the drive root's volume; nothing about the folder is remembered. The verdict refuses UNC, extended and device paths by spelling and asks `GetVolumeInformationW` only of a `DRIVE_FIXED` root. A busy host loop delayed the same reads as four pool jobs and loop turns each (`record` 2.2 s per tab at a reload). The pipe `connect`, the `status` request and the attach `connect` stay asynchronous. [ADR-0076](decisions/0076-call-win32-in-process-through-a-staged-koffi-module-instead-of-starting-powershell.md), decision 15.

**A restored tab connects to its broker once, and the log says where the time went (2026-10-28, [ADR-0031](decisions/0031-reattach-a-surviving-managed-host-through-its-broker.md) and [ADR-0035](decisions/0035-distinguish-a-recorded-process-from-a-later-occupant-of-its-pid.md) amendments, [design](designs/2026-09-27-broker-aware-restart-recovery.md) section 2).** In the startup restore pass (`SessionIndex.restoreAll` with the trace, `automatic`), the reconcile of a `managed-rpc` row (`probeRpcHostOnce`, `src/host/rpc-reconcile.ts`) asks `OwnerReconcileRequest.keepConnection`; when its verdict is `attachable` it parks the authenticated connection in the pass's `ParkedRpcConnections` (`src/host/rpc-parked.ts`: one slot, one connection, keyed by the pin, 30 s limit, `release()` closes the rest and refuses later ones) instead of disconnecting. `attachRpcHost` (`src/host/rpc-launch.ts`) takes it for exactly that pin and `PtyBrokerClient.attachRpcRecord` (`reuse`) runs over it the chain a new connection runs after its handshake: the record read now must equal the record the connection was opened from, the shared gates are asked again, then a new `status` (5 s), the child identity wait and `confirmBrokerIdentity`; `concurrentPinProof` is keyed by the handle it started on. A closed socket, a changed record, a differing pin, a failed or slow status or a claim after the limit closes the connection and runs the ordinary attach. The extension wires the store per pass in `startRestore` of `src/wiring/catalog-adoption.ts` (`CatalogAdoption.restoreParking`, released when the pass ends and again right after `restoreAll` returns); Open, Resume and Delete admission never keep a connection. The row's timing line names `connect (kept)` instead of `connect` for such an attach, and splits a dial into `connect socket`, `connect hello` and `connect finish` (socket established, `hello-ok` received, last HMACs), and `connect loop late` and `status loop late` (how late a 10 ms timer of the same thread ran while that round trip waited, `src/wire/loop-gauge.ts`), which separate a busy host loop from a slow broker. The pass's timeline line ends with where the pass spent its time outside the rows: `before rows · rows · after rows · elections · publish · until finish` (`RestoreReport.rowsMs`, `describeRestorePhases` in `src/host/session-restore-trace.ts`).

**Launch in the folder's window** (`planLaunchRoute`, `routeLaunchToFolderWindow`, `serveLaunchRequest` in `src/wiring/window-routing.ts`; `src/host/window-requests.ts`, `src/host/unity-project.ts`; [design](designs/2026-10-10-launch-in-folder-window.md), [ADR-0057](decisions/0057-start-a-session-in-the-window-that-suits-its-folder.md)). An explicit launch (New Session from any entry point, Resume Session from a folder's history, opening a stopped or draft row, in an explicit view or not) starts in a window that has the launch's workspace open: the Sessions folder, or for a Unity project (a directory with `ProjectSettings/ProjectVersion.txt` and `Assets/`, found in the folder or two levels below it, one asked once per folder and remembered) the project folder as one of the window's own roots, while the session's working directory stays the Sessions folder. Here when this window has it; else the most recently focused live window that has it (the registry record's `focusedAt`); else, for an ordinary folder here, for a Unity project a new window opened on it. A hand-over is a `launch` request on the ADR-0056 request channel (workspace, plus `new-session`, `resume-folder` or `open-session`; 11 s lifetime, 10 s answer wait): the receiver checks that it has the workspace and knows the folder or session, starts the launch through the same function a click there runs, never routes it again and answers on admission. The requester never launches locally afterwards: a refusal or a timeout is a message (the request is withdrawn on timeout). A new window's launch is a pending file `pending-launches/<identity key>.<id>.pending` (65 s lifetime, 60 s wait) that the new window claims by exclusively creating a `.claimed` marker, so exactly one window starts it. `ompDesk.launchInFolderWindow` (default true) turns it off. A window whose installed package changed under it publishes `stale: true` in its registry record and is never chosen (a launch whose only candidate it was starts here, also for Unity); one that is asked anyway refuses first with the reason `stale-build`, and only that definite answer makes the requester start the launch itself.

**Reload All Windows** (`ompDesk.reloadAllWindows`: `reloadAllWindows`, `serveReloadRequest` in `src/wiring/window-routing.ts`; `postReloadRequest`, `awaitReloadTakenUp` in `src/host/window-requests.ts`; [ADR-0056](decisions/0056-publish-window-state-in-a-leased-registry-and-switch-windows-by-request.md) amendment). The command (Command Palette, Sessions title overflow, no confirmation) re-reads the window registry, writes a `kind: "reload"` request, which carries no argument, to every other live window's `window-requests/<holder id>.<id>.request` and waits for all of them at once for at most 2 s (`RELOAD_ACCEPT_TIMEOUT_MS`), then runs `workbench.action.reloadWindow` here whatever each window did; a window that is not answering never holds this one back. A receiver takes the request up like a stop (one atomic rename to `.accepted`, so a requester's withdrawal and the take-up cannot both win), writes its response, deletes the accepted file and only then runs its own reload (`ServeOutcome.afterAnswer`), so the extension host that starts afterwards finds nothing to act on and cannot reload in a loop; a request older than 6 s (`RELOAD_REQUEST_TTL_MS`) is deleted unacted, and the requester withdraws one nobody took up when its wait ends. The requester tells the outcomes apart in the log: `.accepted` or `.response` present (the window is reloading), the request deleted with neither (a build that predates the kind: its parser rejects the unknown kind and deletes the file, so that window is not reloaded and must be reloaded by hand), or still present at the bound (withdrawn). A second invocation while one runs joins it.

**Select and launch requests are taken up, waited for by progress and addressed by the holder's published run (2026-10-21, [ADR-0056](decisions/0056-publish-window-state-in-a-leased-registry-and-switch-windows-by-request.md) amendment).** `startRequestWatch` (`src/host/window-requests.ts`) takes every request kind but `models` up on receipt by atomic rename to `.accepted` and rewrites that file with progress (`ServeContext.progress`: after the claim read, after the reveal for a select, after validation for a launch). `awaitSelectAnswer` and `awaitLaunchAnswer` (the state machine of `awaitStopAnswer`) wait with an accept bound (select 5 s, launch 10 s, then the request is withdrawn: `timeout`), a progress bound (15 s, 30 s; each change of the progress file restarts it) and a total bound (30 s, 60 s); a taken-up request that went silent ends as `silent` ("not known whether…"), never as "did not respond". A taken-up request cannot be withdrawn, so `postSwitchRequest` and `postLaunchRequest` write an optional `answerBy` and the receiver's `stillWanted` is `now <= answerBy` (an older requester's request: `createdAt` plus the old bound). `deliverRequest` (`src/wiring/window-routing.ts`) withdraws at the accept bound and on every path that stops waiting without an answer. A `select` and a `handover` request name the run and binding of the holder's published `PublishedRow` when it has one, else the run of this window's catalog after `refreshCatalog` (`CatalogAdoption.adopt`); a difference is logged. The 60 s registry lease and 15 s heartbeat are unchanged (decision recorded in the ADR: a holder that dropped out is found through the pid-based claim liveness, the claim's window URI and the catalog). **Mixed builds:** an older holder serves a request in place and deletes it, which a new requester reads as an answer; an older requester's request carries no `answerBy` and is counted from `createdAt`.

**Move to This Window** (`ompDesk.moveSessionHere`: `moveSessionToThisWindow`, `serveHandoverRequest` in `src/wiring/window-routing.ts`, `handOverSession` in `src/extension.ts`; `postHandoverRequest`, `awaitHandoverAnswer` in `src/host/window-requests.ts`; `SessionRuntime.detach` in `src/runtime/`, `SessionIndex.detachLiveClaim`; [design](designs/2026-10-20-move-session-to-this-window.md), [ADR-0080](decisions/0080-hand-over-a-live-session-through-a-taken-up-request-that-detaches-the-holder-without-stopping-it.md)). A live session changes windows without the process being stopped: the requester re-reads the claim and the holder's published row (an older holder, or a row starting, stopping or restoring, is told apart before anything is posted; a working or questioning session is confirmed once), writes a taken-up `kind: "handover"` request (`tabId`, `incarnation`, `binding`) addressed to the claim holder and waits with the stop waiter's classification (12 s request lifetime, 8 s take-up wait, 30 s total), carrying no focus plan, so no window is brought forward. The holder refuses `stale-build` and an asker that is not another live registry window, then `SessionRuntime.detach` takes the tab's gate and re-reads the exact run under it (`not-holder`, `terminal`, `busy`), drops the conversation, ends the runtime record, broker watch and exit handling, closes the control channel keeping its credentials, and `detachLiveClaim` releases the claim without writing a run intent, a recorded host or `releasedAt`; the window then forgets its outcome, repaints and names the asker in a notification, while the editor stays as a read-only history with a note. On `ok` the requester runs the ordinary open (`openSession(… "opened", noRouting, keepLiveView)`), whose claim and attach proofs alone decide ownership, and the adopter retries the broker input claim a bounded number of times. The surfaces are the Sessions row menu (every row open in another window, no window identity needed), a `Move to This Window` action on the claim-conflict notice, and **OMP Desk: Move Session to This Window** (`ompDesk.moveSessionHere.palette`, the same handler under the palette's title).

**Outside OMP writers.** A plain `omp` (typically in a terminal) that writes a session holds OMP's session ownership lease, a named Windows mutex, until it exits; the extension only *opens and closes* it read-only ([ADR-0046](decisions/0046-probe-omp-session-lease-for-external-writers.md), [design](designs/2026-10-07-external-omp-session-lease.md)). `src/host/omp-session-lease.ts` derives the name (`Global\omp-file-lock-` plus two XXH64 values of the lock path `<home>/<PI_CONFIG_DIR or .omp>/run/session-owners/<session id>.lock`, verified against OMP 18.6.3) and `ExternalLeaseObserver` serves all rows with one in-process pass of `nativeLeaseProbe` (`OpenMutexW(SYNCHRONIZE)` per name through the staged `koffi.node`, `src/wire/win32-native.ts`, [ADR-0076](decisions/0076-call-win32-in-process-through-a-staged-koffi-module-instead-of-starting-powershell.md); cached 2 s; a `fresh` request waits for a newer pass; an access-denied answer and `ERROR_INVALID_HANDLE` read as held and absent; an unavailable module answers unknown with the failure backoff). `launcherOwnershipFacts` adds `externalOmp` only when no claim of another window, no live claim of this window and no verified broker writer can be the holder (their children hold leases too; a child none of these can verify is reported as external); `sessionItemState` shows **Open in another OMP process** (`externalOmp`, `terminal` icon) after `otherWindow`, `starting` and `restoring`. A click or Open on such a row re-probes and asks; every explicit attempt that can start a writer (`openTabOnce`: verb *resumed*, or a row whose run intent is not stopped) asks the same question through `confirmOpenAnyway`, and `openSession` reports whether it proceeded so Add to Session adds nothing after Cancel; history views of stopped rows never ask. Known limitation: a restored editor and the activation pass's automatic relaunch do not consult the lease. A failed probe, an unknown derivation or an unreadable header leaves rows as before and logs. The lease exists only after the other process has written the session.

**Session row context menu.** The menu is the `when` clauses in `package.json` evaluated over the row's `contextValue`, `ompSession.<forgettable|held>.<state>.<resumable|live-only>.<deletable|file-kept>.<materialized|fileless>.view-<chat|terminal|none>` (built in `src/views/session-tree.ts`; the `view-*` segment is the view this window's open editor shows, from `SessionLauncherFacts.viewMode`). It follows what each command does: **Open in Chat/Terminal** (`openSessionInMode`, verb *open*) launches, so only stopped and draft rows get it, while a live row's **Show in Chat/Terminal** (`ompDesk.showSessionInChat/Terminal`, the same handler with verb *show*, hidden from the palette) reveals the existing editor, or reopens it when only the tab was closed, and is offered only for the view the open editor is not already showing (both when no editor is open or its view is unknown). `background` ("Waiting for subagents") is live everywhere `working` is. A folder node's `contextValue` is `ompWorkspaceFolder.[shells.]<collapsed|expanded>.<pinned|unpinned>`: Pin Folder is offered on unpinned folders (also inline) and Unpin Folder on pinned ones.

Sessions title navigation contains Open Stats, the layout button, Stop Orphaned and Idle and Kill All; New Session and Resume Session are rows of the list, not title buttons (2026-10-11); default-mode choices stay in overflow. An empty folder has a folder-scoped launch placeholder. Stopped and draft rows expose inline Open as well as their mode-specific menu actions, while existing single-click history and second-click resume behavior remains.

When a successful default-profile `omp models --json` read reports no available models, Sessions prepends one stable account-icon action, **Log in to a model provider to start**, which runs `ompDesk.loginProvider`. The window caches this observation and refreshes asynchronously on activation, when a provider login or logout completes, when a fallback **omp login** terminal closes and when a Chat reports changed model availability. An unresolved OMP adds no row; activation never waits for discovery. The row is independent of folder/session membership and disappears once models are reported.

| Row state | Menu (groups: open/show · manage · remove) |
| --- | --- |
| stopped | Open in Chat · Open in Terminal · Rename · Forget · Delete |
| draft | Open in Chat · Open in Terminal · Rename · Forget (Delete only once it has a file and the rule allows) |
| running, working, background, question, unread, waiting | Show in Terminal and/or Show in Chat · Rename · Reload (only with a durable file) · Close · Delete (stops the verified writer before removing a deletable row) |
| otherWindow (another live window holds the claim; shown with the holder's published status) | Stop (the rival's recorded host) · Switch to Window (the same silent switch a click does) · Move to This Window (takes the live session here without stopping it) |
| externalOmp (a plain `omp` outside this extension holds the session's OMP lease) | Open in Chat · Open in Terminal (each asks Open Anyway / Cancel first) |
| blocked, transcript missing | Forget |
| restoring, starting, stopping, checking | none; the transition owns the row, and clicking it still reveals its editor |

Rename of a live row not yet attached in this window reattaches its verified writer first (as Reload does) and renames through it. Close is the live-session stop: it confirms, stops the exact process and keeps the row as Stopped. `src/views/session-tree.test.ts` evaluates the shipped clauses against every state.

Delete names the session and explains in two short sentences that history and artifacts are removed irreversibly. Exact target paths and inspection notes go to the log, not the modal. The Delete button retains writer-stop, exact-file, ownership and frozen-target checks; Cancel returns without a redundant notification.

**Immediate click feedback** (`src/host/pending-operations.ts`, `pending-display.ts`, `pending-steps.ts`, `src/shared/pending-operation.ts`, `PendingEntry` helper `pendingEntry` in `extension.ts`; [design](designs/2026-10-15-immediate-click-feedback.md), [ADR-0046](decisions/0046-probe-omp-session-lease-for-external-writers.md) amendment). A window-local `PendingOperations` registry records the actions the user asked for that are still being processed: `begin(key, kind, {origin, requestId, step})` returns a handle, `step` names the phase (the launch laps feed it through `observeLaunchSteps`, entry points name `ownership`, `confirm`, `checking`, `working`, `saving`), `end` is idempotent, and a 60 s mark only changes the wording. Keys are the tab id for a session action, `folder:<id>` for a folder-level one, `window` for Export HTML and Reload All Windows, `<inventory kind>:<row name>` for a checkbox. The registry is display only: nothing decides from it, and `openingTabs`, the index's duplicate guard, `stoppingTabs`, ownership and run intent stay the authorities. `PendingDisplay` projects it to the Sessions tree (`SessionTreeProvider.setPending`: row text, icon, menus, the row held in its click-time group; the view progress bar), to the status bar for command-origin operations with no surface of their own (after a short grace), and to every Chat page of the tab (`omp:chat-operation`, rendered by the composer and the banner; the page also acknowledges Resume itself in the frame of the click and drops the state on a host answer, a route loss or 15 s of silence). Open and Resume of a row this window does not run open the editor before the owned-writer check, the Stop-and-Open question and the fresh lease probe; a Cancel closes an editor the attempt created unless the row is stopped (`TabState.editorOfAttempt`, `openDeclined`). A New Session for a folder whose first one is still starting is ignored and reveals the starting editor (`newSessionsInFlight`). Close, Kill, Delete, Forget, Rename, a Chat/Terminal switch (`view-switch`), Export HTML, Reload All Windows and the inventory switch (`InventoryController.toggle`, one switch per row at a time) are wrapped the same way; their checks before a confirmation read `checking`.

**Sessions panel.** Persisted rows publish immediately as **Checking** while ownership/title enrichment proceeds, rather than waiting for the complete restore scan. Only current-revision publication updates retained row/folder identities; superseded reads cannot mutate the visible projection. Positive local/authenticated ownership remains distinct from activity. Rows distinguish **Working**, **Waiting for subagents** (including asynchronous jobs and undelivered results), **Needs your answer** only for structured dialogs, and **Idle** only at proven idle. Fresh non-running sessions also use Idle; stopped saved sessions remain Stopped. **Unread reply** is supplementary, not proof of idle.
