Disabling capture is durable; history deletion requires positive proof that each namespace's exact producer stopped, so an unknown producer causes refusal rather than unsafe erasure. Automatic file Undo/Redo and checkpoint restoration remain absent until both attribution and filesystem exclusion gates in [ADR-0007](decisions/0007-native-file-evidence-and-guarded-restore.md) pass. Native hooks and storage have targeted tests and a headless integration smoke; in the real window, consent, the admission-to-post-result diff and the deletion refusal were observed, while the remaining observation surfaces stay unexercised.

**Owning VS Code instance exit.** For a folder shell, the PTY broker's staged Windows helper attests the extension host's direct parent against an owning main-process generation, retaining a kernel handle to every admitted main. Host-role recognition requires exact legacy extensionHost argv, or modern utility/NodeService argv plus the independently read extensionHost role and extensionHostProcess entrypoint environment pair set by VS Code's utility factory. Generic NodeService utilities, incidental command-line substrings and unreadable or conflicting markers fail closed; no unrelated environment value is materialized or reported. Parent, executable-image, older-main and retained-generation checks remain mandatory. The broker starts finite grace only after all admitted handles signal; a verified new owner cancels pending grace and an unattestable authenticated owner disarms automatic cleanup. Grace authorizes one identity-safe shell stop *attempt*, still retaining `unknown`/`remaining` recovery, and never auto-stops managed OMP. An unverified owning instance leaves its shell alive for manual Close rather than guessing from frontend or extension-host loss ([ADR-0030](decisions/0030-watch-the-verified-owning-vscode-process-for-shell-grace.md)). Installed isolated acceptance now proves real modern owner-monitor arming with exact staged-helper and owning-main kernel-generation evidence. Actual last-window grace expiry and cross-window concurrency remain unexercised; read-only command-line topology alone proves neither.

**Folder shell (kept).** A staged broker runtime is proved before any session runs from it ([ADR-0032](decisions/0032-prove-the-pty-runtime-with-a-console-child.md)): the entry is started once with `--self-check`, must load the native addon, run a real console child and — since the rpc cutover — a pipe child, whose per-run markers are read back together with the backend's exit report; the exit code is not the proof. Since 2026-10-12 a run that only reached its 15-second deadline with nothing observed to be wrong is *inconclusive* (entry exit 8): `PtyRuntimeProof` (`src/host/pty-runtime-proof.ts`) admits the launch unproven without caching it, re-runs the check in the background 20 s later with a 90-second deadline per phase, keeps a proof, and holds a refusal for 60 s; an observed fault (including any pipe-child write error or inexact echo) refuses at once (exit 7), and the host accepts lateness only from a consistent exit-8 `inconclusive` answer to a default-deadline run, or a kill of an entry that printed nothing. See [ADR-0032](decisions/0032-prove-the-pty-runtime-with-a-console-child.md), 2026-10-12 amendment. Folder shells use separate `folder-shell` broker slots with post-close recovery; terminal input/resize/screen snapshots traverse a separately authorized document bridge and a passive editor cannot claim PTY input. A document-local nonce guard permits dynamically created xterm styles under strict CSP ([ADR-0028](decisions/0028-stamp-document-nonce-on-renderer-created-styles.md)). The prebuilt Windows ConPTY addon cannot prove whole-tree termination: stops report `unknown` or observed `remaining`, never a false `verified:true` ([ADR-0029](decisions/0029-report-uncontained-pty-tree-stop-as-unknown.md)). The chat no longer mounts a terminal pane.
After authenticated folder-shell attach, `openShellEditor` starts its terminal pipeline for an existing restored panel as well as a new panel. A folder shell's missing-pipeline fallback describes a detached folder terminal, never a missing OMP chat host.
The first proof in a window is logged with its times (`PTY runtime proved itself: self-check N ms, ready in M ms`: the entry's own run, and the whole `ensurePtyRuntime` including listing, staging and verifying the tree), or as `PTY runtime proof reused from <time>: ready in N ms` when a stored proof was used; a provisional admission and the repeated check log theirs. Measured on the development machine 2026-10-12: self-check about 4.3 s, the whole proof about 8.6 s, of which `stageRuntimeTree` alone 2.2–3.3 s even for an already staged tree (its two `ensureRuntimeAccess` passes each run the private-storage ACL and owner scripts under PowerShell; hashing is about 30 ms and the byte verification 17 ms). The ADR-0032 amendment of 2026-10-13 shares the self-check across windows (below); the staging passes are not shared.

**A Chat start does not wait for the full runtime proof (2026-10-23, [design](designs/2026-10-23-chat-start-does-not-wait-for-the-full-pty-proof.md)).** Every broker start is gated inside `PtyBrokerClient.startBrokerOn` by the proof of the runtime it starts on: a Chat on the session's `bun.exe` by the pipe-only proof its Bun plan makes (ADR-0074), a Chat that falls back to `Code.exe`, a terminal, a folder shell and Stats by the full proof. The launcher used to wait for the full proof up front for every launch, which after an extension update (a new tree digest, so no stored proof; the console-child self-check then outlives its 15 s deadline on the development machine, 36–78 s until admission) held a Chat start for the rest of a proof the Chat never uses. `LocalLauncher` now asks `chatBrokerClient` for a Chat (`PtyReadinessGate.chatLaunchClient()`: the attach client when it can answer, else the full gate, after the ADR-0068 settings barrier) and `brokerClient` for a terminal. In the start timeline the `runtime proof` lap is the wait for the client the launch kind needs: a few milliseconds for a Chat, the full proof for a terminal; for a Chat that falls back to `Code.exe` the wait for the full proof is part of the broker's `runtime check` phase instead. A Chat start on a build seen for the first time overlaps the Bun plan's pipe-only self-check with the full one; so that the overlap alone does not cost the Bun runtime, `proveBunRuntime` (`src/host/pty-bun-proof.ts`) does not act on a pipe-only answer that was inconclusive while the window's first full probe was running: it waits for that probe and checks once more, and the second answer is judged as before (ADR-0074 amendment 2026-10-23). A Chat that still falls back after the second check is not held off Bun for ten minutes, because that answer may itself have been contended; the time the plan spent is the `bun attempt` phase. The `Chat broker runtime:` line says which runtime a start got. The background start of the full proof after the startup restore is unchanged.

**One runtime check for all windows, and the Bun check warmed at activation (2026-10-28, [design](designs/2026-10-28-one-pty-check-for-all-windows.md), ADR-0032 and ADR-0074 amendments of the day).** `ensurePtyRuntime` runs the self-check of one proof key in one window at a time (`src/host/pty-proof-flight.ts`). After the staged tree is verified and the stored proof (`runtime/pty-proof/<key hash>.json`) is missing, the window takes the lock `runtime/pty-flight/<key hash>.lock` (created whole by linking a temporary file; it holds the holder's process id and the longest time its attempt can last) and runs the check, storing a proven answer before it lets go; a window that finds the lock held polls the stored proof every 250 ms and reuses it (`reusedProof`, `flight.via: "stored"`; the log line says it waited). A lock whose process is gone, whose time is past or whose content is corrupt is taken over after a second read; a lock that cannot be read for a moment is waited for. The wait is bounded by `2 × deadline + 5 s`; past it (or when the directory cannot be used) the window runs its own check without the lock. A check that ends inconclusive or broken stores no proof but leaves a short outcome beside the lock, which only a window that waited for that very holder, with a deadline no longer than the holder's, takes as its own answer (`flight.via: "shared"`: a provisional admission or a refusal); a window that starts later runs its own check. The key covers the tree digest, the runtime version, the executable file (path, size, time), the OS build and the pipe-only flag, so windows with another key never wait for each other. At the end of `activate`, `startChatRuntimeWarmup` (`src/host/chat-runtime-warmup.ts`) hands the Bun plan's pipe-only check to a later turn of the event loop: it resolves the OMP launch through the window's one shared resolution (`sharedOmpBinary`) and `PtyBrokerClient.warmChatRuntime` runs the same `proveBunRuntime` a Chat launch runs, for a bun-shim OMP on Windows x64 with the setting `auto`; it reads no trust, records no hold and starts no broker, and leaves the ordinary proof in the process and in the store (`Bun runtime pre-check ahead of the first Chat: …` in the log). A launch that starts while it runs waits on the same lock instead of repeating the check.

**Helper processes are scheduled (2026-10-28, [design](designs/2026-10-28-helper-process-scheduler.md), [ADR-0084](decisions/0084-run-helper-processes-through-one-per-window-scheduler-with-cross-window-slots.md), accepted after one independent review).** The short-lived OMP processes a window starts for what a Chat shows beside the conversation (provider usage `omp usage --json --redact --provider …`, the default profile's `omp models --json`, `omp config get/list` settings reads, the inventory and served-model Bun workers, the `--version` probes of other installs) go through one `HelperScheduler` per window (`src/host/helper-scheduler.ts`, created in `activate`, disposed with the window). Policy: **`visible`** work (the usage of the active tab or of any visible Chat panel and any forced refresh, a settings question a page asked, the served-model verdicts a page asked, every inventory read and switch) is bound only by the window's limit of 2 helpers at once and goes ahead of background work; **`background`** work (the usage of tabs with no visible panel, the default catalogue read, other installs' versions) starts only after `startupRestoreOver` plus a 2 s settle delay, runs at most 1 at a time and takes one of 2 cross-window slots first (`src/host/helper-slots.ts`: the PTY slot lock Stats' launch uses, names `omp-helper:0/1`, at most 30 s wait plus a poll, then it runs anyway; the lock takes a dead holder over only when no live process has its process id, so a reused id can keep a slot busy until the wait ends). A job with a `key` that is queued or running is shared in the window (usage: scope and provider; `models:default`); a queued background job a visible caller needs is promoted (`run` with the same key, or `promote(key)`, which the usage read calls for a tab in front because `ProviderUsageCache` joins a pending read without calling the scheduler). `dispose` rejects queued work (`HelperCancelledError`), aborts running work (`runOmpCli` and `runSettingsWorker` take the `AbortSignal` and end their process) and starts nothing afterwards; call sites do not log a cancellation as a failure. The default catalogue read has two reasons: `credentials` (sign-in, sign-out, login terminal close, another window's announcement, a session whose models answer changed) repeats a read that is running; `discovery` (activation, a session's first look when the catalogue was never read) joins it, where each tab's first look used to ask for a read of its own. Results are not shared across windows: `omp usage` runs the folder's extension discovery and its reports hold account labels, and the catalogue boolean is read in the first workspace folder. Not covered: explicit user actions (Stats, provider login, the settings editors), executable resolution (`resolveOmpBinary`'s `omp --version` for a standalone install, shared only while in flight) and the restore's own processes (`rpc-launch`, the launcher capability probe, the PTY proof). Log: a debug line per helper whose run ended (`helper run: <label> (<class>) waited N ms …, ran M ms`, with the error kind for a failed one) and one info line when the window's helpers drain after the gate (`helper runs: N started since activation (a visible, b background), at most P at once, first at +X ms, last at +Y ms, longest wait Z ms …`; P counts runs in progress, not runs waiting for a slot). Model of a four-window reload with six tabs each (`helper-startup.test.ts`, virtual clock, not a measurement): before 36 helpers, peak 20 at once; after 28 helpers, peak 4, no background helper before its window's restore ended.

**Re-adoption identity (2026-10-13, [ADR-0035](decisions/0035-distinguish-a-recorded-process-from-a-later-occupant-of-its-pid.md) amendment, accepted after independent architecture review).** A broker of the current build attests in its authenticated status that it is new enough to be believed about itself (`brokerSelfRead`, a version marker: every broker has always MAC'd its own creation time into `hello-ok`) and whether it still holds its child's process handle (`childHandleLive`, true only for a `managed-rpc` pipe child; false for a pseudo-console child). The one proof built on it (`connectRecord`, `provedBrokerPin`) serves re-adoption (`attachRpcHost`, `attachNativeHost`), the ownership-verdict probes (`probeRpcHost`, `probeBrokerSlotForRunningChild`) and the stop's pre-dispatch pin proof (`stopBrokerOwnedHost`). It checks the handshake (whose MAC covers the broker's pid and creation time) and the status against the record and the row's pin and takes no kernel reading; a malformed attestation counts as absent. A broker without the attestation, a child whose handle is no longer held, and every pseudo-console child, are read through the kernel as before, once per proof: each reading is one synchronous in-process call through the staged native module (`queryPtyProcessIdentity`, [ADR-0076](decisions/0076-call-win32-in-process-through-a-staged-koffi-module-instead-of-starting-powershell.md)) that shares and keeps nothing, so the attach that follows a reconcile reads again. Readings per re-adopted row (reconcile plus attach): `managed-rpc` with an attesting broker 0 (was 4), an older broker 4, a native Terminal row 2 (was 4). The first attach of a window stages nothing for a reading (`attachIdentity()` only hashes the packaged tree, which carries the native module the readings use), and the broker state-directory access verification, which every attach still waits for, starts at activation. A proven PTY runtime self-check is stored under `runtime/pty-proof/` (`src/host/pty-runtime-proof-store.ts`; key: tree digest, runtime version, executable, platform, OS build number, so a cumulative update does not invalidate it; believed for 24 h; [ADR-0032](decisions/0032-prove-the-pty-runtime-with-a-console-child.md) amendment) and reused by later windows; the check passes only when the staged native module loads in the runtime under test and reads one creation time, a provisional admission is never stored, and a launch that fails before a broker exists while running on a reused proof deletes that proof and clears the in-process proofs, so the next launch re-runs the self-check. Staging and its access verification still run each time. Since ADR-0076 the PowerShell identity probe helper is gone; the dated paragraphs below describe the PowerShell readings of their day.

**Restore waits on PowerShell starts; each one is logged (2026-10-13).** Reload Window restores a session after the broker state-directory access verification (every attach waits for it) and, for a broker that does not attest itself, after the identity probe helper is staged (`stageRuntimeTree`: two access passes when it writes, one when the tree is already staged, see the next paragraph) and the broker's and the child's kernel creation times are read. All of that is PowerShell, and a start costs 0.3–0.6 s idle and 3.5–7 s on a loaded machine. Three changes shorten it without relaxing a check: (1) a private-storage pass is one process instead of four (above), so the state-directory gate is one start and a staging is at most two instead of eight; measured on an idle machine against a scratch store with a counting `spawn` wrapper: state-directory pass 4 starts / 1.5–1.8 s → 1 / 0.4–0.5 s, probe-helper staging of a staged tree 8 / 2.8–3.3 s → 2 / 1.0 s (one start since the next paragraph's change). (2) The two independent readings of one proof run together: reconcile's `unreachable`/`no-record` absence proof reads the child and the broker concurrently, and an attach or a reconcile probe starts the child's pin proof (`concurrentPinProof`, or `provedBrokerPin` in the probe) as soon as the broker's status is known, through the `whileConfirming` hook of `attachRpcRecord`/`attachRecord`, while the broker's own identity is confirmed; two readings 0.63–0.76 s one after the other, 0.30–0.39 s together. Each reading is still taken inside its own proof and compared with the recorded creation time, nothing is kept between proofs (ADR-0035 §3), and either failing refuses (the broker's reason first). (3) Every PowerShell or `icacls` start of the private-storage code, the identity probe helper and the session-lease probe is one line in the OMP Desk output, `tool spawn: <tool> <purpose> pid <pid> <ms> ms <outcome>` (`src/wire/tool-spawn-trace.ts`; the purpose is a fixed phrase such as `broker state security read (9 paths)`, `process probe generation` or `session lease probe (3 names)`, never a path, argument or output; the window's own start-time reading already logs `process start time read`). The lines attribute each second of a restore to a purpose.

**Display reads wait for the restore; a staging that writes nothing is one pass; the state check starts first (2026-10-13, second pass).** A real Reload of three windows after the first pass still took 26.7–45.7 s per window to restore, with 36–41 PowerShell starts per window in the first minute: 22–26 `process probe generation` (the restore's own readings of the rows whose broker does not attest, plus readings that only paint Sessions: the Processes reader's creation-time and child reads of every live broker of the profile, and the ownership observation of every row this window does not run, whose verified-writer check reconciles it), 7–8 `runtime tree security read` (four stagings of two passes each: the host-control assets at activation, the identity probe helper, the session-lease helper and the PTY runtime proof) and 4–7 `session lease probe` (repeated 5 s timeouts). The split of the generation reads by caller is read from the code, not measured. Three changes follow, none of which relaxes a check. (1) **Display reads wait.** A row's ownership observation (its verified-writer reconcile and the session-lease probe, which stages the lease helper) and the Processes reader (its poll, the read when the view is shown, the read after a launcher refresh) wait until the window's startup restore is over (`startupRestoreOver()`; at most 120 s, so a pass that never settles cannot leave a row undetermined). Meanwhile a row the restore covers reads Restoring and another row reads Checking. An observation of a row another live window holds is not deferred (it needs no probe), and no explicit action waits: a click or an open (`fresh` reads), Stop, Kill All, Refresh and the Processes commands read at once. (2) **A staging that writes nothing verifies once** ([ADR-0012](decisions/0012-run-child-process-entries-from-staged-copies.md) 2026-10-13 amendment): `stageRuntimeAssets` and `stageRuntimeTree` read first, writing nothing, whether every copy already holds its digest, and then run one verification pass over the whole final layout and hash the copies again, instead of the pass over the runtime directory, the writes and the pass over the layout (a copy that no longer holds after the pass takes the writing flow); a staging that writes still establishes the rules before its first byte. Four such stagings are four PowerShell starts per window instead of eight, and a steady probe-helper staging measured 0.93–1.04 s → 0.41–0.44 s on an idle machine. (3) **The state-directory check starts first.** `activate` creates the broker client and starts `stateAccess()` before the profile catalog is read, the index is built or any registry exists, beside the window's own start-time read, instead of after them.

**The catalog is read without returning to the event loop, and the committing store no longer re-reads what it just wrote (2026-10-13, [ADR-0034](decisions/0034-store-sessions-in-one-profile-catalog.md) amendment).** A Reload of three windows at once once left a window restoring for 62.8 s with two PowerShell starts: its `intent` and `persist` steps (catalog commits) took 7–19 s and a slot binding was refused as "could not be recorded durably", while the windows' event loops stalled for seconds. On Windows a rename over the catalog fails while any process has it open, and a committing window holds the catalog lock while it retries (about 2.1 s) against a 4 s lock bound for everyone else. `readCatalogDocument` therefore reads with `readFileSync` (open, read and close without returning to the loop; an asynchronous read stayed open across the loop turns of a stalled window and kept a peer's rename failing for the whole stall; a stall inside the one synchronous call still holds the file and blocks this window's thread); the committing store applies the document it wrote (`CatalogCommitResult.document`, parsed from the committed text) instead of reading it back (the adoption the commit triggers still reads once); `startCatalogWatch` reports only `catalog.json` itself, not a commit's staged `catalog.json.<pid>.<uuid>.tmp`; and the catalog adoption (`CatalogAdoption`, `src/wiring/catalog-adoption.ts`) runs one adoption at a time through `CoalescedRun` (`src/host/coalesced-run.ts`), folding the triggers that arrive meanwhile (own commits, the watcher, focus, visibility, explicit refresh) into one follow-up that starts after them, so another window's commit is adopted when the adoption in flight ends. Measured with three processes on a copy of the real catalog, one of them stalling its loop right after starting each read (decisions 1 to 3 only; the coalescing is unmeasured): the other two committed with a median of 2.2–2.4 s and 13–18 of 25 commits refused before, 50–62 ms and none after. That the incident was this mechanism is an inference; a lock holder whose own loop stalls still delays the others. The session lease probe's failure backoff now doubles (15 s, 30 s, … 5 min) while probes keep failing and starts over after one that reports (`leaseFailureBackoffMs`), instead of one doomed 5 s PowerShell start every ~30 s per window.

**Contention for the catalog is saved again, not shown (2026-10-15, [ADR-0034](decisions/0034-store-sessions-in-one-profile-catalog.md) amendment).** A write refused with `CatalogError` `busy` (another window held the lock for 4 s, expected when several windows start together) is a `warn` line with its cause, never a notification. The session index and the folder registry save their whole in-memory state again after 1 s, doubling to 30 s and then every 30 s while the lock is held (`src/host/persist-retry.ts`), and a write that lands ends the retry; contention that lasts two minutes is reported once as a fault. Any other save failure is logged every time and shown once per window per text (`src/host/persist-report.ts`). `setEditorMode` keeps the mode on contention and names the cause when it refuses.

**Native Models and Agents settings** (`src/host/omp-settings-editor.ts`, `src/wire/omp-settings-core.ts`, `src/wire/omp-settings-worker.ts`, `src/host/omp-settings-apply.ts`, `settings-pickers.ts`, `settings-pick-items.ts`, `settings-pick-requests.ts`, `src/omp/settings-worker.ts`, `src/webview/settings/`; [design](designs/2026-10-08-native-models-and-agents-settings.md), [ADR-0048](decisions/0048-write-omp-settings-directly-under-native-file-locks.md)). Profile/scope-bound native webviews are independent of conversation slots and claims. A scope is global or one project folder: the commands open the global scope, whose worker runs from a drive root with no project layer, and an in-editor scope control rebinds the editor to a Sessions folder; Chat `/models` binds the Chat's folder and `/agents` the global scope. Every model value is chosen in a host QuickPick built from the editor's current snapshot (roles used first, then provider groups, purpose-specific role, `@role`, agent-default and `provider/*` rows) and returned to the page as a typed `settings:picked` answer; role assignments save at once with a thinking step, list values are page drafts. Bare `/model`/`/models` and `/agents` open them before prompt/steer/follow-up dispatch; model selector arguments retain RPC semantics. A content-addressed verified Bun worker imports the selected installed source for settings, model resolution and agent discovery. Opening uses read-only settings and SQLite snapshots, with editor-private catalogue initialization and no provider refresh, extension loading or credential writes. The 2026-10-13 revision ([design](designs/2026-10-13-models-agents-master-detail-and-resolution.md), [ADR-0072](decisions/0072-resolve-roles-and-agents-in-the-worker-with-omps-own-functions-and-write-agent-overrides-to-either-layer.md)): the worker (`src/omp/resolution.ts`) computes each role's and agent's effective model, thinking level, source, decision trace and "if you reset" outcome with OMP's own resolution functions (`resolveModelRoleValue`, `resolveRoleChain` for the roles OMP's tools resolve as a chain, `resolveAgentModelPatterns`; what an unset role follows is probed with sentinel selectors and pinned by a live test) and the page (`src/webview/settings/{common,roles,agents,health}.tsx`) only renders it. Launch-time `PI_*_MODEL` role overrides apply to a child `Settings.overlay()` that only the resolution reads. Project writes also carry agent model, prewalk and advisor overrides (reset removes the key), under ADR-0071's home-folder and ancestor-context refusals. Sign in calls `signInProvider`, an in-process entry into the ADR-0070 login flow with the editor's exact profile and directory. The editors bind the project folder like the OMP settings editor: the folder sessions run in, a session's own cwd when opened for a session ([ADR-0073](decisions/0073-run-sessions-at-the-checkout-root-and-let-the-editors-follow.md)).

**OMP Settings editor** (`src/host/omp-config-editor.ts`, `omp-config-controller.ts`, `yaml-key-line.ts`, `src/omp/config-settings-worker.ts`, `config-schema.ts`, `config-conditions.ts`, `config-ancestor.ts`, `src/protocol/omp-config-model.ts`, `src/webview/omp-settings/`; [design](designs/2026-10-13-omp-settings-editor.md), [ADR-0071](decisions/0071-edit-omps-settings-from-its-own-registry-in-a-staged-worker-and-write-one-layer-at-a-time.md)). One editor per OMP profile and scope (global, or one project folder; the home folder has no project layer), opened by `ompDesk.openOmpSettings`, a bare `/settings` in Chat and the Sessions title menu. A second staged Bun worker imports the selected OMP's `config/registry.ts`, `all-settings.ts`, `settings.ts`, `discovery/index.ts` and `pi-tui`'s `settings-defs.ts`, projects OMP's own tabs, groups, labels, controls and visibility into bounded JSON (credentials carry only set or unset, by any of OMP's three markers), reads the layers with `Settings.loadReadOnly` and the project files OMP merges from its settings capability (the last file that sets a key wins and is named), and writes one setting to one layer through `writeConfig` (ADR-0048). There is no version gate (ADR-0011): runtime shape checks refuse an unrecognised schema, and the snapshot says whether the installed release is the verified one. The project folder is the folder sessions run in: a session's own cwd for `/settings` in a Chat, the Sessions rows and a folder chosen with the dialog as they are, and for the first workspace folder with no session to follow `editorProjectFolder` in `session-folder-setting.ts` (the checkout root, or the opened folder when `ompDesk.runSessionsAtRepositoryRoot` is off or no checkout is found), so Unity projects opened below the repository root write `<root>/.omp` with no setting of their own. The first project write is still refused when it would stop an ancestor `.omp`'s AGENTS.md, RULES.md or SYSTEM.md loading (`ancestorContextDir`): OMP finds those by walking to the nearest `.git`, so this applies to a root of another system, a folder in no checkout and a folder whose setting is off, not to a Git root; it also applies to a session folder that is not its root (a session recorded in a subfolder before the upgrade). The page is message-only and presentation-only: `model.ts` holds the logic, `components/` and `styles.ts` the Settings-style layout.

Configuration transactions reread the migrated global layer under OMP's exact native file lock and merge only edited entries/whole arrays, preserve unknown values, validate before staging/fsync and atomic replacement, and refuse overlapping changes, malformed YAML or lost/retargeted files. Comments are removed by the native serializer. Project writes are model roles only, with null tombstones and explicit multi-file partial outcomes for global thinking. Other hub settings are global; browser membership/provider preferences stay read-only. Agent creation uses exclusive complete-file publication after native architect generation and a read-only preview. Explicit Refresh/Generate use OMP's persistent AuthStorage API, including native token rotation effects, never extension-owned credential database writes; refreshed catalogues remain editor-private. Future resolution reloads, not existing children/current models. Explicit current-default application is bound to the launching live idle Chat and confirmed with `get_state`; auto thinking and upstream routing are refused rather than approximated.

Installed isolated acceptance observed both settings tabs, role and agent-model saves through their forms, fresh native OMP Settings/AgentsHubDeps readback and `/models` / `/agents` submission from a visibly active Chat. Repeat opens reused the matching editors. Typecheck and 43 focused tests passed. The proof used owned configuration and provider metadata without real credentials; live provider Refresh/Generate were not exercised. Native Stop, scoped dialog handling and window close left no owned broker or child process running.

**Removed by the cutover** (no shim, callers migrated): `src/relay/**` and the `out/relay.js` build entry with its relay staging and the `ws` dependency; `src/views/session-link.ts`, `src/host/collab-room-identity.ts`, the guest wire stack `webview/lib/{client,socket,link,codec,use-guest,agent-transcript,raw-capture,wire-constants,timers}.ts` and the Collab error-text module `src/capability-text.ts`; the whole raw recorder family (`src/host/raw-recorder-*.ts`, `src/raw-recorder-ui.ts`, its seven commands); the Agents Hub, connect screen and Chat | Terminal switch (`AgentsPanel`, `ConnectScreen`, `ModeTabs`, `omp:surface`/`omp:select-surface`/`omp:terminal-seen`), `omp.openNativeTerminal` and its `ctrl+alt+t` keybinding; `src/host/native-switch.ts`, `src/host/draft-promotion.ts` (replaced by the exact `get_state` binding) and the native A→B `TransferWitness` machinery; the Collab CLI invocations (`omp collab list|link`), room-based absence rules and lost-room recovery. `managed-omp` broker records and the persisted `instanceId`/`generation`/`heldTransfers`/binding `witness` fields are parse-only so existing catalogs and slots still load.

**Processes view** (`src/host/broker-processes.ts`, `broker-process-records.ts`, `broker-process-stop.ts`, `broker-process-controller.ts`, `process-wiring.ts`, `src/views/process-tree.ts`; [design](designs/2026-10-07-processes-view.md)). A second view in the same container lists live brokers from the PTY registry records of this profile (and, read-only and list-only, the predecessor id's). A record is a row only when the broker PID is alive and a kernel creation-time reading equals the one the broker recorded about itself; verdicts of gone or recycled are cached for good because the registry keeps one record per stopped broker. Nothing authenticates to list; an explicit refresh adds one owner-hint-free read-only attach per authenticable row to learn whether the broker's child still runs. A slot is *in use* when any of a session row's recorded host, a conversation or editor broker-slot mapping (conversation mappings only while their row exists) or a shell slot names it, *orphaned* when none does and every source was read in full, *unknown* otherwise. Stop re-derives the row from fresh records and a refreshed catalog, asks nothing, and routes at once: a session this window drives goes through `confirmAndStopRuntime` with its runtime and transition facts read immediately before; a row's recorded rpc host through `stopSessionHost` with its `{slot, attempt, pid}` read immediately before (a native row is reattached first and read after that); everything else (terminals, orphans, idle brokers) through the broker itself: graceful `stop`, an optional authenticated force stop (the only prompt of a single stop, offered when the graceful stop failed), `shutdown({ requireStopped: true })`. Only Stop Orphaned and Idle asks, once, for many processes. No process id is ever signalled. A confirmed child stop retires a shell recovery slot or an unowned session mapping, without claiming whole-tree termination; recorded session rows remain. Brokers this build cannot authenticate to are listed with the reason.

**Sessions processes (2026-10-11 amendment; [design](designs/2026-10-11-sessions-unify-processes.md), [ADR-0060](decisions/0060-merge-background-processes-into-sessions-and-kill-all-through-authenticated-routes.md)).** The Processes view above is gone: its model, reader, records, authenticated stop and controller are unchanged, but the list is served by the Sessions provider (`setProcesses`, `src/views/process-rows.ts`, pure projection in `src/views/session-processes.ts`; `src/views/process-tree.ts` is deleted) and `process-wiring.ts` is now `registerSessionProcesses`, the one owner of the reader: every read, the poll's and each controller's, runs through one queue and is published to the provider. Background reads run only while Sessions is visible (every 5 s in the `terminals` layout, 30 s elsewhere, plus one debounced tick 2 s after a launcher refresh; a verified identity is trusted for 60 s of wall time); explicit actions always read fresh. A session row marks its process in the tooltip and accessibility label from its own evidence (this window runs the writer or an authenticated broker witnessed it; `otherWindow` says *held by another window*), with the snapshot only adding uptime or an idle note. Processes no session row stands for are served in a **Terminals** group after the sessions in the flat and folders layouts, and every process at the root of the new `terminals` layout (no session, folder or New Session rows; `beginReveal`, the pinned-selection rule and the notification reveal skip it). A process row opens what it stands for (`ompDesk.openProcess`: the session's row click, the terminal's editor or a reconnect, the Stats panel). Stop, **Stop Orphaned and Idle** and **Kill All** are Sessions title buttons; New Session and Resume Session left the title bar and stay as rows. Single Stop, the bulk stop and Kill All share one `stopRow`, which reports per row whether its route confirmed the stop. **Kill All** (`planKillAll`) offers *Stop in This Window* (sessions this window drives and folder terminals its editors carry, by exact identity; a session another window holds is that window's even with an editor here) and *Stop in All Windows* (rows other windows hold, and rows this window does not run) behind one modal, re-derives after the choice and never forces silently, signals a PID or stops another live window's processes itself. This window's rows are stopped serially through their own lifecycle or authenticated route (a recorded-host stop binds to the slot, attempt and broker id/generation it was derived from; local classification uses the exact slot, broker id and generation, editor-only runtimes included; a driven or recorded route proves the writer gone and the broker's exit is probed separately, so an unproven retirement is reported as not confirmed). *Stop in All Windows* sends each other live window a versioned `stop` request on the ADR-0056/0057 window request channel (`window-requests.ts`, at most 32 row identities, no PIDs); the receiver takes it up by atomic rename, re-derives its own rows, stops through its own lifecycle only those that still equal an identity named and that it provably runs, never forces, and answers per row (`stopped`, `unconfirmed`, `refused`, `changed`). A row no window answers for (refused by all, not taken up, or its window gone) is stopped through the authenticated broker and reported as stopped without its window; a live window that took the request up and went silent, whose request could not be withdrawn, or whose take-up evidence may have been lost while this window was suspended is left alone and reported as uncertain until its extension host (identified by process id and creation time when it was asked, `peer-executor.ts`) is positively proven gone; a lapsed lease, an unreadable registry or an undecided probe prove nothing, and a registry that cannot be read leaves every row another window may run alone. The receiver is graceful only (no Force Stop or other modal; a failed graceful stop answers `unconfirmed`), a window's `stopped` counts only after an independent broker-exit probe, and the fallback route is this window's own lifecycle for a row that became locally driven and otherwise the authenticated broker, never a recorded reattach. Rows no window runs (orphans, idle, detached terminals, Stats) are stopped through the broker as before. Every dispatch is revalidated against a fresh read (the target set never grows) and one report is written per target from a final fresh read. `ompDesk.refreshProcesses` stays as a hidden internal command (Stats panel, shell exit).

**Kill (2026-10-12 amendment, [ADR-0060](decisions/0060-merge-background-processes-into-sessions-and-kill-all-through-authenticated-routes.md), the only place a process id is signalled).** The context menu of a session row (and of a process row) offers Kill: `src/host/session-kill.ts` attempts a tree kill of the recorded broker, and of the recorded OMP child, with System32's `taskkill /PID <pid> /T /F` at once, with no question to OMP and no look at the session's state, claims or owner. The one check kept: a fresh reading of each id equals the creation time Desk recorded (the broker's record, or the session's recorded host); an id that is gone or now belongs to a later process is "already gone", and an unreadable identity kills nothing. The check and the kill are two steps, so this is best-effort against a reissued id, not atomic. Each recorded root is then shown gone by a liveness look and the creation-time probe; the tree is never claimed empty, because what `taskkill` reaches is its own behaviour (ADR-0029). Desk's records are retired only on evidence about the writer, bound to the examined incarnation: a session row is recorded stopped by `SessionIndex.reconcileKilledHost` only when the recorded OMP child (pid and creation time) was shown gone, under the tab's lifecycle gate, with no live rival claim holder and no runtime driven here, and with a conditional shared write that the catalog refuses when the committed row no longer records the same launch attempt, slot and broker (a replacement launch another window published is never recorded stopped); a broker's absence alone is never writer evidence. Kill forgets only the broker-slot mapping of a process row; a folder shell's recovery record stays until the owning window's shell watch proves the shell root gone too. A window that drives a runtime looks once a second (`src/host/broker-loss.ts`, no process spawn) for its broker's process and, when it is gone, waits up to 30 s for both recorded creation times (broker and OMP or shell root) to name gone processes before it publishes the ending (`handleRpcHostExited` for chat, `handleNativeHostExited` for a terminal, slot retirement for a folder shell, re-checked inside the slot's gate); an unprovable root keeps the runtime and records and warns. Failures, incomplete tree kills and failed record updates are warnings and log lines; a successful kill is silent. Stop, Stop Orphaned and Idle and Kill All never reach this module. The other direction holds too: a Close, Stop or Reload whose stop the broker could not confirm (it was killed, or the process cannot be reached) first asks whether the recorded OMP root already ended (`recordedRootGone` in `src/host/native-session.ts`: its id no longer in use, or now another process's by creation time) and, when it did, finishes as a confirmed stop, so a session ended from outside is never left needing a Force Stop.

**Reload and view switch of a session whose OMP process already ended (2026-10-13).** A Reload or a Chat/Terminal switch normally reads the session's facts, asks for its one confirmation, stops the exact process and launches the successor on the same file. When the facts cannot be read, or the stop cannot be confirmed, `switchSessionMode` (`src/extension.ts`) asks whether the recorded OMP root already ended (`alreadyEnded`, the check Close and Stop use). If it did, the request is finished instead of refused: a facts read that failed leads to `endEndedRuntime`, which ends the runtime the way its exit would (`handleRpcHostExited` / `handleNativeHostExited`) and then takes the request as one for a stopped session, a Resume of the row's exact file in the requested view; an abort or settlement a gone process cannot give, or an unconfirmed stop, counts as the confirmed stop and the ordinary replacement continues. No Force Stop is offered and nothing is reported as failed. A root that is not proven gone keeps today's refusals, and Reload of a draft without a file is still refused before any of this.

**What the end of an OMP process records (2026-10-17, [ADR-0031](decisions/0031-reattach-a-surviving-managed-host-through-its-broker.md) amendment).** An OMP process that ends without Desk asking (`LocalExitOperations.rpcExited` for Chat, `nativeExited` for a Terminal, and the broker loss that leads to them) is classified by how the broker's own exit status says it ended (`exitOutcome`, `src/runtime/local-exit.ts`; `waitForManagedRootExit` returns it in `NativeStopVerdict.exit`). Exit code 0 with no signal is OMP's own way out (`/exit`, `/quit`, Ctrl+D and a closed stdin all end in `postmortem.quit(0)` or `disposeAndExit`, read from OMP 18.11.1) and records the stopped intent, so the row stays stopped. Anything else leaves the run intent as it was and the row is relaunched when its home window is restored at the next startup (see "A session has a home window" below): a non-zero code (the broker logs show `4` for every child when Windows ends the session, `1` for a startup failure, `130` for a force-quit, `-1` with signal 15 for a forced termination), a signal, no reported code, or no answer at all from a broker that was killed with the process. The tab and the Sessions row show the process as ended at once either way. The end is one `SessionIndex.closeSession` (`runIntent: "stopped" | "unchanged"`): the intent decision, the cleared host and the claim release with its release evidence are a single catalog write, so a window killed in the middle can never leave a stopped intent beside a retained host; a row that still records the host of a dead broker with a running intent and no claim is relaunched by the same absence evidence as any other. Desk's own Stop ends the process under the tab's gate and records the stopped intent itself (the exit it causes finds the runtime already cleared and records nothing), and Kill now records it before it ends the process (`KillCommandPorts.recordStopIntent`, `SessionIndex.recordKillIntent`: exactly the captured incarnation, put back if the kill failed, nothing is killed if it cannot be recorded), so only an end nobody chose in Desk leaves the intent. Accepted consequence: a session whose process keeps crashing is retried each time VS Code starts, and an Open of such a row launches it, as for any row whose intent is running. A Terminal closed with Ctrl+C while its teardown is stuck exits with code 130 and is relaunched too.

**The end of an OMP process after the user's Kill or Stop, and the bound of its proof (2026-10-21, amendment to the paragraph above).** `exitOutcome(stop, recorded)` also reads the run intent that the row records when the exit is handled: this window's copy, else the newest committed record (`SessionIndex.committedRunIntent` re-reads the catalog, so a Stop or Kill another window committed a moment ago is seen before this window's own watch has adopted it, [ADR-0034](decisions/0034-store-sessions-in-one-profile-catalog.md) amendment of 2026-10-21). A row that already records `stopped` was stopped by the user (Kill and a Stop of a recorded host write the intent before the process ends), so its end is recorded as that stop whatever the broker said: `closeSession(runIntent: "stopped")`, the row detail "The session was stopped. Resume to continue.", and an Info line that starts `user stop:`. The "ended unexpectedly … restarts when its window's editor is restored at the next VS Code start" wording stays for the end of a row that records `running`. Every await of the proof (`LocalExitOperations.#proveEnded`, shared by `rpcExited` and `nativeExited`) is bounded by `EXIT_PROOF_DEADLINE_MS` (3 s each, injectable as `proofDeadlineMs`): the broker client, the broker's reading of how the root exited, and the kernel's check of the recorded root (`StopOperations.confirmEnded`). A step that has no client, throws or does not answer in time goes on to the next, and only an answer proves the root gone: a failed or unanswered probe is never proof of death, so the row then stays as it is, fenced, with the reason on the tab. An answer that arrived while the host was stalled is taken before the step is given up. One Info line per exit lists the steps with their elapsed milliseconds and the verdict (`exit proof: broker client 12 ms, broker proof 1000 ms (writer not proven), kernel root check 3 ms -> gone`). `endEnded` bounds its kernel check the same way.

**A Stop or Kill from any window stays stopped (2026-10-21, [ADR-0034](decisions/0034-store-sessions-in-one-profile-catalog.md) amendment).** The catalog merge (`mergeSessionIndexRecords`) settles the *run state* of a row that two windows changed field by field: `runIntent`, `host` and `ownership` (the release stamp) keep the committed value unless this window changed that field relative to the value it last saw; every other field of a row this window changed is still this window's (both versions recorded when the other window changed the row too). A write that clears the host of the run this window knew does not touch a newer launch attempt another window committed meanwhile (a different `startedAt`): the committed run state stands whole. So an exit (`closeSession` with `runIntent: "unchanged"`: host cleared, release stamped) never writes the intent, and a Kill or Stop another window recorded within the same moment survives it; a close that wrote reports the settled intent (`CloseSessionResult.runIntent`), adopts the run state the merge settled into the window's own copy and moves the merge base with it. `SessionIndex.recordRunIntent(tabId, intent, { expect?, detail? })` is the one owner of a deliberate intent change (`setRunIntent`, `recordKillIntent` and the intent `stopRecordedHost` writes first go through it); with `expect` it is a conditional transaction on the committed row, refused (`target-changed`) when another window replaced the run it examined. The schema is unchanged. **Mixed builds:** a window of an older build keeps the old rule, so while one is open its end-of-process write can still revert another window's Stop or Kill when the two happen within the same moment (the row then reads as a crash: relaunched at the next start, openable now); closing and reopening the remaining windows picks up the new build everywhere, and the new build never depends on the intent having survived.

The same rule has a mirror and two guards (ADR-0034 amendment, review round 1). A write that records a *new attempt* of the row (a launch or an attach: a `startedAt` the window did not know) carries its own intent, host and ownership, so a stop or release another window committed for the older attempt does not stay glued to the new host. `recordRunIntent` reads the committed intent back after the write: a value other than the one written means the merge read the write as no change, so the window follows the committed row and writes once more and the deliberate decision lands. A conditional Stop whose write could not land is retried once, conditionally, after the catalog is re-read and refuses ("The stopped intent could not be recorded") when it still cannot be recorded; a Kill keeps one fallback, its ordinary save, because the process ends whatever the catalog says.

**A session has a home window (2026-10-21, [ADR-0080](decisions/0080-hand-over-a-live-session-through-a-taken-up-request-that-detaches-the-holder-without-stopping-it.md) amendment, [ADR-0034](decisions/0034-store-sessions-in-one-profile-catalog.md) second amendment).** The shared session row records `home`, the URI of the window (`file:` folder or saved workspace, compared through `windowUriIdentity`) that last became its driver; the `windowUri` provider of `SessionIndex` reads it from `owningWindowUri()` when the row is written. It is written in the same persist as the host at every place a window becomes the driver (the launch and the attach of a surviving host in `#openEntry`, the re-attach of a live draft in `#reattachDraft`, and `transferNativeHost`), never cleared by a stop, a close or a crash, and merged by `settleRunState` as the fourth field of the run state (a stale window's unrelated write keeps the committed home, the early return for a write that only clears the host takes it, a write that records a new attempt carries its own). At the next VS Code start `homeAllows(entry, windowUri)` decides whether a window may restore a row: a `running` row whose recorded home is another window is not in the recovery cohort (`inLocalRecoveryCohort`, which also feeds the pending "Restoring" fact and the gone-record sweep's `ownedHere`), and the editor the move left behind shows the saved history read-only through `openRestoredTabIfUncovered` with "This session belongs to another window. Use Sessions to switch to it, or Move to This Window.", taking no claim and starting nothing. A row with no home, a row that is not `running`, and a row the window already runs a host for are unchanged. Home is a URI, so two windows on the same folder are both home and the claim race decides; a session whose home window is not restored is not relaunched by another window until the user opens it. The schema gains one optional field; a build that does not know it drops it when it rewrites the row.

**Stats dashboard** (`src/host/stats-dashboard.ts`, `stats-wiring.ts`, `stats-webview.ts`, `media/stats-opener.mjs`; [design](designs/2026-10-08-stats-dashboard.md)). `OMP Desk: Open Stats`, also a Sessions title action, embeds the actual loopback dashboard through `asExternalUri`, an exact-origin iframe CSP and no host bridge; the iframe retains same-origin fetch/EventSource. The existing broker owns `omp stats` in a `stats-dashboard` PTY with a fresh slot; a separate profile-wide admission lock serializes kind discovery, preflight and launch. Compatible identity-header responses only reveal/open presentation. Stats records are their own durable background-work references, never orphaned; Processes Stop authenticates the exact child, and bulk Stop includes Stats only when idle. Tab close/reload do not stop the server; retained dead-broker records do not block another fresh slot. Unknown live brokers and readiness failures remain tracked and visibly recoverable.

The Stats launcher stages and verifies its child preload, then tests it using a harmless version invocation. Supported runtimes suppress only the exact native Windows opener for the URL that same Stats process printed. Unsupported runtimes require explicit Start/Cancel consent before a native browser opening; consent is not remembered and is bypassed when the dashboard is already running. Changed opener formatting passes through with suppression unconfirmed in Output. No global browser setting is used, and the preload is carried in `BUN_OPTIONS` only for a launcher that is not Bun's own CLI and only after that executable showed the marker ([ADR-0075](decisions/0075-carry-bun-runtime-flags-by-launcher-kind-and-probe-before-using-an-environment-carrier.md)). A compatible externally started server is unowned, and native OMP can still reclaim an older/OMP-identifiable listener racing extension preflight. See [ADR-0049](decisions/0049-intercept-stats-browser-opener-via-preload.md).

Installed isolated Stats acceptance passed typecheck and 189 targeted tests, captured the iframe's live EventSource messages and verified same-broker reuse across repeat open, tab close/reopen and reload. Authenticated UI Stop ended the exact root/broker; restart used a fresh slot while retaining the earlier record. A separately owned incompatible listener produced an error without launch. Browser-opener suppression was observed on the supported global runtime, never by launching the unsupported path. All owned proof processes/windows/listeners were stopped; see the design's implementation evidence.

**Tree presentation and action notices.** Processes rows use **Open in this window** or **Open in another window** when that observation is known, **Running** otherwise, and retain explained **Orphaned**, **Idle** and unchecked states. Tooltips show kind, what is hosted, locale-formatted and relative start time, and uptime. Uptime reuses the Chat's pure duration formatter; IDs and connection metadata are only in Copy Diagnostics. Direct Pin, Unpin, Add Folder, Forget and successful Stop update their visible surfaces without success toasts. Errors and confirmations use session/editor language and actionable next steps; low-level stop failures are retained in Output.

**Evidence and unverified boundaries.** Observed with the installed OMP 18.4.3 in scratch directories (recorded in the design's S0 results): `ready` in about 1.7–2.0 s (one cold probe took 30 s), protocol-2 negotiation, `get_state`/`get_entries` identity and cursor behaviour, stdin EOF closing the session, `session_exit` appended on exit, `switch_session` to another cwd returning `cancelled`, `--preload` and `-e` loading under rpc-ui. Focused unit tests cover the frame decoder, history reader, `RpcSession` (fake channel), chat reducer, chat client and messages, launch spec, reconcile verdicts, bridge routing and `ChatRuntime`. **Observed in installed isolated VS Code windows (2026-09-29/30, runs ACC-6 to ACC-13, summarized in the design's acceptance record):** New Session through Starting; prompt/stream/abort, steer and follow-up with the `queued ×N` chip; tool approval and `ask` select dialogs answered from the page; model and thinking changes read back; Sessions without blanking and with the active row selected while switching tabs; Close Session through Stopping to Stopped; stopped-row Open as view-only without a process and Resume on the same JSONL; a second window of the same profile seeing held sessions as Blocked and refused as a second writer; extension-host-only restarts keeping the same child/broker pids and the surviving page live; full window restart restoring tabs with history visible in about 0.4–2.6 s; and the upgrade from a previous (Collab-era) build: legacy row and banner, explicit Stop of the legacy child and its broker, view-only then Resume into rpc, and a previous-build folder shell reconnected (after a host-only restart through `Reconnect Terminal`). **Not observed:** image attachments through rpc `prompt`, a passive editor's read-only notice (`omp:passive` no longer exists, so none is shown), and MCP-heavy `get_state` sizes. Still true from earlier baselines: the terminal-only folder-shell stop cannot prove a whole tree stopped, so Terminate keeps the broker and slot and says so (ADR-0029), and the owner-exit watcher has only real-process fixture evidence (ADR-0030). Settings writes, native per-tool approval, tool selection, MCP servers that need an interactive OAuth consent (a future companion OMP extension) and safe automatic file restore remain unavailable.

**Initial TUI-parity source acceptance (2026-10-02).** The initial parity package passed typecheck and the full 1,110-test suite (1,109 pass, one Windows platform skip), including headless Chromium HUD expansion/counts, grouped reads, native message/error/event rendering, lazy read-only child output and actual Codicon font loading. A no-prompt `--mode rpc-ui --no-session` smoke against installed OMP 18.4.4 observed ready, state, progress subscription, roster, entries, unknown-child rejection and clean EOF exit. It did not create a real running child or submit a provider prompt. No VS Code process/window/install operation was permitted for that implementation package; the maintainer's later installed observations and source correction are distinguished below.

**Native-visible transcript correction (2026-10-02).** The maintainer's isolated installed-VSIX inspection confirmed Edit/grouped Read/Bash/Write and the collapsed TODO surface, and identified internal metadata/entry noise. The corrected source keeps internal data for reconciliation but excludes it from semantic card counts and the DOM. User messages have no role echo; model/thinking changes are compact muted lines. Tool expansion uncaps native content rather than exposing call/result/provider records; nullable/default Bash parameter noise and redundant complete/queued labels are absent. Native benign interrupted waits are omitted exactly as installed OMP does; ordinary wait snapshots and real failures remain visible. Actual owned Chromium scenarios exercise these boundaries. This correction package performs no GUI/install/session mutation; updated installed-window acceptance remains separate. Full evidence and native citations are in the [parity design](designs/2026-10-01-tui-parity-transcript.md#installed-window-correction-2026-10-02).

**Native roster and payload-table correction (2026-10-02).** The maintainer's next installed-window inspection confirmed the earlier visibility fixes and identified raw registry/progress, wait-job and async-task tables. All semantic renderers select corresponding native content instead of recursively displaying arbitrary details; the unknown-tool fallback shows native bounded arguments/output, never a details archive. A collapsed Wait is one header line (job name, outcome and clock, or a count and a truncated name list; this supersedes the original compact job rows under the header); result summaries are expansion-only. Collapsed Task cards show agent name/type/status rows; shared context, assignment, progress, review and results are expansion-only. Background work is a header badge, not a fabricated acknowledgement or zero dispatch timer. Owned Chromium exercises an overflowing 25-agent roster, matched live task progress, tool disclosure, lazy child access and exclusion of internal keys. Verification and shipping outcomes are recorded in the [parity design](designs/2026-10-01-tui-parity-transcript.md); updated installed-window acceptance is the maintainer's separate boundary.

**Earlier collapsed tool previews (2026-10-02; superseded by Paseo-style Chat).** The parity correction originally used native per-family body previews and explicit full disclosure, preserving meaningful Bash/Task/Wait content without internal tables. The 2026-10-04 cutover removes those closed-row preview mounts and obsolete read-group presentation, retaining the same native expanded body grammar, diagnostics and artifact recovery. Initial parity evidence above describes that earlier baseline, not the current flat-row contract.

**Paseo Chat source and installed acceptance (completed 2026-10-05).** All four slices are implemented. Owned Chromium exercised 10,000 mixed/giant histories with ≤80 mounted rows/calls, preserved focus/selection and ≤2 CSS px settled anchor drift; those are browser bounds, not an installed performance claim. Coordinated typecheck/full suite/VSIX/exact isolated installation passed (1,290 tests passed, one platform skip); final VSIX SHA-256 is `c95ef9d47456220053e0083bf088f2d8ca006f75726b3f4e05ea90cacf0d21a9`, with packaged/built/installed extension, guest and shell equality. Genuine OMP ask, arriving write source, failing tool, background scout and 120-call history were exercised in owned isolated Chat documents. The one-file Overview retains all 120 genuine call IDs/targets/statuses across actual normal disclosure and accessible paging; native read range/raw selectors do not inflate distinct-file counts. The native high-contrast Skill picker uses paired selected foreground/background theme variables, measured 18.494:1 contrast for all four selected text parts, and first Enter inserts without sending.

Actual installed Light/Dark/high-contrast, 320 CSS px width, native DPR-2 zoom, reversible accessible paging and Accessibility-domain/keyboard observations are recorded in the [design evidence](designs/2026-10-04-paseo-style-chat.md#implementation-evidence-completed-2026-10-05), including screenshot paths and receipt hashes. Narrator speech is unexercised; normal-wheel automation did not re-prove full history depth; the installed bottom-clamped prepend drift was +7.0625 px, not the browser ≤2 px bound. Historical response-only timing is not reconstructed live-turn timing. The isolated window remains running with fixtures/settings preserved. Post-proof documentation closeout does not claim a rebuilt artifact.


**Same-editor native mode source/runtime evidence (2026-10-02).** Independent architecture review accepted the design and confirming spot check. Actual owned Chromium exercises both selectors, xterm output, native grid changes after container resize, stopping fences, stopped recovery and return to Chat. A private no-prompt smoke against installed OMP 18.4.4 exercised public SDK state/Rename/shutdown, matched broker exit and root creation-time absence before exact-file/SID RPC continuation, and a fileless no-turn native session followed by a fresh empty RPC session. All four owned roots exited and scratch was removed; tree emptiness was not proved. The [same-editor design](designs/2026-10-02-same-editor-chat-and-native-terminal.md#package-state-and-evidence-ledger) distinguishes the final package checks from the maintainer's still-separate installed-window acceptance.
Installed isolated-window acceptance subsequently observed real native turns, Chat/Terminal continuity, explicit row actions, profile Terminal default/new session and busy cancellation. Final font/notice acceptance showed no stale warnings, actual Nerd Font status/recent-session/throughput glyphs, live terminal metrics and editor-family fallback updates without replacing either surviving process. Exact builds, hashes and scoped permissions are recorded in the same design's evidence ledger.

**Earlier-baseline observations (Collab era, not evidence for the current pairing).** Isolated installed-VSIX windows on VS Code 1.139.x and OMP 18.3.0 previously verified exact-file restoration across full restart, file-observation consent producing a VS Code diff, settings display with provenance, interactive History resume and workspace-folder launcher behaviour ([History](designs/2026-09-25-interactive-history-resume.md), [launcher](designs/2026-09-25-workspace-folder-launcher.md)). They exercised the removed Collab guest and its former in-tab PTY pairing. Native PTY rendering now returns beside RPC Chat, without restoring Collab; those older observations are not acceptance of this pairing or RPC Chat. They prove only the then-current index, claims, folder Resume/Delete, bridge, file observation and settings surface. Actual rendered chat was compared against the installed comparison extension (Pi Code 0.73.0) during development; those screenshots are not kept in the repository.


**Packaging and platform.** OMP Desk targets Windows only: the broker depends on ConPTY through `node-pty`, the host control and desktop toasts run PowerShell and named pipes, and process identity readings and the session-lease probe call Win32 in process through a staged `koffi.node` ([ADR-0076](decisions/0076-call-win32-in-process-through-a-staged-koffi-module-instead-of-starting-powershell.md); the PTY runtime tree carries the addon at `native/koffi.node`, so a broker loads the exact file its proofs name). One VSIX is built per target (`win32-x64`, `win32-arm64`); `OMP_DESK_TARGET` makes `esbuild.mjs` ship only that target's `node-pty` prebuilds, and `scripts/package-vsix.mjs` sets it for `npm run vsix:x64` and `npm run vsix:arm64`. The extension declares `extensionKind: ["ui"]` and no support for untrusted or virtual workspaces, because it starts local processes in a workspace folder. Release steps and Marketplace constraints are in [Publishing OMP Desk](publishing.md).

The shipped bundles are minified (`esbuild.mjs`, `minify: !watch`): the extension host parses `out/extension.js` at every window start and a session editor `media/guest.js` when it opens, and the unminified bundles were about twice the size (2.1 MB and 2.0 MB against 1.1 MB each; the broker 590 KB against 354 KB). The watch build stays readable for debugging. Source maps are still written and still excluded from the VSIX.

**The terminal renderer and the rarely used host code are files of their own (2026-10-28, [design](designs/2026-10-28-lazy-xterm-and-rarely-used-host-code.md), [ADR-0085](decisions/0085-load-the-terminal-renderer-and-rarely-used-host-code-from-files-of-their-own.md), [ADR-0069](decisions/0069-load-mermaid-as-a-separate-local-script-and-show-diagrams-as-svg-images.md) amendment).** `media/terminal.js` (xterm.js, its addons and stylesheet; entry `src/webview/terminal/entry.ts`, options in `scripts/terminal-bundle.mjs`) is added by `guest.js` and `shell.js` through `lib/sibling-script.ts` (the loader the diagram renderer shares: folder of the page's own script, the document's nonce) the first time a `TerminalPane` mounts its renderer (`lib/terminal-loader.ts`, `terminal/contract.ts`: a `contract` number refuses a page and a renderer from two builds); the pane attaches to the host only after the renderer exists and shows `The terminal renderer could not be loaded…` / `…is out of date…` / `The terminal could not start: …` in place of a terminal that will not come. `out/rare-subsystems.js` (entry `src/wiring/rare-subsystems.ts`, loaded by `src/wiring/rare-subsystems-loader.ts`, whose one import the esbuild plugin `rareSubsystemsExternal` turns into a `require`) holds provider login and logout, `SettingsEditors`, `OmpConfigEditors` and the Desk Settings view; `wiring/settings-editors.ts` and `wiring/desk-settings-view.ts` register the commands, panel serializers and view provider at activation and create the editors/view on first use. The second bundle carries its own copy of the modules it imports, so what must stay single (`native-terminal`, `runtime-assets`, `OmpUnavailableError`) is handed in as `OmpRuntimeServices` (`src/host/omp-runtime-services.ts`). `esbuild.mjs` fails the build when xterm enters a page bundle or a rarely used module enters `out/extension.js`.

**Lifecycle and detail loading refinement (2026-10-08).** Session view requests project a pending context key so editor-title mode actions are disabled during a transition. Reversible admission fences are released on readback, gate-admission or publication failure; ten-second read/admission deadlines invalidate late queued requests. A shutdown that may already have been accepted retains its safety fence and offers the existing Stop/Force Stop recovery without automatically launching a replacement. Delete preflight errors produce visible failure feedback and bounded read-only inspection, while the destructive transaction remains exact-file and claim-protected. Single-agent detail tabs wait for the parent snapshot and first child read rather than treating missing initial data as proof the parent stopped.

**A session that does not answer is unreadable, not an error (2026-10-27).** `LocalStopOperations.readTransitionFacts` (the facts a Close, Stop, Reload or Restart is confirmed against) answers `null` when the session is not live, the read fails or OMP gives no answer within `SESSION_TRANSITION_READ_TIMEOUT_MS` (10 s), and logs one `the session's state could not be read (…)` line; it never rejects. A Close or Stop of a hung OMP therefore ends in the usual question and, once the graceful attempt says the session could not be checked, in the Force Stop question. Before, the rejection left `confirmAndStopRuntime` through its `finally`: the admission fence ("The session view is changing or its process is stopping.") stayed up for the 10 s of the read, then dropped with no dialog, no log line and nothing stopped, and only Kill ended the process. The gate-wait deadline of the bounded read (`bounded: true`, used by Delete and the view switch) still rejects, because a busy gate is not an unreadable session.

**The catalog survives a wipe of VS Code's extension storage (2026-10-21, [ADR-0081](decisions/0081-keep-a-private-backup-of-the-catalog-and-broker-records-outside-vs-codes-storage-and-restore-it-over-a-missing-catalog.md), [design](designs/2026-10-21-catalog-survives-a-storage-wipe.md)).** `src/host/catalog-backup.ts` keeps a private copy of the catalog (`catalog-g<generation>-r<revision>-c<rows>x<folders>-<hash>.json`, a generation being one incarnation of the catalog) and of the records of live brokers under `%LOCALAPPDATA%\omp-desk\recovery\<key>`, behind the owner-only convention, written from the store's change notifications and pruned by newest/daily/peak retention. `transactCatalog` takes `recover`/`isReset` hooks that run under the lock on the document just read: a missing or empty-shell document is replaced by the newest backup (and the broker records of running processes are published through `claimPtyRecord` after the `pty/` folder is shown owner-only) before the caller's operation runs; `createProfileCatalog` runs it once before any consumer reads (`src/wiring/catalog-recovery.ts`). `src/host/catalog-loss.ts` decides the notices: a restore, a loss with no usable backup but with evidence, a regression. `src/host/unmanaged-brokers.ts` and two `src/wire/win32-native.ts` reads list brokers of this storage that no record names as `unmanaged` rows in the Sessions view (Kill only).

## Cross-window invariants

Several windows and the detached brokers of one VS Code profile write the same files. This table is the maintained index of **who writes which shared fact, under which rule, where the code enforces it and which record decided it**; the gap column says what the rule does not cover. Rules use four words. *Lock*: a mutual-exclusion file or directory taken before a read-modify-write. *Claim*: an exclusive record whose holder is proved alive by pid and start time (ADR-0035). *Merge*: a write is combined with what another window committed meanwhile, per key or per field. *Lease*: a record that counts only while its stamp is fresh and its process exists. Cells name symbols, not lines; a change to a shared fact, its writer set or its rule updates its row in the same commit. Written on 2026-10-29 from the source and checked by an independent read-only `reviewer` pass against the code and the records (nine findings, all applied).

| Shared fact | Writers and rule | Enforced at | Record | Known gaps |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **The catalog document** (the one file holding the session index, broker slots, shell slots and folders) | Every window through its own store; one transaction = read, merge, stage a sibling, rename, under one cross-process exclusive-create lock file. `revision` is a clock, not a compare-and-swap token: a key without a registered merger commits only if the committed value still equals the window's last-seen one (else `divergence`); an unreadable document refuses the write. | `src/host/profile-catalog.ts` `transactCatalog`, `createCatalogStore`; `src/host/catalog-merge.ts` `mergeKeyedArray`, `mergeKeyedMap` | ADR-0034 (amendments 2026-10-12, 2026-10-13, 2026-10-15) | A lock is reclaimed only when its owner is provably gone and it is 10 s old; a lock file that cannot be read is never reclaimed, so the write ends `busy` after 4 s and is saved again (2026-10-15). |
| **Session rows**: title, scope, ordinal, reply markers | Any window; three-way merge per row against the window's merge base: a row this window changed wins (both versions go to the conflict ledger when another window changed it too), an untouched row takes the committed one, a removal stands unless the other window changed the row. The merge base follows every row the window adopts. Reply markers only advance. `availability` and `detail` are this window's own observations (`SESSION_INDEX_LOCAL_KEY`, workspace state), never part of the committed row and never merged. | `src/host/session-index.ts` `mergeSessionIndexRecords`, `SessionIndex.refreshFromStore` (`#sharedBase`); `src/host/session-unread.ts` `laterReplyMarker` | ADR-0034 (amendment 2026-10-17) | Outside the run state a changed row is still whole-row, "this window's facts win". The reply-marker rule is recorded only in the "Reply identities only advance across windows" paragraph above, which dates it 2026-10-14; ADR-0034 has no amendment of that date. |
| **Run state of a row**: `runIntent`, `host`, `ownership` (release stamp), `home` | A deliberate Stop or Kill intent is written through `SessionIndex.recordRunIntent` (`setRunIntent`, `recordKillIntent` and `stopRecordedHost` go through it); a launch or attach writes its intent, host and home together; an abnormal end writes none (`exitOutcome` → `closeSession` with `runIntent: "unchanged"`), while a clean exit and an explicit Close record `stopped` through `closeSession` itself. Merge is field by field: a field keeps the committed value unless this window changed it against its merge base; a write of a new attempt (a `startedAt` the window did not know) carries its own run state; `recordRunIntent` reads the committed intent back, and a Stop is retried once conditionally. `exitOutcome` reads the committed intent before blaming a crash. | `src/host/session-index.ts` `settleRunState`, `mergeSessionIndexRecords`, `recordRunIntent`, `committedRunIntent`, `closeSession`; `src/runtime/local-exit.ts` `exitOutcome` | ADR-0034 amendments 2026-10-21 and 2026-10-21 (second: `home`); ADR-0080 | A build older than the amendment still merges the row whole and can revert another window's Stop in the same moment (the file cannot tell writers apart; accepted in the ADR). |
| **Records of ended processes no window holds** | The activating window, once, `SessionIndex.sweepGoneRecords` 20 s after its restore settles: only a row whose claim is absent or held by a provably gone holder and whose process the reconciler proves gone; it takes the claim under the identity lock, clears `host` with a conditional write and releases. | `src/host/session-index.ts` `sweepGoneRecords`; `src/wiring/catalog-adoption.ts` `GONE_RECORD_SWEEP_DELAY_MS`; `src/host/session-claim.ts` `claimHolderProvablyGone`, `removeClaimOfGoneHolder` | ADR-0034 (amendment 2026-10-17, second) | Clears only `host`, never `runIntent`; a process that ends after the sweep waits for some window's next activation. |
| **Editor-slot bindings** (inside the session-index record) | Every window, for the slots of its own editors. A slot becomes `controlling` only when no live foreign claim holds the row (`claimHolderMayBeAlive`); a rival slot of the same window is demoted; the elections of one slot are serialized; a passive binding of a closed editor is dropped by the window that held it, after it has been absent from every live window's published `slots` for 30 s. Merged keyed by `slotId`. | `src/host/session-index.ts` `bindEditorSlot`, `#controllerRefusal`; `src/host/editor-slots.ts` `createEditorSlotRegistry`; `src/host/editor-slot-prune.ts` `EditorSlotWatch`, `EditorSlotPruner` | ADR-0025 (amendments 2026-10-17, 2026-10-19); ADR-0056 (2026-10-19 `slots`) | The merged list is not checked for a single `controlling` slot per conversation: two windows electing two slots at once can both commit `controlling`, and the claim is the only cross-window arbiter. The serialized-elections rule (2026-10-27) is recorded only in this document; ADR-0025 has no such amendment. |
| **Catalog backup and restore** | Each window's backup writer, throttled, **without a lock**: copy names address generation and content, written with `replaceFileAtomic`, pruning is idempotent. Restore is a hook of the catalog transaction: under the catalog lock, only when the document is missing or an empty shell, and it commits a base generation above everything seen. Broker records of running sessions are republished by `claimPtyRecord`. Which window shows a loss or restore notice is decided by a marker file any window may write (`claimNotice`, a 10-minute window). | `src/host/catalog-backup.ts` `CatalogBackupWriter`, `createRecoverySource`, `restoreBrokerRecords`, `claimNotice`; `src/host/profile-catalog.ts` `transactCatalog` (`options.recover`) | ADR-0081 | A stale backup restores stale rows (the notice says how old); a restored `running` row whose broker record was not backed up can resume into a second writer (ADR-0081 "Residual"). |
| **Session claims** (`session-claims/<identity>.claim`) | The window that launches, attaches or restores a session files its own claim, replaced under a per-identity mutex directory (fails closed with `ClaimBusyError`, never stolen by age). Admission refuses only on a rival that `claimHolderMayBeAlive` (pid exists and its creation time matches), whatever its owner generation. Another holder's claim is deleted only on a positive death fact (`claimHolderProvablyGone`), re-read under the lock. | `src/host/session-claim.ts` `acquireClaim`, `withIdentityLock`, `claimHolderMayBeAlive`, `claimHolderProvablyGone`, `removeClaimOfGoneHolder` | ADR-0039, ADR-0035; ADR-0045 | An unreadable start time never frees a claim (deliberate). |
