| **"The holder is alive"** (derived, not stored) | Admission, stop, delete and the Kill reconcile ask `claimHolderMayBeAlive`; claim deletion asks `claimHolderProvablyGone`; display and navigation ask `holderSeemsAlive`: the registry's lease and pid first, else `claimHolderSeemsAlive`, an in-process start-time reading cached for 20 s (ADR-0076). | `src/host/session-claim.ts`; `src/host/holder-liveness.ts` `holderSeemsAlive`; `src/host/window-registry.ts` `WindowRegistry.liveHolderPid` | ADR-0039, ADR-0056, ADR-0076 | The verdicts differ on purpose and can disagree: a row can read "other window" while Kill reports a rival or admission frees the claim. A rival whose claim pid differs from its registry pid is not seen by the registry path. |
| **Window registry** (`window-registry/<holder id>.window`) | Each window rewrites only its own record (pid, start time, lease stamp, URIs, `{status, incarnation, binding, lastActivityAt}` per session, `slots`), debounced, atomically, renewed every 15 s on a 60 s lease; a record counts only inside its lease with an existing pid; dead records are swept by readers. The directory watch is a hint; the heartbeat and a refresh on focus recover from a missed event. It never admits, refuses or frees a claim. | `src/host/window-registry.ts` `WindowRegistry` (`setSnapshot`, `liveHolderPid`), `sessionIncarnation`; `src/host/directory-watch.ts` `watchDirectory` | ADR-0056 (amendments 2026-10-19, 2026-10-21) | A host stalled for over 60 s leaves the registry and liveness falls back to the display reading; that is safe only because admission never consults the registry. |
| **Window requests** (`<to>.<id>.request`: select, launch, stop, reload, models, handover) | The requester writes; the addressee takes every kind but `models` up by atomic rename to `.accepted`, answers in `.response` and removes both; select, launch and stop also rewrite the taken-up file with their progress; only an untaken request is withdrawn; each kind has a lifetime. The stop and handover serve paths refuse a requester that is not another live registry window. | `src/host/window-requests.ts` `startRequestWatch`, `requestTtlMs`; `src/wiring/window-routing.ts` `serveStopRequest`, `serveHandoverRequest` | ADR-0056 (amendment 2026-10-21), ADR-0057, ADR-0060, ADR-0080 | A taken-up request keeps its permission until its `answerBy`. The requester filter exists in the stop and handover serve paths only: select, launch and reload act for any requester. |
| **The run a request acts on** | The requester names the holder's own published `incarnation` and `binding` (`runToRequest`); the holder serves only if its run still matches: `heldBinding` for a select, `#detachHeld` under the tab gate for a handover. | `src/wiring/window-routing.ts` `runToRequest`, `heldBinding`; `src/runtime/local-transition.ts` `detach`; `src/host/window-registry.ts` `sessionIncarnation` | ADR-0056 (amendment 2026-10-21), ADR-0080 | One predicate in two places with different strength: `#detachHeld` also checks `runIntent: "running"` and the recorded RPC identity, `heldBinding` does not. |
| **Which window drives a session** (claim, `controlling` slot, broker input ownership, row `home`) | One driver at a time. A handover detaches the holder without stopping OMP (re-check under the tab gate, release the conversation, dispose the control server, release the claim, no durable write); the adopter's ordinary open then takes the claim, attaches the surviving broker, reclaims broker input with a bounded retry and elects its slot `controlling`. `home` is written with the host at every place a window becomes the driver and is merged as part of the run state. | `src/runtime/local-transition.ts` `detach`; `src/host/session-index.ts` `detachLiveClaim`, `homeAllows`; `src/runtime/local-launcher.ts` `ADOPTED_INPUT_RECLAIM`; `src/wiring/window-routing.ts` `serveHandoverRequest` | ADR-0080 (amendment 2026-10-21); ADR-0034 (amendment 2026-10-21, second); ADR-0066 | The three authorities move in sequence, not atomically: a failed claim release leaves the holder read-only with the claim still its own. A collaborator added to the runtime record must be ended by `detach` (ADR-0080 consequence); nothing enforces it mechanically. `home` is a window URI, so two windows on one folder are both home. |
| **Which window restores a session at startup** | A row is in this window's cohort when it already has a live host here, else when VS Code restored a local editor for it and `homeAllows`; restore runs the cohort only, then elects each cohort editor `controlling`; admission still takes the claim, so a verified-live rival wins. | `src/wiring/catalog-adoption.ts` `localRecoveryCohort`, `inLocalRecoveryCohort`; `src/host/session-index.ts` `restoreAll`, `homeAllows` | ADR-0031 (notes 2026-10-21, 2026-10-28), ADR-0034, ADR-0080 (2026-10-21) | Placement follows restored editors and the recorded home, not intent: a running row with no editor anywhere stays inert until clicked. |
| **Broker records** (`pty/pty-<hash>.json`) | The broker process writes its own record, by exclusive create (`link()` of a staged file, never an overwrite); a launching window writes only the slot lock (an exclusive create); the one other writer is the backup restore. Readers attach through the token handshake and the broker's attestation, not a pid alone. A record is kept after shutdown as recovery metadata and retired only when the child never started. | `src/wire/pty-registry.ts` `claimPtyRecord`, `acquirePtySlotLock`; `src/broker/pty-broker.ts` `PtyBroker.run`; `src/host/pty-client.ts` `launchSlot`; `src/host/rpc-reconcile.ts` `provedBrokerPin` | ADR-0031, ADR-0035 (amendment 2026-10-13), ADR-0037, ADR-0060, ADR-0081 | A slot whose broker died with an unproven child tree stays occupied: replacing it "needs an explicit abandon", which this build does not offer. |
| **Broker slots** (conversation and editor to broker, in the catalog) | Any window that launches, attaches, repoints or forgets a host; per-identity keyed merge of the `byEditor` and `byConversation` maps, so two windows' different hosts both survive; a conflicting entry stays a marker, so "unknown transport" never becomes "none"; a repoint is planned against the newest committed table inside the transaction; refusal at 64, never eviction. | `src/host/broker-slots.ts` `mergeBrokerSlotRecords`, `planBrokerRepoint`, `createBrokerSlotStore` | ADR-0031, ADR-0034, ADR-0060 | None found. |
| **Shell slots** (folder-shell records, in the catalog) | Any window that starts or ends a folder shell; per-`slot` keyed merge, never dropped by a merge; refusal at 32, never eviction; retired only on a confirmed child exit or a launch verdict proving nothing started. | `src/host/shell-slots.ts` `mergeShellSlotRecords`, `createShellSlotStore` | ADR-0024, ADR-0029, ADR-0030, ADR-0034 | A slot of an unproven tree is kept by design (ADR-0029); the bound is per profile, so many windows' shells can together refuse a launch. |
| **PTY runtime check result** (`runtime/pty-proof/`, `runtime/pty-flight/`) | A window whose own check proved the runtime writes the proof (`writePtyProof`, atomic); one check per proof key at a time: a lock created whole by `link()`, stale when its pid is gone, its `expiresAt` has passed or it is corrupt, taken over after a confirming re-read; other windows wait a bounded time, then run their own check unlocked. The stored proof is the only success a waiter takes (an outcome file never is) and is believed for at most 24 h. | `src/host/pty-proof-flight.ts` `singleFlight`; `src/host/pty-runtime-proof-store.ts` `ptyProofKey`, `writePtyProof`; `src/host/pty-runtime.ts` `ensurePtyRuntime` | ADR-0032 and ADR-0074 (amendments 2026-10-28) | Leftover lock, outcome and temp files are never pruned; a reused but live pid makes waiters wait out the lock; the key does not cover environment or policy, hence the 24 h ceiling. |
| **Helper scheduler slots** (`omp-helper:0`, `omp-helper:1`, lock files in the PTY state directory) | Every window owns one `HelperScheduler` (two at a time, background work after its restore and one at a time); across windows two slot locks (exclusive creates), a 30 s wait, **fail-open**: the helper runs anyway when no slot frees. A throttle, not a correctness lock. | `src/host/helper-scheduler.ts` `HelperScheduler`; `src/host/helper-slots.ts` `createHelperSlots`; `src/wire/pty-registry.ts` `acquirePtySlotLock` | ADR-0084 | Liveness is by pid only: a reused pid keeps a slot busy until the wait ends (ADR-0084, consequences). An unreadable lock is replaced, so two windows can rarely share a slot (the slots' design and the header of `helper-slots.ts`; the ADR does not say it). |
| **Runtime store** (`runtime/<digest>/…`, the staged helpers, broker tree and native module) | Any window, with **no lock**: safety is in the names and the publication. A directory is named by the content digest, so two builds are two copies; publish by `link()` (a racing identical staging is "already there") else `rename()`; the bytes are hashed before they are handed out and again before use (the native module is hashed in memory, then mapped from those bytes); the owner-only layout is verified. | `src/wire/runtime-assets.ts` `stageRuntimeAssets`, `stageRuntimeTree`, `verifyStagedRuntimeAsset`, `verifyStagedRuntimeTree`; `src/host/pty-runtime.ts` `verifyPtyRuntime`; `src/wire/win32-native.ts` `loadWin32Native` | ADR-0012 (amendment 2026-10-13), ADR-0076 | Nothing evicts an old digest, deliberately (another window may still run it). |

Where the records and the source disagree, the source decides, and the disagreement is listed here until a record is amended: the two dated rules above that only this document carries (reply markers 2026-10-14, serialized editor-slot elections 2026-10-27), and the body of ADR-0056, which still describes a select's single 5 s withdrawal timer where its 2026-10-21 amendment (take-up, progress bounds) is what the code implements. Files this table names are written with `replaceFileAtomic` (`src/wire/atomic-file.ts`) unless the row says otherwise: the slot locks are exclusive creates, the catalog and the runtime store use their own stage-and-rename or `link()` publication.
